\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta})
)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}_{0}={\rm PolyMod}^{\rm ff}
denote the full subcategory of
\operatorname{SCRMod}^{\rm cn}
spanned by objects of the form
(A=\mathbb{Z}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{m}],M=A^{n})
, as in Notation
2.9
. Then
\mathcal{C}_{0}
admits finite coproducts given by the formula
(A,M=A^{n})\coprod(B,N=B^{t})=(A\otimes B,M\boxplus N)
, where
A=\mathbb{Z}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{m}]
,
B=\mathbb{Z}[y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}]
,
A\otimes B\simeq\mathbb{Z}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{m},y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}]
, and
M\boxplus N\simeq(A\otimes B)^{n+t}
. By virtue of Proposition
2.7
, the coproduct structure of
\mathcal{C}_{0}
extends to a coproduct structure on
\operatorname{SCRMod}^{\rm cn}\simeq\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\mathcal{C}_{0})
for which the inclusion
\mathcal{C}_{0}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{SCRMod}^{\rm cn}
preserves finite coproducts. The forgetful functor
\operatorname{SCRMod}^{\rm cn}\to\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
preserves finite coproducts, therefore coproducts in
\operatorname{SCRMod}^{\rm cn}
takes the form
(A,M)\coprod(B,N)=(A\otimes B,M\boxplus N)
, where
A\otimes B
is the pushout of the diagram of simplicial commutative rings
A\leftarrow\mathbb{Z}\rightarrow B
, and
M\boxplus N
is an
A\otimes B
-module. We will refer
M\boxplus N\in\operatorname{Mod}_{A\otimes B}
as the
external direct sum of
M
and
N
.

We denote the coproduct of two objects
(A\to A^{\prime})
and
(B\to B^{\prime})
in
\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta})
by
(A\to B)\coprod(A^{\prime}\to B^{\prime})=(A\otimes A^{\prime},B\boxtimes B^{\prime})
, where
A\otimes B
is the pushout of the diagram of simplicial commutative rings
A\leftarrow\mathbb{Z}\rightarrow A^{\prime}
as before, and
B\boxtimes B^{\prime}\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}_{A\otimes A^{\prime}}
denotes the simplicial commutative ring
B\otimes B^{\prime}
with the induced
A\otimes A^{\prime}
-algebra structure. We will refer to
B\boxtimes B^{\prime}\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}_{A\otimes A^{\prime}}
as the
external tensor product of
B
and
B^{\prime}
.

Definition 2.15
.

Let
A
be a simplicial commutative ring and
n\geq 0
an integer. We say an
A
-module
M
is
perfect
(resp.,
perfect to order
n
, in the sense of
[
DAG
, Proposition 2.5.7]
) if the functor
\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{Mod}_{A}}(M,\underline{\hphantom{A}})
preserve filtered colimits (resp., if the restriction of functor
\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{Mod}_{A}}(M,\underline{\hphantom{A}})
to
\tau_{\leq n}\operatorname{Mod}_{A}
preserves filtered colimits). We
M
is
almost perfect
if it is perfect to order
n
for all
n\geq 0
. Let
\phi\colon A\to B
be a map of simplicial commutative rings. We say
\phi
is
locally of finite presentation
(resp.,
of finite presentation to order
n
, in the sense of
[
DAG
, Proposition 3.1.5]
) if the functor
\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}_{A}}(B,\underline{\hphantom{A}})
preserve filtered colimits (resp., if the restriction of functor
\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}_{A}}(B,\underline{\hphantom{A}})
to
\tau_{\leq n}\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}_{A}
preserves filtered colimits). We say
\phi
is
almost of ﬁnite presentation
if it is of finite presentation to order
n
for all
n\geq 0
.

The following proposition is a list of basic properties of derived symmetric algebras:

Proposition 2.16
.

Let
A
be a simplicial commutative ring and
M
a connective
A
-module.

(1)

(Classical truncations)
There is a canonical equivalence of discrete
\pi_{0}A
-algebras

\pi_{0}(\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}(M))\simeq\pi_{0}(\operatorname{Sym}_{\pi_{0}A}^{*}(\pi_{0}M))\simeq S^{*}_{\pi_{0}A}(\pi_{0}M),

where
S_{\pi_{0}A}^{*}(\pi_{0}M)
denotes the classical symmetric algebra of the discrete module
\pi_{0}(M)
over the discrete commutative ring
\pi_{0}A
.

(2)

(Finiteness conditions)
The map of simplicial commutative rings
A\to\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}(M)
is of finite presentation to order
n
(resp. almost of ﬁnite presentation, locally of finite presentation) if and only if the
A
-module
M
is perfect to order
n
(resp. almost perfect, perfect).

(3)

(Relative cotangent complexes)
Let
L_{\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}(M)/A}
(resp.
L_{A/\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}(M)}
) denote the relative cotangent complex of the map of simplicial commutative rings
A\to\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}(M)
(resp. the augmentation map
\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}(M)\to A
). Then there are canonically equivalences
L_{\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}(M)/A}\simeq M\otimes_{A}\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}(M)\in\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}(M)}^{\rm cn}
and
L_{A/\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}(M)}\simeq\Sigma\,M\in\operatorname{Mod}_{A}^{\rm cn}
.

(4)

(Base-change) The functor
(A,M)\in\operatorname{SCRMod}^{\rm cn}\mapsto(A,\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}M)\in\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta})
carries
q
-coCartesian edges of
\operatorname{SCRMod}^{\rm cn}
to
\mathrm{ev}_{\{0\}}
-coCartesian edges of
\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta})
. In other words, for any map
A\to A^{\prime}
in
\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
and any connective
A
-module
M
, there is a canonical equivalence
A^{\prime}\otimes_{A}\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}(M)\simeq\operatorname{Sym}_{A^{\prime}}^{*}(A^{\prime}\otimes_{A}M)\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}_{A^{\prime}}
.

(5)

(Direct sums)
For
A,B\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
,
M\in\operatorname{Mod}^{\rm cn}_{A}
,
N\in\operatorname{Mod}^{\rm cn}_{B}
, there is a canonical equivalence
\operatorname{Sym}_{A\otimes B}^{*}(M\boxplus N)\simeq\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}(M)\boxtimes\operatorname{Sym}_{B}^{*}(N)
in
\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}_{A\otimes B}
. In particular, for any connective
A
-modules
M
and
M^{\prime}
, there is a canonical equivalence of algebras
\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}(M\oplus M^{\prime})\simeq\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}(M)\otimes_{A}\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}(M^{\prime})\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}_{A}
.

Proof.

Assertion (
5
) follows from the combination of Remark
2.14
and Proposition
2.7
: let
\mathcal{C}_{0}={\rm PolyMod^{ff}}
be as defined in Remark
2.14
, then it is direct to see that the restriction
(\operatorname{Sym}^{*}){|\mathcal{C}_{0}}\colon\mathcal{C}_{0}\to\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta})
preserves finite coproducts. Therefore, by virtue of Proposition
2.7
(
3
),
\operatorname{Sym}^{*}\colon\operatorname{SCRMod}^{\rm cn}\to\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta})
preserves finite coproducts. The last statement of (
5
) follows from base change along the multiplication map
A\otimes A\to A
.

We next prove assertion (
4
). To show the canonical map
\alpha_{(M,A^{\prime})}\colon A^{\prime}\otimes_{A}\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}(M)\to\operatorname{Sym}_{A^{\prime}}^{*}(A^{\prime}\otimes_{A}M)
is an equivalence, arguing as the proof of
[
SAG
, Proposition 25.2.3.1]
, (since the functor
M\mapsto\alpha_{(M,A^{\prime})}
commutes with sifted colimits) we may first reduce to the case where
M=A\times_{\mathbb{Z}}M_{0}
,
M_{0}\simeq\mathbb{Z}^{m}
. Then we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{\prime}\otimes_{A}A\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\operatorname{Sym}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{*}(M_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{\prime}\otimes_{A}\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}(A\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}M_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sym}_{A^{\prime}}^{*}(A^{\prime}\otimes_{A}A\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}M_{0}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{M}}

To show
\alpha_{M}
is an equivalence, it suffices to prove in the case where
A=\mathbb{Z}
. Since the functor
A^{\prime}\mapsto\alpha_{M,A^{\prime}}
commutes with sifted colimits, we may further reduce to the case where
A^{\prime}=\mathbb{Z}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}]
. In this case,
\alpha_{M}
is evidently an isomorphism of polynomial algebras.

For assertion (
3
), the formula
L_{\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}(M)/A}\simeq M\otimes_{A}\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}(M)
is
[
SAG
, Example 25.3.2.2]
. By virtue of
[
SAG
, Remark 25.3.2.5]
, the composition
A\to\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}(M)\to A
of simplicial commutative rings induces a
fiber sequence

A\otimes_{\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}(M)}L_{\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}(M)/A}\to L_{A/A}\to L_{A/\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}(M)}.

The formula
L_{A/\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}(M)}\simeq\Sigma\,M
follows from the above fiber sequence and
L_{A/A}\simeq 0
.

For assertion (
2
), if the map
A\to\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}(M)
is of finite presentation to order
n
(resp. almost of ﬁnite presentation, locally of finite presentation), then it follows from
[
DAG
, Proposition 3.2.14]
that
L_{\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}(M)/A}
is perfect to order
n
(resp., almost perfect, perfect) as an
\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}(M)
-module. Consequently,
M=L_{\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}(M)/A}\otimes_{\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}(M)}A
is perfect to order
n
(resp., almost perfect, perfect) as an
A
-module. For the converse direction, from the canonical equivalence of spaces

\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}_{A}}(\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}(M),B)\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{Mod}_{A}}(M,B^{\circ})

for all
B\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}_{A}
and the fact that
(\tau_{\leq n}B)^{\circ}\simeq\tau_{\leq n}(B^{\circ})
, we deduce that if
M
is perfect to order
n
(resp. perfect), then
A\to\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}(M)
is of finite presentation to order
n
(resp. locally of finite presentation).

Finally, we prove assertion (
1
). Apply assertion (
4
) to the map
A\to\pi_{0}(A)
, we obtain an equivalence
\pi_{0}(A)\otimes_{A}\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}(M)\simeq\operatorname{Sym}_{\pi_{0}A}^{*}(\pi_{0}A\otimes_{A}M)
. Consequently, we have

\pi_{0}(\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}M)\simeq\pi_{0}(\operatorname{Sym}_{\pi_{0}A}^{*}(\pi_{0}A\otimes_{A}M)).

Let
R=\pi_{0}A
. It remains to show that for
N=\pi_{0}(A)\otimes_{A}M\in\operatorname{Mod}^{\rm cn}_{R}
, there is a canonical equivalence
\pi_{0}(\operatorname{Sym}_{R}^{*}N)\simeq S_{R}^{*}(\pi_{0}N)
, where
S_{R}^{*}(\pi_{0}N)
is the classical symmetric algebra. Let
P_{\bullet}
be a simplicial resolution of
N
, where each
P_{n}
is a projective
R
-module. Since the derived symmetric algebra functor
\operatorname{Sym}_{R}^{*}(\underline{\hphantom{A}})
preserve sifted colimits, we obtain equivalences

\operatorname{Sym}_{R}^{*}(M)\simeq|\operatorname{Sym}_{R}^{*}(P_{\bullet})|\simeq|S_{R}^{*}(P_{\bullet})|.

Consequently,
\pi_{0}(\operatorname{Sym}_{R}^{*}(N))
is isomorphic to the colimit of the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{R}^{*}(P_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{R}^{*}(P_{0})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{R}^{*}(d_{1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{R}^{*}(d_{0})}

(where
d_{0},d_{1}\colon P_{1}\rightrightarrows P_{0}
are the face maps). On the other hand, the colimit of the above diagram is canonically isomorphic to
S_{R}^{*}(\pi_{0}N)
, by the right-exactness of the classical symmetric algebra functors (see, for example,
[
Bou
, Chapter III, §6.2, Proposition 4, p.499]
).
∎

Proposition 2.17
.

Let
R
be a simplicial commutative ring, and let
\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M
be a map of connective
R
-modules. Then there is a pushout diagram in
\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}_{R}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sym}_{R}^{*}(M^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sym}_{R}^{*}(M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sym}_{R}^{*}({\rm cofib}(\rho)).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sym}_{R}^{*}(\rho)}

Proof.

Being a left adjoint (Definition
2.12
), the functor
\operatorname{Sym}_{R}^{*}
preserves small colimits. Consequently, the desired result follows from applying
\operatorname{Sym}_{R}^{*}
to the pushout square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{cofib}(\rho).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}

∎

2.5.
Universal fiber sequences associated to symmetric powers

In this subsection, we review and generalizes Lurie’s construction in his proof of Illusie’s result
[
SAG
, Proposition 25.2.4.1]
to obtain universal fiber sequences associated to symmetric powers.

Notation 2.18
.

Let
\mathcal{E}
denote the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\operatorname{SCRMod}^{\rm cn})\times_{\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta})}\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
whose objects are pairs
(A,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M)
, where
A
is a simplicial commutative ring and
\rho
is a morphism of connective
A
-modules. Let
\mathcal{D}
denote the full subcategory of the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1}\times\Delta^{1},\operatorname{SCRMod}^{\rm cn})\times_{\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1}\times\Delta^{1},\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta})}\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
spanned by objects
(A,\alpha)
, where
A
is a simplicial commutative ring, and
\alpha
is a fiber sequence of connective
A
-modules:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M^{\prime\prime}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}

As usual, we will abbreviate the above fiber sequence as
\alpha\colon M^{\prime}\to M\to M^{\prime\prime}
. Let
\theta\colon\mathcal{D}\to\mathcal{E}
denote the forgetful functor which carries
(A,\alpha\colon M^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\rho}M\xrightarrow{\varphi}M^{\prime\prime})
to
(A,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M)
. Then by virtue of
[
HA
, Remark 1.1.1.7]
, that is, by applying
[
HTT
, Proposition 4.3.2.15]
twice, we deduce that
\theta
is a trivial Kan fibration.

Let
\mathcal{E}_{0}\subseteq\mathcal{E}
be the full subcategory spanned by those pairs
(R,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M)
, where
R
is a polynomial ring
\mathbb{Z}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}]
and
\rho
fits into a short exact sequence
0\to M^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\rho}M\to M^{\prime\prime}\to 0
of finitely generated free
R
-modules. Similarly, let
\mathcal{D}_{0}\subseteq\mathcal{D}
be the full subcategory spanned by those pairs
(R,\alpha)
, where
R
is a polynomial ring
\mathbb{Z}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}]
, and
\alpha
is a short exact sequence
0\to M^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\rho}M\xrightarrow{\rho^{\prime}}M^{\prime\prime}\to 0
of finitely generated free
R
-modules. Then the argument of the proof of
[
SAG
, Proposition 25.2.4.1]
shows that the inclusions
\mathcal{E}_{0}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{E}
and
\mathcal{D}_{0}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{D}
induce equivalences of
\infty
-categories
\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\mathcal{E}_{0})\simeq\mathcal{E}
and
\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\mathcal{D}_{0})\simeq\mathcal{D}
, respectively.

Construction 2.19

(Koszul complexes)
.

Let
R
be a discrete commutative ring, and suppose we are given a morphism
\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M
of finite generated locally free
R
-modules. Let
S_{R}^{*}M
denote the ordinary symmetric algebra generated by
M
over
R
. Then there is canonically a Koszul complex of locally free
S_{R}^{*}(M)
-modules of finite rank:

(2.1)

\displaystyle\cdots\to(\bigwedge\nolimits_{R}^{2}M^{\prime})\otimes_{R}(S_{R}^{*}M)\to(\bigwedge\nolimits_{R}^{1}M^{\prime})\otimes_{R}(S_{R}^{*}M)\to(\bigwedge\nolimits_{R}^{0}M^{\prime})\otimes_{R}(S_{R}^{*}M)

associated to the cosection
M^{\prime}\otimes_{R}S_{R}^{*}(M)\to S_{R}^{*}(M)
induced by
\rho
. Restricting to homogeneous elements of degree
n
, we obtain a complex of locally free
R
-modules of finite rank:

(2.2)

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}^{n}(R,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M)\colon\quad\bigwedge\nolimits^{n}_{R}M^{\prime}\xrightarrow{d_{n}}\cdots\xrightarrow{d_{2}}\bigwedge\nolimits_{R}^{1}M^{\prime}\otimes_{R}(S_{R}^{n-1}M)\xrightarrow{d_{1}}S_{R}^{n}M.

The construction
(R,\rho)\mapsto(R,\mathbf{S}^{n}(R,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M))
determines a functor from the ordinary category of pairs
(R,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M)
, where
R
is a (discrete) commutative ring and
\rho
is a morphism of finite generated locally free
R
-modules, to the ordinary category category of pairs
(R,M_{*})
, where
R
is a (discrete) commutative ring and
M_{*}
is a non-negatively graded chain complexes of finitely generated locally free
R
-modules. By construction, the formation of
\mathbf{S}^{n}(R,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M)
commutes with base change
R\to R^{\prime}
of discrete commutative rings. For any integer
n^{\prime}\geq 0
, from the
S_{R}^{*}(M)
-module structure of (
2.1
), multiplication with elements of
S_{R}^{n^{\prime}}M
induces a morphism of non-negatively graded chain complexes of finitely generated locally free
R
-modules:

\mathbf{S}^{n}(R,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M)\otimes_{R}(S_{R}^{n^{\prime}}M)\to\mathbf{S}^{n+n^{\prime}}(R,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M).

Remark 2.20
.

By choosing local basis for the locally free modules
M^{\prime}
and
M
, respectively, the differentials
d_{i}
of the complex
\mathbf{S}^{n}(R,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M)
of (
2.2
) can be described explicit as follows: for all local elements
x_{1},\ldots,x_{i}\in M^{\prime}
and
y\in S_{R}^{d-i}M
, we have

\displaystyle d_{i}(x_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge x_{i}\otimes y)=\sum_{j=1}^{i}(-1)^{j-1}(x_{1}\wedge\cdots\widehat{x_{j}}\cdots\wedge x_{i})\otimes(\rho(x_{j})\cdot y)\in\bigwedge\nolimits_{R}^{i-1}M^{\prime}\otimes_{R}S_{R}^{d-i+1}M.

Variant 2.21
.

There is an analogous construction for exterior algebra. Let
R
be a discrete commutative ring, and suppose we are given a morphism
\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M
of finite generated locally free
R
-modules. For any
n\geq 0
, there is a Koszul complex of exterior type

(2.3)

\mathbf{\Lambda}^{n}(R,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M)\colon\quad\Gamma_{R}^{n}(M^{\prime})\xrightarrow{d_{n}^{\prime}}\cdots\xrightarrow{d_{2}^{\prime}}\Gamma_{R}^{1}(M^{\prime})\otimes_{R}\bigwedge\nolimits_{R}^{n-1}(M)\xrightarrow{d_{1}^{\prime}}\bigwedge\nolimits_{R}^{n}M

which could be defined by the following formula:

\mathbf{\Lambda}^{n}(R,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M)=\Sigma^{n}\,(\mathbf{S}^{n}(R,\rho^{\vee}\colon M^{\vee}\to M^{\prime\vee}))^{\vee}.

(Here
(\underline{\hphantom{A}})^{\vee}=\operatorname{Hom}_{R}(\underline{\hphantom{A}},R)=\operatorname{Ext}^{0}_{R}(\underline{\hphantom{A}},R)
denotes the operation of taking duals over
R
in the classical sense.) Concretely, the differentials
d_{j}^{\prime}
of the complex (
2.3
) are described by the following formula: for all local elements
g\in\bigwedge^{d-j}M
and
\varepsilon_{1}^{(\nu_{1})}\cdots\varepsilon_{m^{\prime}}^{(\nu_{m^{\prime}})}\in\Gamma^{j}(M^{\prime})
(where
\varepsilon_{1},\ldots,\varepsilon_{m^{\prime}}
is a local basis of
M^{\prime}
,
\nu_{i}\geq 0
and
\sum\nu_{i}=j
), we have

d_{j}^{\prime}(g\otimes\varepsilon_{1}^{(\nu_{1})}\cdots\varepsilon_{m^{\prime}}^{(\nu_{m^{\prime}})})=\sum_{i=1}^{m^{\prime}}(\rho(\varepsilon_{i})\wedge g)\otimes\varepsilon_{1}^{(\nu_{1})}\cdots\varepsilon_{i}^{(\nu_{i}-1)}\cdots\varepsilon_{m^{\prime}}^{(\nu_{m^{\prime}})}\in\bigwedge\nolimits_{R}^{d-j+1}M\otimes_{R}\Gamma_{R}^{j-1}M^{\prime}.

The formation of
\mathbf{\Lambda}^{n}(R,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M)
enjoys similar functorial properties as the formation of
\mathbf{S}^{n}(R,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M)
, and all the constructions and results of this subsection have direct analogue for the exterior powers. The complex
\mathbf{\Lambda}^{n}(R,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M)
will not play an essential role in this paper; However, it is useful for computations in various situations.

Remark 2.22
.

The totality
\mathbf{S}^{*}(R,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M)=\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}\mathbf{S}^{n}(R,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M)
has a canonical graded
R
-Hopf algebra structure and a bi-graded dga structure; The totality
\mathbf{\Lambda}^{*}(R,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M)=\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}\mathbf{\Lambda}^{n}(R,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M)
has a canonical bigraded
R
-Hopf algebra structure and a bi-graded dga structure. We will not use these algebra structures in this paper; but they will play a role in the theory of derived Schur functors in our forthcoming paper.

Construction 2.23
.

Following Lurie
[
SAG
, proof of Proposition 25.2.4.1]
, we let
R
be a discrete commutative ring, and consider a short exact sequence of finite generated free
R
-modules

0\to M^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\rho}M\to M^{\prime\prime}\to 0.

Then the Koszul complex of (
2.1
) is an exact sequence of finite generated free
S_{R}^{*}(M)
-modules that resolves
S_{R}^{*}M^{\prime\prime}
. Restricting to homogeneous elements of degree
n
, we obtain that the complex
\mathbf{S}^{n}(R,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M)
of (
2.2
) is a resolution of the module
S_{R}^{n}M^{\prime\prime}
. Let
f_{i,n}(R,\rho)
denote the image of the differential
d_{i}
of
\mathbf{S}^{n}(R,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M)
, and let
\alpha_{i,n}(R,\rho)
denote the short exact sequence:

0\to f_{i+1,n}(R,\rho)\xrightarrow{a_{i}}(\bigwedge\nolimits^{i}_{R}M^{\prime})\otimes_{R}(S_{R}^{n-i}M)\xrightarrow{b_{i}}f_{i,n}(R,\rho)\to 0.

Then
a_{i-1}\circ b_{i}=d_{i}
, and we have canonical equivalences:

f_{0,n}(R,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M)\simeq S_{R}^{n}M^{\prime\prime},\qquad f_{n,n}(R,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M)\simeq\bigwedge\nolimits_{R}^{n}M^{\prime}.

For any integer
n^{\prime}\geq 0
, multiplication with elements of
S_{R}^{n^{\prime}}M
induces functors

\alpha_{i,n}(R,\rho)\otimes_{R}S_{R}^{n^{\prime}}M\to\alpha_{i,n+n^{\prime}}(R,\rho)

for which the induced map on middle terms coincides with the map induced by the multiplication map
S_{R}^{n-i}M\otimes_{R}S_{R}^{n^{\prime}}M\to S_{R}^{n+n^{\prime}-i}M
.
Since
\mathcal{P}_{\sigma}(\mathcal{E}_{0})\simeq\mathcal{E}
, and
\mathcal{D}
admits sifted colimits, by virtue of Proposition
2.7
, for each integer
0\leq i\leq n-1
, the functors
\mathcal{E}_{0}\to\mathcal{E}
and
\mathcal{E}_{0}\to\mathcal{D}
,

(R,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M)\mapsto(R,d_{i+1}\text{~of~}\mathbf{S}^{n}(R,\rho))\qquad(R,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M)\mapsto(R,\alpha_{i,n}(R,\rho))

have essentially unique extensions
D_{i+1,n}\colon\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}
and
U_{i,n}\colon\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{D}
,

(A,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M)\mapsto(A,d_{i+1,n}(A,\rho))\qquad(A,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M)\mapsto(A,\alpha_{i,n}(A,\rho)),

respectively; here
d_{i+1,n}(A,\rho)
is a morphism of connective
A
-modules:

(\bigwedge\nolimits^{i+1}_{A}M^{\prime})\otimes_{R}(\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{n-i-1}M)\to(\bigwedge\nolimits^{i}_{A}M^{\prime})\otimes_{R}(\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{n-i}M),

and
\alpha_{i,n}(A,\rho)
is a fibre sequence of connective
A
-modules:

F_{i+1,n}(A,\rho)\xrightarrow{a_{i,n}(A,\rho)}(\bigwedge\nolimits^{i}_{A}M^{\prime})\otimes_{A}(\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{n-i}M)\xrightarrow{b_{i,n}(A,\rho)}F_{i,n}(A,\rho).

Moreover, there are canonical equivalences

d_{i,n}(A,\rho)\circ d_{i+1,n}(A,\rho)\simeq 0,\qquad a_{i,n}(A,\rho)\circ b_{i+1,n}(A,\rho)\simeq d_{i+1,n}(A,\rho),

F_{0,n}(A,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M)\simeq\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{n}(\operatorname{cofib}(\rho)),\qquad F_{0,n}(A,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M)\simeq\bigwedge\nolimits_{A}^{n}M^{\prime}.

Furthermore, for any integer
n^{\prime}\geq 0
, there are canonical morphisms of fiber sequences

\alpha_{i,n}(A,\rho)\otimes_{A}\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{n^{\prime}}M\to\alpha_{i,n+n^{\prime}}(A,\rho)

for which the induced map on middle terms coincides with the map induced by the multiplication map
\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{n-i}M\otimes_{A}\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{n^{\prime}}M\to\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{n+n^{\prime}-i}M
of Remark
2.13
.

Remark 2.24
.

The functors
D_{i,n}\colon\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}
,
(A,\rho)\mapsto(A,d_{i,n}(A,\rho))
, where
1\leq i\leq n
, by construction commute with sifted colimits, and the diagrams

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D_{i,n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{id}}

commute up to canonical equivalences (here the vertical arrow
q
is the natural forgetful map the carries
(A,\rho)
to
A
). Furthermore,
[
SAG
, Proposition 25.2.3.1]
implies that the functor
D_{i,n}\colon\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}
carries
q
-coCartesian edges to
q
-coCartesian edges.

Remark 2.25

(Base change)
.

The functors
U_{i,n}\colon\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{D}
,
(A,\rho)\mapsto(A,\alpha_{i,n}(A,\rho))
, where
0\leq i\leq n-1
, by construction commute with sifted colimits, and the diagrams

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{i,n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{id}}

commute up to canonical equivalences (here the vertical arrows
q
and
q^{\prime}
are the natural forgetful maps). We claim that each functor
U_{i,n}\colon\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{D}
carries
q
-coCartesian edges to
q^{\prime}
-coCartesian edges. That is, for every morphism
\phi\colon A\to B
of simplicial commutative rings and every morphism
\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M
of connective
A
-modules, the canonical map

B\otimes_{A}\alpha_{i,n}(A,\rho)\to\alpha_{i,n}(B,B\otimes_{A}\rho)

is an equivalence in
\mathcal{D}\times_{\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}}\{B\}
. In fact, for a given
n\geq 0
, we prove by induction on
0\leq i\leq n-1
. In the case
i=0
, the equivalence
B\otimes_{A}\alpha_{0}(A,\rho)\simeq\alpha_{0}(B,B\otimes_{A}\rho)
follows from the fact that the functor
\operatorname{Mod}_{A}\to\operatorname{Mod}_{B}
,
M\mapsto B\otimes_{A}M
is exact, and the formations of
\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{n}M
and
\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{n}(\operatorname{cofib}(\rho))
commute with base change (see
[
SAG
, Proposition 25.2.3.1]
). For the induction step, the case of
(i+1)
follows from the case
i
by virtue of
loc. cit.
and the fact that the final vertex of
\alpha_{i+1}(A,\rho)
coincides with the initial vertex of
\alpha_{i,n}(A,\rho)
.

Construction 2.26
.

Let
R
be a discrete commutative ring, and suppose we are given a short exact sequence
0\to M^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\rho}M\to M^{\prime\prime}\to 0
of finite generated free
R
-modules. For integers
n\geq 0
and
0\leq i\leq n
, we let
g_{i,n}(R,\rho)\in\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\mathrm{cn}}
denote the class of the “brutal” truncation

(\bigwedge\nolimits^{i}_{R}M^{\prime})\otimes_{R}(S_{R}^{n-i}M)\xrightarrow{d_{i}}\cdots\to M^{\prime}\otimes_{R}S_{R}^{n-1}M\xrightarrow{d_{1}}S_{R}^{n}M

of the complex (
2.2
). Then there are canonical equivalences

g_{0,n}(R,\rho)=S_{R}^{n}M,\qquad g_{0,n}(R,\rho)\simeq S_{R}^{n}M^{\prime\prime}.

Since the
R
-modules

\operatorname{Ext}^{s}_{R}\left((\bigwedge\nolimits_{R}^{i}M^{\prime})\otimes_{R}(S_{R}^{n-i}M),(\bigwedge\nolimits_{R}^{j}M^{\prime})\otimes_{R}(S_{R}^{n-j}M)\right)

vanish for all
s\neq 0
and
i,j\in\mathbb{Z}
(here
M,M^{\prime}
are finite generated free
R
-modules), the exact sequence (
2.2
) determines essentially unique fiber sequences

\beta_{i,n}(R,\rho)\colon\quad\Sigma^{i}(\bigwedge\nolimits_{R}^{i+1}M^{\prime})\otimes_{R}(S_{R}^{n-i-1}M)\to g_{i,n}(R,\rho)\to g_{i+1}(R,\rho).

For any integer
n^{\prime}\geq 0
, multiplication with elements of
S_{R}^{n^{\prime}}M
induces functors

\beta_{i,n}(R,\rho)\otimes_{R}S_{R}^{n^{\prime}}M\to\beta_{i,n+n^{\prime}}(R,\rho)

for which the induced map on middle terms coincides with the map induced by the multiplication map
S_{R}^{n-i-1}M\otimes_{R}S_{R}^{n^{\prime}}M\to S_{R}^{n+n^{\prime}-i-1}M
.
As in Construction
2.23
, by virtue of Proposition
2.7
, the functors
\mathcal{E}_{0}\to\mathcal{D}
,

(R,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M)\mapsto(R,\beta_{i,n}(R,\rho))

extend essentially uniquely to functors
V_{i,n}\colon\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{D}
,

(A,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M)\mapsto(A,\beta_{i,n}(A,\rho)),

where
\beta_{i,n}(A,\rho)
is a fibre sequence of connective
A
-modules:

\Sigma^{i}(\bigwedge\nolimits_{A}^{i+1}M^{\prime})\otimes_{A}(\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{n-i-1}M)\xrightarrow{c_{i,n}(A,\rho)}G_{i,n}(A,\rho)\to G_{i+1,n}(A,\rho).

Let
e_{i,n}(A,\rho)
denote the morphism
G_{i+1,n}(A,\rho)\to\Sigma^{i+1}(\bigwedge\nolimits_{A}^{i+1}M^{\prime})\otimes_{R}(\operatorname{Sym}^{n-i-1}M)
obtained from
\beta_{i,n}(A,\rho)
. Then the composition of the natural maps

\Sigma^{i}(\bigwedge\nolimits_{A}^{i+1}M^{\prime})\otimes_{A}(\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{n-i-1}M)\xrightarrow{c_{i,n}(A,\rho)}G_{i,n}(A,\rho)\xrightarrow{e_{i-1,n}(A,\rho)}\Sigma^{i}(\bigwedge\nolimits_{A}^{i}M^{\prime})\otimes_{A}(\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{n-i}M)

is canonically equivalent to
\Sigma^{i}\circ d_{i+1,n}(A,\rho)
, and there are canonical equivalences:

G_{0,n}(A,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M)\simeq\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{n}M,\qquad G_{n,n}(A,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M)\simeq\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{n}(\operatorname{cofib}(\rho)).

For any integer
n^{\prime}\geq 0
, there are canonical morphisms of functors

\beta_{i,n}(A,\rho)\otimes_{A}\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{n^{\prime}}M\to\beta_{i,n+n^{\prime}}(A,\rho)

for which the induced map on the initial vertices coincides with the map induced by the multiplication
\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{n-i-1}M\otimes_{A}\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{n^{\prime}}M\to\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{n+n^{\prime}-i-1}M
of Remark
2.13
.

Remark 2.27

(Base change)
.

For all
0\leq i\leq n-1
, by construction the functors
V_{i,n}\colon\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{D}
,
(A,\rho)\mapsto(A,\beta(A,\rho))
commute with sifted colimits, and the following diagrams

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{i,n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{id}}

commute up to canonical equivalences (where the vertical arrows
q
and
q^{\prime}
are the natural forgetful maps). By a similar argument as in Remark
2.25
, we deduce that each functor
V_{i,n}\colon\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{D}
carries
q
-coCartesian edges to
q^{\prime}
-coCartesian edges. In other words, for every morphism
\phi\colon A\to B
of simplicial commutative rings and every morphism
\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M
of connective
A
-modules, the canonical map

B\otimes_{A}\beta_{i,n}(A,\rho)\to\beta_{i,n}(B,B\otimes_{A}\rho)

is an equivalence in
\mathcal{D}\times_{\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}}\{B\}
.

Lemma 2.28
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a stable
\infty
-category (or a prestable
\infty
-category in the sense of
[
SAG
, Deﬁnition C.1.2.1]
), let
n\geq 1
be an integer, and suppose we are given a composable sequence of morphisms in
\mathcal{C}
:

C_{n}\xrightarrow{d_{n}}C_{n-1}\xrightarrow{d_{n-1}}\cdots\to C_{1}\xrightarrow{d_{1}}C_{0}

for which the successive compositions satisfy
d_{i}\circ d_{i+1}\simeq 0
,
1\leq i\leq n-1
. Assume that

\operatorname{Ext}^{-k}_{\mathcal{C}}(C_{i+j},C_{i}):=\pi_{k}(\mathrm{Map}_{\mathcal{C}}(C_{i+j},C_{i}))\simeq 0\quad\text{for}\quad i\geq 0,j\geq 1,k\geq 1.

Then there are essentially unique objects
\{D_{i}\in\mathcal{C}\}_{0\leq i\leq n}
that fit into essentially unique cofiber sequences
\beta_{i}\colon\Sigma^{i}C_{i+1}\xrightarrow{f_{i}}D_{i}\xrightarrow{g_{i}}D_{i+1}
such that
D_{0}=C_{0}
,
f_{0}=d_{1}
, and
h_{i-1}\circ f_{i}\simeq\Sigma^{i}\,d_{i+1}
, where
h_{i}\colon D_{i+1}\to\Sigma^{i+1}C_{i+1}
denotes the connecting morphism determined by
\beta_{i}
.

Proof.

This result is well-known in the context of triangulated categories (see, for example,
[
O97
, Lemma 1.5]
), and the same proof strategy works here. We will illustrate with the case
n=2
; the general situation follows from a similar argument and induction. Let
D_{1}
be the cofiber of
d_{1}\colon C_{1}\to C_{0}
, let
\beta_{0}\colon C_{1}\xrightarrow{f_{0}=d_{1}}C_{0}\xrightarrow{g_{0}}D_{1}
denote the corresponding cofiber sequence, and let
h_{0}\colon D_{1}\to\Sigma C_{1}
denote the essentially unique connecting morphism determined by
\beta_{0}
. From the fiber sequences of spaces

\mathrm{Map}_{\mathcal{C}}(\Sigma C_{2},D_{1})\xrightarrow{h_{0}\circ}\mathrm{Map}_{\mathcal{C}}(\Sigma C_{2},\Sigma C_{1})\xrightarrow{\Sigma d_{1}\circ}\mathrm{Map}_{\mathcal{C}}(\Sigma C_{2},\Sigma C_{0}),

there exists an element
f_{1}\in\mathrm{Map}_{\mathcal{C}}(\Sigma C_{2},D_{1})
lifting the element
\Sigma d_{2}\in\mathrm{Map}_{\mathcal{C}}(\Sigma C_{2},\Sigma C_{1})
. The space
\Omega\mathrm{Map}(\Sigma C_{2},C_{0})
of such liftings is contractible by our assumption and Whitehead’s theorem (
[
HTT
, p.16]
). Therefore,
f_{1}
is determined up to contractible choices. Let
D_{2}
be the cofiber of
f_{1}
, and
\beta_{1}
the corresponding cofiber sequence. Then
D_{2}
(resp.
\beta_{1}
) exists and is determined up to contractible choices. This proves the desired assertion.
∎

Remark 2.29
.

We see from the above proof Lemma
2.28
that the conditions of the lemma are much stronger than necessary (for example, only the conditions
\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{C}}^{-k}(C_{2},C_{0})\simeq 0
,
k\geq 1
, were used in the proof of the case
n=2
). However, the above version is enough for the purpose of applications in this paper; in a forthcoming paper, we plan to include a more systematical study of these systems and stronger versions of Lemma
2.28
.

Corollary 2.30

(Illusie)
.

Let
R
be a discrete commutative ring, and let
\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M
be a morphism of finitely generated locally free
R
-modules. Let
n\geq 0
be an integer, and let
\mathbf{S}^{n}(R,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M)
denote the Koszul complex (
2.2
). Then there is a canonical equivalence

[\mathbf{S}^{n}(R,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M)]\simeq\operatorname{Sym}_{R}^{n}(\operatorname{cofib}(M^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\rho}M))\in\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\mathrm{cn}}

where
[\mathbf{S}^{n}(R,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M)]
denotes the image of the complex
\mathbf{S}^{n}(R,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M)
under the canonical equivalence
\mathcal{D}_{\geq 0}(\operatorname{Ch}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\heartsuit}))\simeq\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\mathrm{cn}}
of
[
HA
, Proposition 7.1.1.15]
.

Proof.

By virtue of Lemma
2.28
, we have a canonical equivalence
[\mathbf{S}^{n}(R,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M)]\simeq G_{n,n}(R,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M)
(where
G_{i,n}(R,\rho)
is defined as in Construction
2.26
). Then the desired assertion follows from the properties of the fiber sequences
\beta_{i}(A,\rho)
of Construction
2.26
.
∎

Variant 2.31

(Illusie)
.

In the same situation of as Corollary
2.30
, let
\mathbf{\Lambda}^{n}(R,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M)
denote the Koszul complex of exterior type (
2.3
). Then there is a canonical equivalence

[\mathbf{\Lambda}^{n}(R,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M)]\simeq\bigwedge\nolimits_{R}^{n}(\operatorname{cofib}(M^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\rho}M))\in\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\mathrm{cn}}.

Proof.

The proof is completely analogous to the proof of Corollary
2.30
.
∎

2.6.
Filtrations of symmetric powers

In this subsection, we review and slightly generalizes Lurie’s
[
SAG
, Construction 25.2.5.4]
. Let
R
be a discrete commutative ring, and suppose we are given a short exact sequence of finite generated free
R
-modules:

0\to M^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\varphi}M\to M^{\prime\prime}\to 0.

For each non-negative integer
n
and an integer
0\leq i\leq n
, there are natural morphisms
(S_{R}^{n-i}M^{\prime})\otimes_{R}(S_{R}^{i}M)\to S_{R}^{n}M
,
(\bigwedge_{R}^{n-i}M^{\prime})\otimes_{R}(\bigwedge_{R}^{i}M)\to\bigwedge_{R}^{n}M
, and
(\Gamma_{R}^{i}M^{\prime})\otimes_{R}(\Gamma_{R}^{n-i}M)\to\Gamma_{R}^{n}M
given by the respective multiplication maps; We let
\overline{F}^{i}(R,\varphi)\subseteq S_{R}^{n}M
,
\overline{G}^{i}(R,\varphi)\subseteq\bigwedge_{R}^{n}M
, and
\overline{H}^{i}(R,\varphi)\subseteq\Gamma_{R}^{n}M
denote the corresponding images. Then we obtain finite filtrations

\displaystyle 0=\overline{F}^{-1}(R,\varphi)\subseteq\overline{F}^{0}(R,\varphi)\subseteq\cdots\subseteq\overline{F}^{n}(R,\varphi)=S_{R}^{n}(M),

\displaystyle 0=\overline{G}^{-1}(R,\varphi)\subseteq\overline{G}^{0}(R,\varphi)\subseteq\cdots\subseteq\overline{G}^{n}(R,\varphi)=\bigwedge\nolimits_{R}^{n}(M),

\displaystyle 0=\overline{H}^{-1}(R,\varphi)\subseteq\overline{H}^{0}(R,\varphi)\subseteq\cdots\subseteq\overline{H}^{n}(R,\varphi)=\Gamma_{R}^{n}(M),

respectively, such that the induced maps to the respective successive quotients

\displaystyle S_{R}^{n-i}M^{\prime}\otimes S_{R}^{i}M^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle\to\overline{F}^{i}(R,\varphi)/\overline{F}^{i-1}(R,\varphi),

\displaystyle\bigwedge\nolimits_{R}^{n-i}M^{\prime}\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits_{R}^{i}M^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle\to\overline{G}^{i}(R,\varphi)/\overline{G}^{i-1}(R,\varphi),

\displaystyle\Gamma_{R}^{n-i}M^{\prime}\otimes\Gamma_{R}^{i}M^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle\to\overline{H}^{i}(R,\varphi)/\overline{H}^{i-1}(R,\varphi)

are isomorphisms. In particular, for each
0\leq i\leq n
, we obtain canonical fiber sequences

\displaystyle\alpha^{i}(R,\varphi)\colon\qquad\overline{F}^{i-1}(R,\varphi)\to\overline{F}^{i}(R,\varphi)\to(S_{R}^{n-i}M^{\prime})\otimes_{R}(S_{R}^{i}M^{\prime\prime}),

\displaystyle\beta^{i}(R,\varphi)\colon\qquad\overline{G}^{i-1}(R,\varphi)\to\overline{G}^{i}(R,\varphi)\to(\bigwedge\nolimits_{R}^{n-i}M^{\prime})\otimes_{R}(\bigwedge\nolimits_{R}^{i}M^{\prime\prime}),

\displaystyle\gamma^{i}(R,\varphi)\colon\qquad\overline{H}^{i-1}(R,\varphi)\to\overline{H}^{i}(R,\varphi)\to(\Gamma_{R}^{n-i}M^{\prime})\otimes_{R}(\Gamma_{R}^{i}M^{\prime\prime}).

Let
\mathcal{E}_{0},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{D}
be the
\infty
-categories defined in §
2.5
, Notation
2.18
, then by virtue of Proposition
2.7
, the above functors extend essentially uniquely to functors

\mathcal{E}\to\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{n},\operatorname{SCRMod}^{\rm cn})\times_{\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{n},\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta})}\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta},

\displaystyle(A,\varphi\colon M^{\prime}\to M)\mapsto(A,F^{0}(A,\varphi)\xrightarrow{d_{1}}F^{1}(A,\varphi)\to\cdots\to F^{n}(A,\varphi))

\displaystyle(A,\varphi\colon M^{\prime}\to M)\mapsto(A,G^{0}(A,\varphi)\xrightarrow{e_{1}}G^{1}(A,\varphi)\to\cdots\to G^{n}(A,\varphi))

\displaystyle(A,\varphi\colon M^{\prime}\to M)\mapsto(A,H^{0}(A,\varphi)\xrightarrow{f_{1}}H^{1}(A,\varphi)\to\cdots\to H^{n}(A,\varphi))

and functors
\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{D}
,

\displaystyle(A,\varphi\colon M^{\prime}\to M)\mapsto(A,\alpha^{i}(A,\varphi)\colon F^{i-1}(A,\varphi)\xrightarrow{d_{i}}F^{i}(A,\varphi)\to(\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{n-i}M^{\prime})\otimes_{A}(\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{i}M^{\prime\prime})),

\displaystyle(A,\varphi\colon M^{\prime}\to M)\mapsto(A,\beta^{i}(A,\varphi)\colon G^{i-1}(A,\varphi)\xrightarrow{e_{i}}G^{i}(A,\varphi)\to(\bigwedge\nolimits_{A}^{n-i}M^{\prime})\otimes_{A}(\bigwedge\nolimits_{A}^{i}M^{\prime\prime})),

\displaystyle(A,\varphi\colon M^{\prime}\to M)\mapsto(A,\gamma^{i}(A,\varphi)\colon H^{i-1}(A,\varphi)\xrightarrow{f_{i}}H^{i}(A,\varphi)\to(\Gamma_{A}^{n-i}M^{\prime})\otimes_{A}(\Gamma_{A}^{i}M^{\prime\prime})),

where
\alpha_{i,n}(A,\varphi)
,
\beta_{i,n}(A,\varphi)
, and
\gamma_{i}(A,\varphi)
are cofiber sequences, and
M^{\prime\prime}
is the cofiber of
\rho
. Moreover, we have canonical equivalences:

\displaystyle F^{0}(A,\varphi)=\operatorname{Sym}^{n}_{A}M^{\prime}

\displaystyle F^{n}(A,\varphi)=\operatorname{Sym}^{n}_{A}M,

\displaystyle G^{0}(A,\varphi)=\bigwedge\nolimits^{n}_{A}M^{\prime}

\displaystyle G^{n}(A,\varphi)=\bigwedge\nolimits^{n}_{A}M,

\displaystyle H^{0}(A,\varphi)=\Gamma^{n}_{A}M^{\prime}

\displaystyle H^{n}(A,\varphi)=\Gamma^{n}_{A}M.

Example 2.32
.

Let
A
be a simplicial commutative ring, and
\rho\colon M\to M^{\prime}
a morphism of connective
A
-modules such that
\operatorname{cofib}(A,\varphi)
is a line bundle (that is, a locally free module of rank
1
). Then for each
n\geq 1
, we obtain a fiber sequence:

\beta^{1}(A,\rho)\colon\qquad\bigwedge\nolimits_{A}^{n}M^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\bigwedge^{n}\varphi}\bigwedge\nolimits_{A}^{n}M\to\left(\bigwedge\nolimits_{A}^{n-1}M^{\prime}\right)\otimes_{A}\operatorname{cofib}(A,\varphi).

3.
Quasi-coherent sheaves and algebras on prestacks

In this section, we first review Lurie’s machinery in
[
SAG
, §6.2.1]
, which allows one to “globalize” constructions from affine cases to the cases of general prestacks (§
3.1
). In §
3.2
, we review the theory of quasi-coherent sheaves on prestacks. In §
3.3
, we apply the machinery of §
3.1
to construct the
\infty
-category of quasi-coherent algebras
\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}(X)
on a prestack
X
, study its basic properties and define
relative spectra
. Finally, in §
3.4
and §
3.5
, we apply the machinery of §
3.1
to globalize the constructions and results of §
2
on symmetric, exterior, and divided powers, and symmetric algebras, respectively, to the case of prestacks.

3.1.
\infty
-categories of sheaves of objects on prestacks

Let
q\colon\mathcal{C}\to\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
be a coCartesian fibration. In this subsection, we will apply the machinery developed by Lurie in
[
SAG
, §6.2.1]
to construct an
\infty
-category
\underline{\mathcal{C}}(X)
of “sheaf of objects of
\mathcal{C}
on a prestack
X
”. Recall that a prestack
X
is functor from the
\infty
-category of simplicial commutative rings
\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
to the
\infty
-category of spaces
\mathcal{S}
. By virtue of
[
HTT
, Proposition 3.3.2.5]
, a prestack
X
is classified by a left fibration which we denote by
\int_{\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}}X\to\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
. By virtue of
[
SAG
, Construction 6.2.1.7]
, the coCartesian fibration
q\colon\mathcal{C}\to\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
determines a functor

\Phi(q)\colon\mathrm{Fun}(\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta},\mathcal{S})^{\mathrm{op}}\to\widehat{\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}}_{\infty}

which carries a prestack
X\in\mathrm{Fun}(\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta},\mathcal{S})
to the
\infty
-category

\underline{\mathcal{C}}(X)=\mathrm{Fun}_{/\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}}^{\rm CCart}(\int_{\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}}X,\mathcal{C}),

where
\mathrm{Fun}_{/\mathcal{E}}^{\rm CCart}(\int_{\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}}X,\mathcal{C})
denotes the
\infty
-category of functors from
\int_{\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}}X
to
\mathcal{C}
which commute with their projections to
\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
and carry each edge of
\int_{\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}}X
to a coCartesian edge of
q\colon\mathcal{C}\to\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
(see
[
SAG
, Definition 6.2.1.1]
).

Remark 3.1
.

By virtue of
[
SAG
, Remark 6.2.1.8]
, more informally,
\underline{\mathcal{C}}(X)
can be described as the limit
\varprojlim_{A,\eta\in X(A)}\mathcal{C}_{A}
, where
\mathcal{C}_{A}
is the fiber of
q
over
A\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
. In other words, we can think of an object of
C\in\underline{\mathcal{C}}(X)
as a functor which assigns each
A\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
and
A
-point
\eta\in X(A)
an object
C_{\eta}\in\mathcal{C}
. The assignment
C_{\eta}
depend functorially on
A
in the following strong sense: if
\phi\colon A\to B
is a map of simplicial commutative rings, and
\eta^{\prime}\in X(B)
is the image of
\eta
under
\phi
, then we obtain a q-coCartesian morphism
C_{\eta}\to C_{\eta^{\prime}}
in
\mathcal{C}
.

Remark 3.2
.

The above construction of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}(X)
has the following properties:

(1)

A map
f\colon X\to Y
of prestacks (i.e., a natural transformation from
X
to
Y
) by construction determines a pullback functor
f^{*}_{\mathcal{C}}\colon\underline{\mathcal{C}}(Y)\to\underline{\mathcal{C}}(X)
;

(2)

If
F\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
is a morphism of coCartesian fibrations over
\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
(that is,
p^{\prime}\colon\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\to\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
is another coCartesian fibration, and
F
is a functor that commutes with their respective projections to
\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
up to canonically equivalences and carries
p
-coCartesian edges to
q
-coCartesian edges), then
F
induces a natural transformation from
\Phi(q)
to
\Phi(q^{\prime})
. In particular,
F
determines a functor
F_{X}\colon\underline{\mathcal{C}}(X)\to\underline{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}(X)
for each prestack
X
, and the formation of
F_{X}
commutes with base change (that is, for any map of prestacks
f\colon X\to Y
, there is a canonical equivalence of functors
F_{Y}\circ f_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}\simeq f_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}^{*}\circ F_{X}
).

(3)

(
[
SAG
, Proposition 6.2.1.9]
) Let
\chi\colon\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}\to\widehat{\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}}_{\infty}
be the functor classified by the coCartesian fibration
q\colon\mathcal{C}\to\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
, then
\Phi(q)\colon\mathrm{Fun}(\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta},\mathcal{S})^{\mathrm{op}}\to\widehat{\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}}_{\infty}
is the right Kan extension of
\chi
along the Yoneda embedding
j\colon\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}\to\mathrm{Fun}(\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta},\mathcal{S})^{\mathrm{op}}
. (Notice by
[
HTT
, Lemma 5.1.5.5]
, this is equivalent to
\Phi(q)
commutes with small limits, and there is a canonical equivalence
\Phi(q)\circ j\simeq\chi
.) In particular, for any
A\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
, there is a canonical equivalence
\underline{\mathcal{C}}(\operatorname{Spec}A)\simeq\mathcal{C}_{A}
.

3.2.
Quasi-coherent sheaves on prestacks

This subsection reviews the construction and basic properties of the functor
\operatorname{QCoh}
. Many materials of this subsection parallel Lurie’s exposition in
[
SAG
, §6.2-6.4]
, which studies the functor
\operatorname{QCoh}
in the spectral setting.

Construction 3.3

(Quasi-coherent sheaves; compare with
[
SAG
, Deﬁnition 6.2.2.1]
)
.

Let
q\colon\operatorname{SCRMod}\to\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
denote the coCartesian fibration which classifies the functor
(A\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta})\mapsto(\operatorname{Mod}_{A}\in\widehat{\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}}_{\infty})
. Apply the construction §
3.1
to
q
, we obtain a functor

\operatorname{QCoh}\colon\mathrm{Fun}(\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta},\mathcal{S})^{\mathrm{op}}\to\widehat{\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}}_{\infty},

which carries each
X\in\mathrm{Fun}(\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta},\mathcal{S})
to the
\infty
-category

\operatorname{QCoh}(X)=\mathrm{Fun}_{/\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}}^{\rm CCart}(\int_{\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}}X,\operatorname{SCRMod}).

For a prestack
X\in\mathrm{Fun}(\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta},\mathcal{S})
, we will refer to
\operatorname{QCoh}(X)
as the
\infty
-category of quasi-coherent sheaves on
X
(or
\infty
-category of quasi-coherent
\mathscr{O}_{X}
-modules)
, and elements
\mathscr{F}\in\operatorname{QCoh}(X)
as
quasi-coherent sheaves on
X
(or
quasi-coherent
\mathscr{O}_{X}
-modules
).

Remark 3.4

(Compare with
[
SAG
, Remark 6.2.2.7]
)
.

By virtue of
[
SAG
, Remark 6.2.1.8]
(reviewed in Remark
3.1
), more informally, we can describe
\operatorname{QCoh}(X)
as the limit of stable
\infty
-categories
\varprojlim_{R\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta},\eta\in X(R)}\operatorname{Mod}_{R}
(hence
\operatorname{QCoh}(X)
is a stable
\infty
-category). In other words, we can think of an object
\mathscr{F}\in\operatorname{QCoh}(X)
as an assignment
(R\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta},\eta\in X(R))\mapsto(\mathscr{F}(\eta)\in\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
which depends functorially on
R
in the following strong sense: if
\phi\colon R\to R^{\prime}
is a map of simplicial commutative rings such that
\eta^{\prime}\in X(R^{\prime})
is the image of
\eta
under
\phi
, then we obtain a
q
-coCartesian morphism
(R,\mathscr{F}(\eta))\to(R^{\prime},\mathscr{F}(\eta^{\prime}))
in
\operatorname{SCRMod}
, which we will view as an equivalence of
R^{\prime}
-modules
R^{\prime}\otimes_{R}\mathscr{F}(\eta)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathscr{F}(\eta^{\prime})
.

Remark 3.5
.

By virtue of Remark
3.2
,
\operatorname{QCoh}
satisfies the following properties:

(1)

A map
f\colon X\to Y
of prestacks determines a pullback functor
f^{*}\colon\operatorname{QCoh}(Y)\to\operatorname{QCoh}(X)
. Moreover, (since
\operatorname{QCoh}
preserves all small limits)
\operatorname{QCoh}(X)
and
\operatorname{QCoh}(Y)
admit small colimits and
f^{*}
preserves small colimits.

(2)

\operatorname{QCoh}\colon\mathrm{Fun}(\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta},\mathcal{S})^{\mathrm{op}}\to\widehat{\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}}_{\infty}
is the right Kan extension of the functor
R\mapsto\operatorname{Mod}_{R}
along the Yoneda embedding
\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}\to\mathrm{Fun}(\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta},\mathcal{S})^{\mathrm{op}}
. In particular, for any
A\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
, there is a canonical equivalence
\operatorname{QCoh}(\operatorname{Spec}A)\simeq\operatorname{Mod}_{A}
.

Remark 3.6

(Symmetric monoidal structure; compare with
[
SAG
, §6.2.6]
)
.

Since the functor
(R\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta})\mapsto(\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\in\widehat{\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}}_{\infty})
factors as a composition

\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}\xrightarrow{U}\operatorname{CAlg}(\widehat{\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}}_{\infty})\xrightarrow{\theta}\widehat{\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}}_{\infty},

where
U
assigns each
R\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
to the symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
\operatorname{Mod}_{R^{\circ}}^{\otimes}
of
[
HA
, §4.5.3]
. Let
\operatorname{QCoh}^{\otimes}\colon\mathrm{Fun}(\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta},\mathcal{C})\to\operatorname{CAlg}(\widehat{\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}}_{\infty})
be a right Kan extension of
U
, then
\operatorname{QCoh}^{\otimes}
assigns to each functor
X\in\mathrm{Fun}(\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta},\mathcal{C})
a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
\operatorname{QCoh}(X)^{\otimes}
whose underlying
\infty
-category can be identified with
\operatorname{QCoh}(X)
. We will denote the tensor product on
\operatorname{QCoh}(X)
by
\mathscr{F}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}\mathscr{F}^{\prime}
(or simply by
\mathscr{F}\otimes\mathscr{F}^{\prime}
whenever there is no confusion). More informally, the tensor product on
\operatorname{QCoh}(X)
is given point-wise by
(\mathscr{F}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}\mathscr{F}^{\prime})(\eta)\simeq\mathscr{F}(\eta)\otimes_{R}\mathscr{F}^{\prime}(\eta)
. The
unit
of
\operatorname{QCoh}(X)^{\otimes}
is given by the
structure sheaf

\mathscr{O}_{X}
, which assigns to each point
\eta\in X(R)
the module
R
(regarded as a module over itself).

Theorem 3.7

(Lipman–Neeman, Lurie)
.

Let
f\colon X\to Y
be a morphism of prestacks which is quasi-compact, quasi-separated relative derived scheme (or algebraic space). Let
f^{*}\colon\operatorname{QCoh}(Y)\to\operatorname{QCoh}(X)
denote the pullback functor.

(1)

(1a)

The pullback functor
f^{*}
admits a right adjoint
f_{*}\colon\operatorname{QCoh}(X)\to\operatorname{QCoh}(Y)
, called pushforward functor, which preserves small colimits;

(1b)

For every pullback diagram of prestacks

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

the canonical base change transformation (the Beck–Chevalley transformation)
g^{*}f_{*}\to f^{\prime}_{*}g^{\prime*}
is an equivalence of functors from
\operatorname{QCoh}(X)
to
\operatorname{QCoh}(Y^{\prime})
.

(1c)

For every pair of objects
\mathscr{F}\in\operatorname{QCoh}(X)
and
\mathscr{G}\in\operatorname{QCoh}(Y)
, the canonical map
\mathscr{F}\otimes f_{*}\mathscr{G}\to f_{*}(f^{*}\mathscr{F}\otimes\mathscr{G})
is an equivalence.

(2)

If we furthermore assume that
f
is proper, locally almost of finite presentation and locally of finite Tor-amplitude. Then:

(2a)

The pushforward functor
f_{*}\colon\operatorname{QCoh}(X)\to\operatorname{QCoh}(Y)
preserves perfect objects, and admits a right adjoint
f^{!}\colon\operatorname{QCoh}(Y)\to\operatorname{QCoh}(X)
which preserves small colimits.

(2b)

For every pullback diagram of prestacks

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

the canonical base change transformation
g^{\prime*}f^{!}\to f^{\prime!}g^{\prime*}
is an equivalence of functors from
\operatorname{QCoh}(Y)
to
\operatorname{QCoh}(X^{\prime})
.

(2c)

For every pair of objects
\mathscr{F},\mathscr{G}\in\operatorname{QCoh}(Y)
, the canonical map
f^{!}\mathscr{F}\otimes f^{*}\mathscr{G}\to f^{!}(\mathscr{F}\otimes\mathscr{G})
is an equivalence.

(2d)

The pullback functor
f^{*}
admits a left adjoint
f_{!}\colon\operatorname{QCoh}(X)\to\operatorname{QCoh}(Y)
which preserves perfect objects and whose restriction to
\mathrm{Perf}(X)
is given by the formula
f_{!}(\mathscr{F})=(f_{*}\mathscr{F}^{\vee})^{\vee}
.

(2e)

For every pullback diagram of prestacks

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

the canonical base change transformation
f^{\prime}_{!}g^{\prime*}\to g^{*}f_{!}
is an equivalence of functors from
\operatorname{QCoh}(X)
to
\operatorname{QCoh}(Y^{\prime})
.

(2f)

For every pair of objects
\mathscr{F}\in\operatorname{QCoh}(X)
and
\mathscr{G}\in\operatorname{QCoh}(Y)
, the canonical map
f_{!}(f^{*}\mathscr{F}\otimes\mathscr{G})\to\mathscr{F}\otimes f_{!}\mathscr{G}
is an equivalence.

(2g)

Let
\omega_{f}=\omega_{X/Y}
denote the relative dualizing sheaf, that is, the object
f^{!}\mathscr{O}_{Y}\in\operatorname{QCoh}(X)
. Then there are canonical natural isomorphisms of functors

f^{!}(\underline{\hphantom{A}})\simeq f^{*}(\underline{\hphantom{A}})\otimes\omega_{f}\quad\text{and}\quad f_{!}(\underline{\hphantom{A}})\simeq f_{*}(\underline{\hphantom{A}}\otimes\omega_{f}).

(3)

In the situation of (
2
), if we furthermore assume that the relative dualizing sheaf
\omega_{f}
is perfect, then each member of the adjunction sequence
f_{!}\dashv f^{*}\dashv f_{*}\dashv f^{!}
preserves perfect objects, almost perfect objects, and locally bounded almost perfect objects (in particular, in the case where
X
and
Y
are locally Noetherian, each of the above functors preserves locally bounded coherent sheaves).

Proof.

In the situation of classical algebraic geometry where
X
,
Y
are quasi-compact, quasi-separated schemes (and the involved base change are Tor-independent), the assertions are consequences of the results of
[
LN
,
Nee96
,
Nee10
,
Lip
]
; see also our summary in
[
J21
, Theorem 3.1]
. In the context of spectral algebraic geometry, assertion (
1
) is a consequence of
[
SAG
, Proposition 6.3.4.1]
and
[
SAG
, Corollary 6.3.4.3]
, assertions (
22a
) through (
22g
) follows from a combination of
[
SAG
, Proposition 6.4.2.1, Lemma 6.4.2.2, Proposition 6.4.5.3, and Proposition 6.4.5.4]
, and assertion (
3
) is a direct consequence of (
2
). The same proof strategy of the above results work in our setting with minimal modifications.
∎

3.3.
Quasi-coherent algebras on prestacks and relative spectra

In this subsection, we apply the construction of
[
SAG
, §6.2.1, Construction 6.2.1.7]
(which we have reviewed in §
3.1
) to study the
\infty
-categories of quasi-coherent algebras on prestacks.

Construction 3.8

(Quasi-coherent algebras)
.

Let
\mathrm{ev}_{\{0\}}\colon\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta})\to\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
denote the evaluation map
(A\to B)\mapsto A
. Then
\mathrm{ev}_{\{0\}}
is the coCartesian fibration which classifies the functor
(R\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta})\mapsto(\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}_{R}\in\widehat{\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}}_{\infty})
(where for a map of simplicial commutative rings
R\to R^{\prime}
, the induced functor
\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}_{R}\to\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}_{R^{\prime}}
is the base change map
A\mapsto A\otimes_{R}R^{\prime}
of
[
SAG
, Notation 25.1.4.4]
; In other words, an edge
(R,A)\to(R^{\prime},A^{\prime})
is a
\mathrm{ev}_{\{0\}}
-coCartesian edge if and only if the induced map
A\otimes_{R}R^{\prime}\to A^{\prime}
is an equivalence of
R^{\prime}
-algebras). Apply the construction §
3.1
to the coCartesian fibration
\mathrm{ev}_{\{0\}}
, we obtain a functor

\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}\colon\mathrm{Fun}(\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta},\mathcal{S})^{\mathrm{op}}\to\widehat{\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}}_{\infty},

which carries each
X\in\mathrm{Fun}(\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta},\mathcal{S})
to an
\infty
-category

\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}(X)=\mathrm{Fun}_{/\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}}^{\rm CCart}\big(\int_{\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}}X,\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta})\big).

For a prestack
X\in\mathrm{Fun}(\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta},\mathcal{S})
, we will refer to
\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}(X)
as the
\infty
-category of quasi-coherent algebras over
X
(or
\infty
-category of quasi-coherent
\mathscr{O}_{X}
-algebras
), and elements
\mathscr{F}\in\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}(X)
as
quasi-coherent algebras over
X
(or
quasi-coherent
\mathscr{O}_{X}
-algebras
).

Remark 3.9
.

By virtue of Remark
3.1
, more informally, we can describe
\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}(X)
as the limit
\varprojlim_{R\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta},\eta\in X(R)}\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}_{R}
. In other words, we can think of an object
\mathscr{A}\in\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}(X)
as an assignment
(R\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta},\eta\in X(R))\mapsto(\mathscr{A}(\eta)\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}_{R})
which depends functorially on
R
in the following strong sense: if
\phi\colon R\to R^{\prime}
is a map of simplicial commutative rings such that
\eta^{\prime}\in X(R^{\prime})
is the image of
\eta
under
\phi
, then we obtain a
\mathrm{ev}_{\{0\}}
-coCartesian morphism
(R,\mathscr{A}(\eta))\to(R^{\prime},\mathscr{A}(\eta^{\prime}))
in
\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta})
, which we will view as an equivalence of simplicial
R^{\prime}
-algebras
R^{\prime}\otimes_{R}\mathscr{A}(\eta)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathscr{A}(\eta^{\prime})
.

Remark 3.10
.

By virtue of Remark
3.2
,
\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}
satisfies the following properties:

(1)

A map
f\colon X\to Y
of prestacks determines a pullback functor
f^{*}_{\rm alg}\colon\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}(Y)\to\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}(X)
. Moreover, since
\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}
preserves all small limits, we obtain that
\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}(X)
and
\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}(Y)
admit small colimits and
f^{*}_{\rm alg}
preserves small colimits.

(2)

\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}\colon\mathrm{Fun}(\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta},\mathcal{S})^{\mathrm{op}}\to\widehat{\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}}_{\infty}
is the right Kan extension of the functor
R\mapsto\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}_{R}
along the Yoneda embedding
\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}\to\mathrm{Fun}(\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta},\mathcal{S})^{\mathrm{op}}
. In particular, for any
A\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
, there is a canonical equivalence
\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}(\operatorname{Spec}A)\simeq\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}_{A}
.

Proposition 3.11

(Compare with
[
SAG
, Proposition 6.2.3.1]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\rm fpqc}
the full subcategory of
\mathrm{Fun}(\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta},\mathcal{S})
spanned by those functors which are sheaves with respect to the fpqc topology, and let
L\colon\mathrm{Fun}(\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta},\mathcal{S})\to\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\rm fpqc}
denote a left adjoint to the inclusion functor.

(1)

The canonical maps
\operatorname{QCoh}\circ L\to\operatorname{QCoh}
and
\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}\circ L\to\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}
are equivalences of functors from
\mathrm{Fun}(\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta},\mathcal{S})^{\mathrm{op}}
to
\widehat{\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}}_{\infty}
.

(2)

The restrictions
\operatorname{QCoh}|_{\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\rm fpqc}^{\mathrm{op}}}\colon\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\rm fpqc}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\widehat{\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}}_{\infty}
, and
\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}|_{\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\rm fpqc}^{\mathrm{op}}}\colon\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\rm fpqc}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\widehat{\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}}_{\infty}
preserve small limits, respectively.

Proof.

The proof for the functor
\operatorname{QCoh}
is completely analogous to the spectral case
[
SAG
, Proposition 6.2.3.1]
; we only need to prove the assertions for
\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}
. By our construction,
\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}
preserves limits (see
[
SAG
, Lemma 6.2.1.12]
). By virtue of
[
SAG
, Proposition 1.3.1.7]
, to prove the assertion (
1
), we only need to show that the functor
(R\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta})\mapsto(\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}_{R}\in\widehat{\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}}_{\infty})
satisfies flat descent: in other words, if
R\to R_{\bullet}
is the Čech nerve of a faithfully flat map
R\to R_{0}
of simplicial commutative rings, then the canonical map

\theta\colon\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}_{R}\to\varprojlim_{[n]\in\operatorname{{\bf\Delta}}_{s}}\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}_{R_{n}}

is an equivalence of
\infty
-categories, where
\operatorname{{\bf\Delta}}_{s}
is the subcategory of
\Delta
whose morphisms are injective maps
[m]\to[n]
of ﬁnite linearly ordered sets. Invoking
[
HA
, Proposition 5.2.2.36]
, it will suﬃce to verify the following assertions:

(i)

The functor
\theta
is conservative. That is, if
\alpha\colon A\to B
is a morphism in
\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}_{R}
such that each of the induced maps
\alpha_{n}\colon R_{n}\otimes_{R}A\to R_{n}\otimes_{R}B
is an equivalence, then
\alpha
is an equivalence. This follows from the fully faithfulness of
R\to R_{0}
.

(ii)

Suppose we are given objects
A_{n}\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}_{R_{n}}
such that for each injective map
[m]\to[n]
in
\operatorname{{\bf\Delta}}
, the (flat) map
R_{m}\to R_{n}
induces an equivalence
A_{n}\simeq R_{n}\otimes_{R_{m}}A_{m}
, then there exists a
R
-algebra
A=\varprojlim A_{\bullet}
such that each of the natural maps
R_{n}\otimes_{R}A\to A_{n}
is an equivalence. Since each map
A_{m}\to A_{n}
is flat, the existence of
A=\varprojlim A_{\bullet}
follows from fact that the identify functor
\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}\to\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
is a fpqc sheaf (see
[
SAG
, Theorem D.6.3.5]
for the corresponding result in spectral setting; The analogous statement implies that the fpqc topology on the
\infty
-category
\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta,\mathrm{op}}
is subcanonical).

The assertion (
2
) is a consequence of assertion (
1
).
∎

Proposition 3.12

(Affine morphisms)
.

Let
f\colon Y\to X
be an affine morphism in
\mathrm{Fun}(\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta},\mathcal{S})
.

(1)

The pullback functor
f^{*}_{\rm alg}\colon\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}(X)\to\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}(Y)
admits a right adjoint

f_{*}^{\rm alg}\colon\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}(Y)\to\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}(X).

Moreover, for each map
\operatorname{Spec}R\to X
, let
Y_{R}=Y\times_{X}\operatorname{Spec}R
denote the fiber product, then the induced pullback diagram of
\infty
-categories

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}(Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}(\operatorname{Spec}R)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}(Y_{R})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}_{\rm alg}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f_{R})_{\mathrm{alg}}^{*}}

is right adjointable.

(2)

(Compare with
[
SAG
, Proposition 6.3.4.5]
) We let
\mathrm{Aff}_{/X}
denote the full subcategory of
\mathrm{Fun}(\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta},\mathcal{S})_{/X}
spanned by affine morphisms
f\colon Y\to X
. Then the construction

((f\colon Y\to X)\in\mathrm{Aff}_{/X})\mapsto(\mathscr{A}(Y/X)=f_{*}^{\rm alg}(\mathscr{O}_{Y})\in\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}(X))

induces an equivalence of
\infty
-categories
\mathrm{Aff}_{/X}\simeq\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}(X)^{\mathrm{op}}
.

(3)

Let
f\colon Y\to X
,
g\colon Z\to X
be morphisms in
\mathrm{Fun}(\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta},\mathcal{S})
. Assume
f
is affine and set
\mathscr{A}=f_{*}^{\rm alg}(\mathscr{O}_{Y})\in\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}(X)
. Then the canonical map

\theta\colon\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{Fun}(\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta},\,\mathcal{S})_{/X}}(Z,\operatorname{Spec}\mathscr{A})\to\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}(X)}(g^{*}_{\rm alg}(\mathscr{A}),\mathscr{O}_{Z})

is a homotopy equivalence.

Proof.

To prove the assertion (
1
), we use a similar strategy as the proof of Proposition
3.19
. Write
X
as a colimit of a diagram
q\colon K\to\mathrm{Fun}(\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta},\mathcal{S})
where
q(s)\simeq\operatorname{Spec}A_{s}
is affine. Then by our assumption
Y_{s}=\operatorname{Spec}A_{s}
, where
A_{s}
is an
R_{s}
-algebra. For each edge
e\colon s\to s^{\prime}
in
K
, since
A_{s}\simeq A_{s^{\prime}}\otimes_{R_{s^{\prime}}}R_{s}
, the associated pullback diagram of
\infty
-categories

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}(\operatorname{Spec}R_{s^{\prime}})\simeq\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}_{R_{s^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}(\operatorname{Spec}A_{s^{\prime}})\simeq\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}_{A_{s^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}(\operatorname{Spec}R_{s})\simeq\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}_{R_{s}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}(\operatorname{Spec}A_{s})^{\mathrm{cn}}\simeq\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}_{A_{s}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q(e)^{*}_{\rm alg}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q(e)^{*}_{\rm alg}}

is evidently right adjointable. Then
[
HA
, Corollary 4.7.4.18]
implies the assertion (
1
).

For assertion (
2
), writing X as a colimit of representable functors again, we can reduce to the case where
X=\operatorname{Spec}R
. Each affine morphism
Y\to X
has the form
Y=\operatorname{Spec}A
, where
R\to A
is a morphism of simplicial commutative rings, and
f_{*}^{\rm alg}(A)=A\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}_{R}
by virtue of (
1
). Then
\lambda
is the evident homotopy inverse of the functor
\operatorname{Spec}\colon\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}_{R}\to{\rm Aff}_{/\operatorname{Spec}R}^{\mathrm{op}}
.

To prove the assertion (
3
), we use the same strategy as the proof of
[
SAG
, Lemma 9.2.1.2]
. When regarded as functors of
Z
, both the domain and codomain of
\theta
carry colimits of functors to limits of spaces. Writing
Z
as a colimit of corepresentable functors, we may reduce to the case
Z=\operatorname{Spec}R
. Replacing
X
by
Z
and
Y
by
Y\times_{X}Z
, we may further reduce to the affine case where
Y=\operatorname{Spec}A
,
X=Z=\operatorname{Spec}R
, and
R\to A
is a map of simplicial commutative rings. In this case, the desired result follows from assertion (
2
).
∎

Definition 3.13

(Relative Spectrum; compare with
[
SAG
, Construction 2.5.1.3]
)
.

Let
X\in\mathrm{Fun}(\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta},\mathcal{S})
, and let
\lambda
denote the homotopy equivalence of Proposition
3.12
(
2
). We let

\operatorname{Spec}_{X}\colon\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}(X)\to\mathrm{Aff}_{/X}

denote a fixed a homotopy inverse to the equivalence
\lambda
. Given a quasi-coherent algebra
\mathscr{A}
over
X
, we let
\mathrm{pr}\colon\operatorname{Spec}_{X}\mathscr{A}\to X
denote the corresponding affine morphism, and we will refer
\operatorname{Spec}_{X}\mathscr{A}
as the
relative spectrum of
\mathscr{A}
over
X
.

Let
\mathrm{pr}\colon\operatorname{Spec}_{X}\mathscr{A}\to X
denote the natural projection, then by construction:

(1)

There is a canonical equivalence
\mathscr{A}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{pr}_{*}^{\rm alg}(\mathscr{O}_{\operatorname{Spec}_{X}\mathscr{A}})\in\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}(X)
.

(2)

For any base change
\phi\colon X^{\prime}\to X
, there is a canonical identification

\operatorname{Spec}_{X^{\prime}}(\phi^{*}\mathscr{A})=(\operatorname{Spec}_{X}\mathscr{A})\times_{X}X^{\prime}.

Remark 3.14

(Non-affine pushforwards of quasi-coherent algebras)
.

In general, the pullback functor
f^{*}_{\rm alg}
admits a right adjoint
f_{*}^{\rm alg}\colon\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}(Y)\to\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}(X)
only under mild assumptions. For example, if
X=\operatorname{Spec}R
, then
f_{*}^{\rm alg}\colon\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}(Y)\to\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}_{A}
is the
simplicial global section functor
: regarding each object
\mathscr{A}\in\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}(Y)
as a functorial assignment
\eta\colon\operatorname{Spec}A\to X
,
\mathscr{A}(\eta)\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}_{A}
, then
f_{*}^{\rm alg}(\mathscr{A})=\varprojlim_{\eta}\mathscr{A}(\eta)\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}_{R}
. Beware that if
f
is not affine, then generally the underlying sheaf of
f_{*}^{\rm alg}(\mathscr{A})
is
not
equivalent to the pushforward
f_{*}(\mathscr{A})
of the underlying sheaves, even in the case
\mathscr{A}=\mathscr{O}_{Y}
(for example,
f_{*}^{\rm alg}(\mathscr{A})
is always connective, but
f_{*}(\mathscr{A})
might not). It is worth studying the functor
f^{*}_{\rm alg}
for general non-affine morphisms, but we will not pursue this direction further in this paper.

3.4.
Derived symmetric powers and universal fiber sequences on prestacks

In this subsection, we apply the construction of §
3.1
to globalize the constructions and results of §
2.5
and obtain universal fiber sequences associated to symmetric powers on prestacks.

Construction 3.15
.

Let
n\geq 0
be an integer, and let
\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{n}(M)
,
\bigwedge_{A}^{n}(M)
and
\Gamma_{A}^{n}(M)
be the derived symmetric powers, derived exterior power and derived divided powers, respectively, constructed in Construction
2.10
. By virtue of
[
SAG
, Proposition 25.2.3.1]
, each of three functors
\operatorname{Sym}^{n},\bigwedge^{n},\Gamma^{n}\colon\operatorname{SCRMod}^{\rm cn}\to\operatorname{SCRMod}^{\rm cn}
sends
q
-coCartesian edges to
q
-coCartesian morphisms. Therefore we could apply Remark
3.2
(
2
) to these functors and obtain natural transformations of functors

\operatorname{Sym}^{n},\bigwedge\nolimits^{n},\Gamma^{n}\colon\operatorname{QCoh}^{\mathrm{cn}}\to\operatorname{QCoh}^{\mathrm{cn}}

from
\operatorname{QCoh}^{\mathrm{cn}}\colon\mathrm{Fun}(\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta},\mathcal{S})^{\mathrm{op}}\to\widehat{\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}}_{\infty}
to itself. Consequently, for any given prestack
X\in\mathrm{Fun}(\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta},\mathcal{S})
and a connective quasi-coherent sheaf
\mathscr{F}
on
X
, we obtain connective connective quasi-coherent sheaves
\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{n}(\mathscr{F})\in\operatorname{QCoh}(X)^{\mathrm{cn}}
,
\bigwedge\nolimits_{X}^{n}(\mathscr{F})\in\operatorname{QCoh}(X)^{\mathrm{cn}}
and
\Gamma_{X}^{n}(\mathscr{F})\in\operatorname{QCoh}(X)^{\mathrm{cn}}
, respectively. We will refer to the connective quasi-coherent sheaves
\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{n}(\mathscr{F})
,
\bigwedge\nolimits_{X}^{n}(\mathscr{F})
, and
\Gamma_{X}^{n}(\mathscr{F})
as the
derived
n
th symmetric powers
,
derived
n
th exterior powers
, and
derived
n
th divided powers
of
\mathscr{F}
over
X
, respectively.

Remark 3.16
.

(1)

The formations of
\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{n}(\mathscr{F})
,
\bigwedge\nolimits_{X}^{n}(\mathscr{F})
and
\Gamma_{X}^{n}(\mathscr{F})
commute with base change of prestacks. In other words, for any map of prestacks
f\colon Y\to X
and
\mathscr{F}\in\operatorname{QCoh}(X)^{\mathrm{cn}}
, there are canonical equivalences of quasi-coherent
\mathscr{O}_{Y}
-modules
f^{*}\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{n}(\mathscr{F})\simeq\operatorname{Sym}_{Y}^{n}(f^{*}\mathscr{F})
,
f^{*}\bigwedge_{X}^{n}(\mathscr{F})\simeq\bigwedge_{Y}^{n}(f^{*}\mathscr{F})
, and
f^{*}\Gamma_{X}^{n}(\mathscr{F})\simeq\Gamma_{Y}^{n}(f^{*}\mathscr{F})
.

(2)

Remark
3.2
(
3
) implies that in the representable case
X=\operatorname{Spec}A
, there are canonical equivalences of functors
\operatorname{Sym}_{\operatorname{Spec}A}^{n}\simeq\operatorname{Sym}^{n}_{A}
,
\bigwedge_{\operatorname{Spec}A}^{n}\simeq\bigwedge^{n}_{A}
and
\Gamma^{n}_{\operatorname{Spec}A}\simeq\Gamma^{n}_{A}
.

Construction 3.17
.

Let
\mathcal{E}
and
\mathcal{D}
be the
\infty
-categories defined in §
2.5
. In particular, objects of
\mathcal{E}
are pairs
(A,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M)
where
A\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
and
\rho
is morphism of connective
A
-modules, and objects of
\mathcal{D}
are pairs
(A,\alpha)
where
A\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
and
\alpha
is a fiber sequence of connective
A
-modules. We apply the construction of §
3.1
to the coCartesian fibrations
q_{\mathcal{E}}\colon\mathcal{E}\to\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
and
q_{\mathcal{D}}\colon\mathcal{D}\to\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
, then we obtain functors

\Phi(q_{\mathcal{E}}),\Phi(q_{\mathcal{D}})\colon\mathrm{Fun}(\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta},\mathcal{S})^{\mathrm{op}}\to\widehat{\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}}_{\infty}

which carry a prestack
X\in\mathrm{Fun}(\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta},\mathcal{S})
to the respective
\infty
-categories

\underline{\mathcal{E}}(X)=\mathrm{Fun}_{/\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}}^{\rm CCart}\big(\int_{\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}}X,\mathcal{E}\big),\qquad\underline{\mathcal{D}}(X)=\mathrm{Fun}_{/\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}}^{\rm CCart}\big(\int_{\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}}X,\mathcal{D}\big).

Unwinding the definition, we obtain that the objects of
\underline{\mathcal{E}}(X)
are morphisms of connective quasi-coherent sheaves
\rho\colon\mathscr{M}^{\prime}\to\mathscr{M}
on
X
, and objects of
\underline{\mathcal{D}}(X)
are fiber sequences
\alpha\colon\mathscr{M}^{\prime}\to\mathscr{M}\to\mathscr{M}^{\prime\prime}
of connective quasi-coherent sheaves on
X
.

Construction 3.18
.

Let
n\geq 1
be a fixed integer. By virtue of Remarks
2.25
and
2.27
, we can apply Remark
3.2
(
2
) to the functors
(A,\rho)\in\mathcal{E}\mapsto(A,d_{i+1,n}(A,\rho))\in\mathcal{E}
,
(A,\rho)\in\mathcal{E}\mapsto(A,\alpha_{i,n}(A,\rho))\in\mathcal{D}
and
(A,\rho)\in\mathcal{E}\mapsto(A,\beta_{i,n}(A,\rho))\in\mathcal{D}
(where
0\leq i\leq n-1
) of Constructions
2.23
and
2.26
, and obtain natural transformations

d_{i+1,n}\colon\Phi(q_{\mathcal{E}})\to\Phi(q_{\mathcal{E}}),\qquad\alpha_{i},\beta_{i}\colon\Phi(q_{\mathcal{E}})\to\Phi(q_{\mathcal{D}}).

In other words, for any prestack
X\in\mathrm{Fun}(\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta},\mathcal{S})
, we have functors

\displaystyle\underline{\mathcal{E}}(X)\to\underline{\mathcal{E}}(X),

\displaystyle\quad(\rho\colon\mathscr{M}^{\prime}\to\mathscr{M})\mapsto d_{i+1,n}(X,\rho);

\displaystyle\underline{\mathcal{E}}(X)\to\underline{\mathcal{D}}(X),

\displaystyle\quad(\rho\colon\mathscr{M}^{\prime}\to\mathscr{M})\mapsto\alpha_{i,n}(X,\rho);

\displaystyle\underline{\mathcal{E}}(X)\to\underline{\mathcal{D}}(X),

\displaystyle\quad(\rho\colon\mathscr{M}^{\prime}\to\mathscr{M})\mapsto\beta_{i,n}(X,\rho).

Here,
d_{i+1,n}(X,\rho)
is a morphism of connective quasi-coherent sheaves on
X
:

d_{i+1,n}(X,\rho)\colon\quad(\bigwedge\nolimits^{i+1}_{X}\mathscr{M}^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}(\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{n-i-1}\mathscr{M})\to(\bigwedge\nolimits^{i}_{X}\mathscr{M}^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}(\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{n-i}\mathscr{M})

such that there are canonical equivalences
d_{i+1,n}(X,\rho)\circ d_{i,n}(X,\rho)\simeq 0
;
\alpha_{i,n}(X,\rho)
and
\beta_{i,n}(X,\rho)
are fiber sequences of connective quasi-coherent sheaves on
X
:

\displaystyle\alpha_{i,n}(X,\rho)\colon

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}_{i+1,n}(X,\rho)\to(\bigwedge\nolimits^{i}_{X}\mathscr{M}^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}(\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{n-i}\mathscr{M})\to\mathscr{F}_{i,n}(X,\rho),

\displaystyle\beta_{i,n}(X,\rho)\colon

\displaystyle\Sigma^{i}(\bigwedge\nolimits_{X}^{i+1}\mathscr{M}^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}(\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{n-i-1}\mathscr{M})\to\mathscr{G}_{i,n}(X,\rho)\to\mathscr{G}_{i+1,n}(X,\rho)

such that the composition of second edge of
\alpha_{i+1,n}(X,\rho)
and the first edge of
\alpha_{i,n}(X,\rho)
:

(\bigwedge\nolimits^{i+1}_{X}\mathscr{M}^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}(\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{n-i+1}\mathscr{M})\to\mathscr{F}_{i+1,n}(X,\rho)\to(\bigwedge\nolimits^{i}_{X}\mathscr{M}^{\prime})

is canonically equivalent to
d_{i+1,n}(X,\rho)
, and the composition of first edge of
\beta_{i,n}(X,\rho)
and the connecting edge of
\beta_{i-1,n}(X,\rho)
:

\Sigma^{i}(\bigwedge\nolimits_{X}^{i+1}\mathscr{M}^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}(\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{n-i-1}\mathscr{M})\to\mathscr{G}_{i,n}(X,\rho)\to\Sigma^{i}(\bigwedge\nolimits_{X}^{i}\mathscr{M}^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}(\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{n-i}\mathscr{M})

is canonically equivalent to
\Sigma^{i}\circ d_{i+1,n}(X,\rho)
. By construction, the formations of
d_{i,n}(X,\rho)
,
\alpha_{i,n}(X,\rho)
, and
\beta_{i,n}(X,\rho)
commute with sifted colimits of
\rho
and arbitary base change of prestacks
X^{\prime}\to X
. Furthermore, there are canonical equivalences:

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}_{0,n}(X,\rho\colon\mathscr{M}^{\prime}\to\mathscr{M})\simeq\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{n}(\operatorname{cofib}(\rho)),\qquad\mathscr{F}_{n,n}(X,\rho\colon\mathscr{M}^{\prime}\to\mathscr{M})\simeq\bigwedge\nolimits_{X}^{n}\mathscr{M}^{\prime};

\displaystyle\mathscr{G}_{0,n}(X,\rho\colon\mathscr{M}^{\prime}\to\mathscr{M})\simeq\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{n}\mathscr{M},\qquad\mathscr{G}_{n,n}(X,\rho\colon\mathscr{M}^{\prime}\to\mathscr{M})\simeq\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{n}(\operatorname{cofib}(\rho)).

Moreover, for any integer
n^{\prime}\geq 0
, there are canonical morphisms of fiber sequences

\alpha_{i,n}(X,\rho)\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{n^{\prime}}\mathscr{M}\to\alpha_{i,n+n^{\prime}}(X,\rho),\qquad\beta_{i,n}(X,\rho)\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{n^{\prime}}\mathscr{M}\to\beta_{i,n+n^{\prime}}(X,\rho)

for which the induced maps on the middle and initial vertices, respectively, coincide with the maps induced by the multiplication maps
\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{n-i}\mathscr{M}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{n^{\prime}}\mathscr{M}\to\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{n+n^{\prime}-i}\mathscr{M}
and
\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{n-i-1}\mathscr{M}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{n^{\prime}}\mathscr{M}\to\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{n+n^{\prime}-i-1}\mathscr{M}
of Remark
3.21
, respectively.

3.5.
Derived symmetric algebras over prestacks

Proposition 3.19

(Derived Symmetric Algebras)
.

Let
X\in\mathrm{Fun}(\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta},\mathcal{S})
be a prestack.

(1)

The forgetful functor
\mathrm{forg}\colon\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}(X)\to\operatorname{QCoh}(X)^{\mathrm{cn}}
admits a left adjoint functor
\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{*}\colon\operatorname{QCoh}(X)^{\mathrm{cn}}\to\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}(X)
. In other words, for all
\mathscr{F},\mathscr{F}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{QCoh}(X)^{\mathrm{cn}}
, the composition with the natural morphism quasi-coherent sheaves
\eta_{\mathscr{F}}\colon\mathscr{F}\to\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{*}(\mathscr{F})
induces a homotopy equivalence

\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}(X)}(\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{*}(\mathscr{F}),\mathscr{F}^{\prime})\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{QCoh}(X)^{\mathrm{cn}}}(\mathscr{F},\mathscr{F}^{\prime}).

(2)

For every map of prestacks
f\colon Y\to X
in
\mathrm{Fun}(\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta},\mathcal{S})
, the associated diagram of
\infty
-categories

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{QCoh}(X)^{\mathrm{cn}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}(Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{QCoh}(Y)^{\mathrm{cn}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{forg}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}_{\rm alg}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{forg}}

is left adjointable. In other words, the Beck–Chevalley transformation
