\lambda_{f}\colon\operatorname{Sym}_{Y}^{*}\circ f^{*}_{\rm alg}\to f^{*}\circ\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{*}
is an equivalence of functors from
\operatorname{QCoh}(X)^{\mathrm{cn}}
to
\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}(Y)
.

Proof.

We use the same strategy as Lurie’s proof of
[
SAG
, Proposition 6.3.4.1]
. Write
X
as a colimit of a diagram
q\colon K\to\mathrm{Fun}(\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta},\mathcal{S})
where
q(s)\simeq\operatorname{Spec}A_{s}
is affine,
s\in K
,
K
is a simplicial set. Then for each edge
e\colon s\to s^{\prime}
in
K
, the associated diagram of
\infty
-categories

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}(\operatorname{Spec}A_{s^{\prime}})\simeq\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}_{A_{s^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{QCoh}(\operatorname{Spec}A_{s^{\prime}})^{\mathrm{cn}}\simeq\operatorname{Mod}_{A_{s^{\prime}}}^{\mathrm{cn}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}(\operatorname{Spec}A_{s})\simeq\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}_{A_{s}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{QCoh}(\operatorname{Spec}A_{s})^{\mathrm{cn}}\simeq\operatorname{Mod}_{A_{s}}^{\mathrm{cn}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{forg}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q(e)^{*}_{\rm alg}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q(e)^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{forg}}

is left adjointable: this follows from the fact that formation of
\operatorname{Sym}^{*}_{R}
commutes with base change. Since
\operatorname{QCoh}(X)=\varprojlim\operatorname{QCoh}(\operatorname{Spec}A_{s})
and
\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}(X)=\varprojlim\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}(\operatorname{Spec}A_{s})
, by virtue of
[
HA
, Corollary 4.7.4.18]
, we obtain that
\mathrm{forg}\colon\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}(X)\to\operatorname{QCoh}(X)^{\mathrm{cn}}
is left adjointable, and the diagram of (
2
) is left adjointable whenever
Y
is affine. To prove the assertion (
2
) for a general morphism of prestacks
f\colon Y\to X
, let
\mathscr{F}\in\operatorname{QCoh}(X)^{\mathrm{cn}}
, and we wish to show that the natural map
\lambda_{f}(\mathscr{F})\colon\operatorname{Sym}_{Y}^{*}(f^{*}\mathscr{F})\to f^{*}\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{*}(\mathscr{F})
is an equivalence. It suffices to show that for every map
h\colon\operatorname{Spec}A\to Y
,
h^{*}\lambda_{f}(\mathscr{F})
is an equivalence. The composition
\operatorname{Spec}A\xrightarrow{h}Y\xrightarrow{f}X
induces a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h^{*}\,\operatorname{Sym}_{Y}^{*}(f^{*}\mathscr{F})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sym}_{\operatorname{Spec}A}^{*}(h^{*}\,f^{*}\mathscr{F})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h^{*}\,f^{*}\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{*}(\mathscr{F})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h^{*}\lambda_{f}(\mathscr{F})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{h}(f^{*}\mathscr{F})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{f\circ h}(\mathscr{F})}

where
\lambda_{f\circ h}(\mathscr{F})
and
\lambda_{h}(f^{*}\mathscr{F})
are equivalences since we have proved the assertion (
2
) in the case where the source
\operatorname{Spec}A
is affine. Therefore
h^{*}\lambda_{f}(\mathscr{F})
is an equivalence.
∎

Remark 3.20
.

Combining Proposition
3.19
(
2
) and Proposition
2.16
(
4
), we obtain that the left adjoint functor
\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{*}
of Proposition
3.19
agrees with the functor obtained by “globalizing” the symmetric algebra
\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}(M)
of Definition
2.12
, that is, the functor obtained by applying Remark
3.2
(
2
) to the functor
(A,M)\mapsto(A,\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}M)\colon\operatorname{SCRMod}^{\rm cn}\to\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta})
. In particular, if
X=\operatorname{Spec}R
, and
\mathscr{F}\in\operatorname{QCoh}(X)
is the sheaf associated to
M\in\operatorname{Mod}_{R}
under the equivalence
\operatorname{QCoh}(X)\simeq\operatorname{Mod}_{R}
, then
\operatorname{Sym}_{\operatorname{Spec}R}^{*}(\mathscr{F})
is canonically equivalent to the quasi-coherent
\mathscr{O}_{X}
-algebra associated to the derived symmetric algebra
\operatorname{Sym}_{R}^{*}(M)
under the equivalence
\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}(X)\simeq\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}_{R}
.
Moreover, for any prestack
X
and any
\mathscr{F}\in\operatorname{QCoh}(X)^{\mathrm{cn}}
, there are canonical equivalences of quasi-coherent sheaves

\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{*}(\mathscr{F})\simeq\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{n}(\mathscr{F}),

where
\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{n}(\mathscr{F})
is the derived
n
th symmetric power of
\mathscr{F}
over
X
(Construction
3.15
).

Remark 3.21

(The underlying commutative algebra objects of derived symmetric algebras)
.

Combining
Remarks
3.2
and
2.13
, we obtain for any prestack
X
a forgetful functor

\Theta_{X}\colon\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}(X)\to\operatorname{CAlg}(\operatorname{QCoh}(X)^{\mathrm{cn}}).

For a quasi-coherent algebra
\mathscr{A}\in\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}(X)
, we will refer to the image
\Theta_{X}(\mathscr{A})
of
\mathscr{A}
in
\operatorname{CAlg}(\operatorname{QCoh}(X)^{\mathrm{cn}})
as the
underlying commutative algebra object in
\operatorname{QCoh}(X)^{\mathrm{cn}}
of
\mathscr{A}
.

For any connective quasi-coherent sheaf
\mathscr{F}\in\operatorname{QCoh}(X)^{\mathrm{cn}}
, we will generally abuse notations by using
\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{*}(\mathscr{F})
to denote the derived symmetric algebra of
\mathscr{F}
over
X
(as a quasi-coherent algebra), its underlying commutative algebra object
\Theta_{X}(\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{*}(\mathscr{F}))
, and its underlying connective quasi-coherent sheaf over
X
. In particular, the underlying quasi-coherent sheaf
\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{*}(\mathscr{F})=\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{n}(\mathscr{F})
is equipped with a multiplication map

m\colon\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{*}(\mathscr{F})\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{*}(\mathscr{F})\to\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{*}(\mathscr{F})

which is unital, graded, and commutative and associative up to homotopy.

Remark 3.22
.

Combining the machinery of §
3.1
(especially Remark
3.2
) and Propositions
2.16
,
2.17
, we will obtain the global version of all the properties listed in Propositions
2.16
and
2.17
for the derived symmetric algebras over prestacks. For example:

•

Proposition
2.16
(
1
) implies that if
X
is a derived scheme,
\mathscr{F}\in\operatorname{QCoh}(X)^{\mathrm{cn}}
, and let
X_{\mathrm{cl}}=(|X|,\pi_{0}(\mathscr{O}_{X}))
denote its underlying classical scheme. Then there is a canonical isomorphism of discrete quasi-coherent
\pi_{0}(\mathscr{O}_{X})
-algebras

\pi_{0}(\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{*}(\mathscr{F}))\simeq\pi_{0}(\operatorname{Sym}_{X_{\mathrm{cl}}}^{*}(\pi_{0}\mathscr{F}))\simeq S^{*}_{X_{\mathrm{cl}}}(\pi_{0}\mathscr{F}),

where
S^{*}_{X_{\mathrm{cl}}}(\pi_{0}\mathscr{F})
denotes the classical symmetric algebra of the discrete quasi-coherent sheaf
\pi_{0}(\mathscr{F})
over the classical scheme
X_{\mathrm{cl}}
.

•

Proposition
2.16
(
4
) implies that if
f\colon Y\to X
is a natural transformation of functors
X,Y\colon\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}\to\mathcal{S}
, and
\mathscr{F}\in\operatorname{QCoh}(X)^{\mathrm{cn}}
, then there is a canonical equivalence of quasi-coherent algebras
f_{\rm alg}^{*}\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{*}(\mathscr{F})\simeq\operatorname{Sym}_{Y}^{*}(f^{*}\mathscr{F})
.

•

Proposition
2.16
(
5
) implies that for any prestacks
X,Y
, and
\mathscr{F}\in\operatorname{QCoh}(X)^{\mathrm{cn}}
,
\mathscr{G}\in\operatorname{QCoh}(Y)^{\mathrm{cn}}
, there is a canonical equivalence of quasi-coherent algebras
\operatorname{Sym}_{X\times Y}^{*}(\mathscr{F}\boxplus\mathscr{G})\simeq\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{*}(\mathscr{F})\boxtimes\operatorname{Sym}_{Y}^{*}(\mathscr{G})
. In particular, for any
\mathscr{F},\mathscr{F}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{QCoh}(X)^{\mathrm{cn}}
, there is a canonical equivalence
\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{*}(\mathscr{F}\oplus\mathscr{F}^{\prime})\simeq\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{*}(\mathscr{F})\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{*}(\mathscr{F}^{\prime})
.

We leave the details to the readers (as we will not directly use these statements in this paper). On the other hand, we will study the geometric (and essentially equivalent) version of these properties in the next section.

4.
Derived projectivizations

Let
X
be a scheme and
\mathscr{F}
a discrete quasi-coherent sheaf on
X
, then Grothendieck’s construction of the (classical) projectivization
\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{cl}}(\mathscr{F})
of
\mathscr{F}
over
X
can be described by any of the following equivalent ways:

(a)

\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{cl}}(\mathscr{F})
is the homogeneous prime spectrum of the graded
\mathscr{O}_{X}
-algebra
S_{X}^{*}(\mathscr{F})
, where
S_{X}^{*}(\mathscr{F})
is the classical symmetric algebras of
\mathscr{F}
over
X
.

(b)

\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{cl}}(\mathscr{F})
is the quotient
[(\mathbb{V}_{\mathrm{cl}}(\mathscr{F})-\mathbf{0}_{\mathscr{F}})/\mathbb{G}_{m,X}]
, where
\mathbb{V}_{\mathrm{cl}}(\mathscr{F})
is the classical affine cone
\operatorname{Spec}_{\mathrm{cl},X}(S_{X}^{*}(\mathscr{F}))
and
\mathbf{0}_{\mathscr{F}}
is the zero section.

(c)

\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{cl}}(\mathscr{F})
represents the functor
\operatorname{CAlg}^{\heartsuit}\to\mathcal{S}{\rm et}
over
X
which carries a morphism of classical schemes
\eta\colon T=\operatorname{Spec}R\to X
, where
R\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\heartsuit}
, to the set of equivalent classes of surjections
\pi_{0}(\eta^{*}\mathscr{F})\twoheadrightarrow\mathscr{L}
, where
\mathscr{L}
is a line bundle on
T
.

Consequently, there are three equivalent approaches to defining the derived projectivizations in the framework of derived algebraic geometry. This paper adopts an approach which is the derived counterpart of
(c)
and close in spirit to Grothendieck’s approach in
[
EGAI
]
.

Let
X
be a prestack and
\mathscr{F}
a connective quasi-coherent sheaf over
X
. In §
4.2
, we define the
affine cone

\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})
of
\mathscr{F}
over
X
and study their properties (see Propositions
4.10
and
4.11
). In §
4.2
, we define the
derived projectivization

\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})
of
\mathscr{F}
over
X
via a similar manner as the above
(c)
, and examine their properties, including finiteness (Proposition
4.26
) and functoriality (Proposition
4.25
), their behaviors under quotient maps (Corollary
4.32
, Proposition
4.33
), and describe their relative cotangent complexes through the Euler fiber sequences which vastly generalize the classical Euler sequences (Theorem
4.27
).

Remark 4.1
.

The readers are referred to Hekking’s paper
[
He21
]
for a different approach which is more closely related to the above perspectives
(a)
and
(b)
.

Remark 4.2
.

We present this section in a way such that all the above constructions and results (especially those in §
4.2
) are readily generalized to the situation of Grassmannians, flags, and their variants (see
[
J20
,
J21
]
). See
[
J22b
]
for details.

4.1.
Affine cones

Let
X
be a prestack
\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}\to\mathcal{S}
and
\mathscr{F}
a connective quasi-coherent sheaf on
X
. For for each
A\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
and each
\eta\colon T=\operatorname{Spec}A\to X
, the construction

(4.1)

\displaystyle(\eta\colon T=\operatorname{Spec}A\to X)\mapsto(\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{QCoh}(T)}(\eta^{*}\mathscr{F},\mathscr{O}_{T})\in\mathcal{S})

defines a prestack
F\in\mathrm{Fun}(\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta},\mathcal{S})_{/X}
with a natural projection
F\to X
. More informally, the prestack
F
parametrizes the “cosections of the sheaf
\mathscr{F}
over
X
”.

Proposition 4.3
.

Let
X
be a prestack and let
\mathscr{F}
be a connective quasi-coherent sheaf on
X
. Then the functor (
4.1
) is representable by the relative affine derived scheme over
X
:

\mathrm{pr}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})}\colon\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})=\operatorname{Spec}(\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{*}(\mathscr{F}))\to X.

The universal morphism of
\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})}
-modules
\mathrm{pr}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})}^{*}\mathscr{F}\to\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})}
is induced from the canonical morphism
\mathscr{F}\to\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{*}(\mathscr{F})
by adjunction.

Proof.

We prove a slightly more general statement: there are canonical equivalences for all prestacks
T
and natural transformations
\eta\colon T\to X
:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{Fun}(\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta},\mathcal{S})_{/X}}(T,\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}))\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}(T)}(\eta^{*}_{\rm alg}\operatorname{Sym}_{S}^{*}(\mathscr{F}),\mathscr{O}_{T})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{QCAlg}^{\Delta}(T)}(\operatorname{Sym}_{T}^{*}(\eta^{*}\mathscr{F}),\mathscr{O}_{T})\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{QCoh}(T)}(\eta^{*}\mathscr{F},\mathscr{O}_{T}).

Here, the first equivalence follows from Proposition
3.12
(
3
), the second from Proposition
3.19
(
2
), and the last from Proposition
3.19
(
1
).
∎

Definition 4.4
.

Let
X
be a prestack and let
\mathscr{F}
be a connective quasi-coherent sheaf on
X
. We will refer to
\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})
as the
affine cone of
\mathscr{F}
over
X
, and we will refer to the morphism of
\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})}
-modules
\mathrm{pr}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})}^{*}\mathscr{F}\to\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})}
as the
universal cosection morphism
.

Remark 4.5

(Grothendieck’s dual convention)
.

Beware that we are following Grothendieck’s convention, so that the affine cone
\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})
parametrizes the
cosections
of
\mathscr{F}
rather than sections of
\mathscr{F}
. To put it slightly differently – for simplicity of exposition, we assume now
\mathscr{F}
is connective and perfect – for any morphism
\eta\colon T=\operatorname{Spec}A\to X
, the space of
T
-valued points of
\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})
over
\eta
is the space of sections of the
dual

(\mathscr{F}_{T})^{\vee}
of
\mathscr{F}_{T}
:

\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})(\eta)\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{QCoh}(T)}(\mathscr{F}_{T},\mathscr{O}_{T})\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{QCoh}(T)}(\mathscr{O}_{T},(\mathscr{F}_{T})^{\vee})\simeq\Omega^{\infty}((\mathscr{F}_{T})^{\vee})\in\mathcal{S}.

(Here,
\mathscr{F}_{T}=\eta^{*}\mathscr{F}
, and
\Omega^{\infty}\colon\mathrm{Sp}\to\mathcal{S}
is the zeroth space functor.) In particular, for any field
\kappa
and a map
\eta\colon\operatorname{Spec}\kappa\to X
, the space of
\kappa
-valued points of
\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})
over
\eta
,

\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})(\eta)\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{Mod}_{\kappa}}(\mathscr{F}_{\kappa},\kappa)\simeq\operatorname{Ext}^{0}_{\kappa}(\pi_{0}(\mathscr{F}_{\kappa}),\kappa)\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{Mod}_{\kappa}}(\kappa,\mathscr{F}_{\kappa}^{\vee})\simeq\pi_{0}((\mathscr{F}_{\kappa})^{\vee}),

is (canonically homotopy equivalent to) a
\kappa
-vector space.

Remark 4.6

(Zero sections)
.

By construction, there is a canonical equivalence from the space of sections of the projection
\mathrm{pr}\colon\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})\to X
to the space
\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{QCoh}(X)}(\mathscr{F},\mathscr{O}_{X})
. In particular, the zero morphism
0\in\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\mathscr{F},\mathscr{O}_{X})
corresponds to a canonical section
i_{\mathbf{0}}\colon X\to\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})
. We will refer
i_{\mathbf{0}}
as the
zero section
of the vector bundle
\mathrm{pr}\colon\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})\to X
and denote the image of
i_{\mathbf{0}}
by
\mathbf{0}_{\mathscr{F}}\subset\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})
.

Example 4.7
.

If
\mathscr{F}=\mathscr{O}_{X}^{\oplus n}
, then
\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{*}(\mathscr{O}_{X}^{\oplus n})=\mathscr{O}_{X}[T_{1},\ldots,T_{n}]=\mathscr{O}_{X}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}[T_{1},\ldots,T_{n}]
(where
T_{i}
are indeterminates) and we have canonical equivalence
\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{O}_{X}^{\oplus n})\simeq X\times_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{n}
.

Example 4.8

(Universal Hom space)
.

Let
X
be a prestack, and let
\mathscr{F}
and
\mathscr{E}
be quasi-coherent sheaves on
X
such that
\mathscr{F}
is connective and
\mathscr{E}
is a truncated perfect complex. Then the functor
H\colon\mathrm{Fun}(\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta},\mathcal{S})_{/X}\to\mathcal{S}
which carries
\eta\colon T\to X
to

H(\eta)=\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{QCoh}(T)}(\eta^{*}\mathscr{F},\eta^{*}\mathscr{E})\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{QCoh}(T)}(\eta^{*}(\mathscr{F}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}\mathscr{E}^{\vee}),\mathscr{O}_{T})

is representable by the relative affine derived scheme
\mathrm{pr}\colon\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}\mathscr{E}^{\vee})\to X
. We will let
\mathrm{pr}\colon|\mathscr{H}\kern-5.0pt\mathcalligra{om}_{X}(\mathscr{F},\mathscr{E})|\to X
denote the relative derived scheme
\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}\mathscr{E}^{\vee})\to X
and refer it as the
universal Hom spaces of morphisms from
\mathscr{F}
to
\mathscr{E}
over
X
. Let
\tau\colon\mathrm{pr}^{*}\mathscr{F}\to\mathrm{pr}^{*}\mathscr{E}
denote the canonical map of quasi-coherent sheaves which corresponds to the tautological cosection map
\mathrm{pr}^{*}(\mathscr{F}\otimes\mathscr{E}^{\vee})\to\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}\otimes\mathscr{E}^{\vee})}
; We will refer
\tau
as the
tautological morphism
. By construction, given
\eta\colon T\to X
, then each morphism
\sigma\colon\eta^{*}\mathscr{F}\to\eta^{*}\mathscr{E}
in
\operatorname{QCoh}(T)
is classified by a map
f\colon T\to|\mathscr{H}\kern-5.0pt\mathcalligra{om}_{X}(\mathscr{F},\mathscr{E})|
lifting
\eta
such that there is a canonical equivalence
\sigma\simeq f^{*}\tau
.

Definition 4.9
.

Let
X
be a prestack and
\mathscr{F}\in\operatorname{QCoh}(X)
. We say that
\mathscr{F}
is
perfect to order
n
(in the sense of
[
DAG
, Definition 2.5.7]
; resp.
almost perfect, perfect)
if for every simplicial commutative ring
R
and every point
\eta\in X(\eta)
, then
R
-module
\mathscr{F}(\eta)
has the same property in the sense of Definition
2.15
(this is well-defined since the above properties are stable under base change
[
DAG
, Proposition 3.5.2]
). Let
f\colon X\to Y
be a morphism of derived schemes. We say that
f
is locally of finite presentation to order
n
(resp. locally almost of finite presentation, locally of ﬁnite presentation), if for every pair of open immersions
\operatorname{Spec}B\subseteq X
,
\operatorname{Spec}A\subseteq Y
, where
A,B\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
, such that
f
restricts to a morphism
\operatorname{Spec}B\to\operatorname{Spec}A
,
B
is of finite presentation to order
n
(resp. almost of finite presentation, locally of ﬁnite presentation) over
A
in the sense of Definition
2.15
. Let
f\colon X\to Y
be a morphism of prestacks which is a relative derived scheme. We say that
f
is
locally of finite presentation to order
n
(resp. locally almost of finite presentation, locally of ﬁnite presentation)
if for every map
\eta\colon\operatorname{Spec}R\to Y
, where
R\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
, the induced morphism of derived schemes
X_{R}=X\times_{\operatorname{Spec}R}Y\to\operatorname{Spec}R
has the same property in the above sense for derived schemes (this is well defined as these properties are stable under base change and local on the target with respect to étale topology; see
[
DAG
, Propositions 3.5.2, 3.5.3]
).

We summarize the basic properties of affine cones in the following proposition:

Proposition 4.10
.

Let
X
be a prestack and
\mathscr{F}
a connective quasi-coherent sheaf on
X
.

(1)

(Underlying classical schemes) If
X
is a derived scheme, then
\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})
is a derived scheme, and the underlying classical scheme of
\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})
is canonically identified with the classical affine cone
\mathbb{V}_{\mathrm{cl}}(\pi_{0}(\mathscr{F}))=\operatorname{Spec}_{X_{\mathrm{cl}}}^{\mathrm{cl}}\operatorname{Sym}_{\mathrm{cl}}^{*}(\pi_{0}(\mathscr{F}))\to X_{\mathrm{cl}}
of the sheaf
\pi_{0}(\mathscr{F})\in\operatorname{QCoh}(X_{\mathrm{cl}})^{\heartsuit}
over
X_{\mathrm{cl}}=t_{0}(X)
.

(2)

(Finiteness conditions) The projection map
\mathrm{pr}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})}\colon\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})\to X
is locally of finite presentation to order
n
(resp., locally almost of ﬁnite presentation, locally of finite presentation) if and only if the quasi-coherent module
\mathscr{F}\in\operatorname{QCoh}(X)^{\rm cn}
is perfect to order
n
(resp. almost perfect, perfect).

(3)

(Relative cotangent complex) The projection
\mathrm{pr}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})}\colon\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})\to X
admits a relative cotangent complex given by
L_{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})/X}=\mathrm{pr}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})}^{*}\mathscr{F}\in\operatorname{QCoh}(\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}))^{\mathrm{cn}}
, and the inclusion map of zero section
i_{\mathbf{0}}\colon X\to\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})
(see Remark
4.6
) admits a relative cotangent complex given by
L_{X/\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})}\simeq\Sigma\,\mathscr{F}\in\operatorname{QCoh}(X)^{\mathrm{cn}}
. In particular, the projection
\mathrm{pr}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})}\colon\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})\to X
is smooth if and only if
\mathscr{F}
is a vector bundle, and is quasi-smooth if and only
\mathscr{F}
has perfect-amplitude contained in
[0,1]
.

(4)

(The formation of affine cones commutes with base change) For any maps of prestacks
f\colon X^{\prime}\to X
, there is a canonical equivalence
\mathbb{V}(f^{*}\mathscr{F})\simeq\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})\times_{X}X^{\prime}
.

(5)

(Direct sums) For any pair of connective quasi-coherent sheaves
\mathscr{E},\mathscr{F}\in\operatorname{QCoh}(X)^{\mathrm{cn}}
, there is a canonical equivalence
\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{E}\oplus\mathscr{F})\simeq\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{E})\times_{X}\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})
.

(6)

Let
\varphi\colon\mathscr{E}\to\mathscr{F}
be a map of connective quasi-coherent sheaves on
X
. Then
\varphi
induces an affine morphism
\mathbb{V}(\varphi^{*})\colon\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})\to\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{E})
such that the pullback of the universal cosection morphism on
\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{E})
is canonically equivalent to the universal cosection morphism on
\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})
. Moreover, the morphism
\mathbb{V}(\varphi^{*})
admits a relative cotangent complex which can be described by the formula

L_{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})/\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{E})}=\mathrm{pr}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})}^{*}({\rm cofib}\big(\varphi\colon\mathscr{E}\to\mathscr{F})\big).

If
\varphi\colon\mathscr{E}\to\mathscr{F}
is surjective (that is, the induced map
\pi_{0}(\mathscr{E})\to\pi_{0}(\mathscr{F})
is an epimorphism in
\operatorname{QCoh}(X)^{\heartsuit}
), then
\mathbb{V}(\varphi^{*})
is a closed immersion. In particular, if the fiber of
\varphi
is a vector bundle on
X
, then
\iota
is a quasi-smooth closed immersion (which is given by the canonical cosection of the vector bundle
{\rm fib}(\varphi)
).

Proof.

As the assertions (
1
) through (
5
) are local, they follow directly from the corresponding properties of symmetric algebras of Proposition
2.16
(
1
) through (
5
), respectively. For the last assertion (
6
), the map of quasi-coherent sheaves
\varphi
induces a map of quasi-coherent algebras
\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{*}(\varphi)\colon\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{*}\mathscr{E}\to\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{*}\mathscr{F}
. Hence by Proposition
3.12
, we obtain an affine map
\mathbb{V}(\varphi^{*})\colon\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})\to\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{E})
. By virtue of
[
DAG
, Proposition 3.2.12]
, the composition of maps
\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})\to\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{E})\to X
induces a canonical cofiber sequence

\iota^{*}L_{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{E})/X}\to L_{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})/X}\to L_{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})/\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{E})}.

Hence the desired expression of
L_{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})/\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{E})}
follows from assertion (
3
). If
\varphi\colon\mathscr{E}\to\mathscr{F}
is sujrective, then
\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{*}(\varphi)\colon\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{*}\mathscr{E}\to\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{*}\mathscr{F}
is surjective by virtue of Proposition
2.16
(
1
), hence
\mathbb{V}(\varphi^{*})
is a closed immersion. Finally, if
\operatorname{fib}(\varphi)
is a vector bundle, then
L_{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})/\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{E})}\simeq\Sigma\operatorname{fib}(\varphi)
is a perfect complex of Tor-amplitude concentrated on degree
1
, hence
\mathbb{V}(\varphi^{*})
is a quasi-smooth closed immersion.
∎

Proposition 4.11
.

Let
X
be a prestack and
\mathscr{F}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\rho^{\prime}}\mathscr{F}\xrightarrow{\rho^{\prime\prime}}\mathscr{F}^{\prime\prime}
a fiber sequence of connective quasi-coherent sheaves on
X
. Then there is a pullback diagram of prestacks:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}^{\prime\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}^{\prime}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{pr}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}^{\prime\prime})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{V}(\rho^{\prime\prime*})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{V}(\rho^{\prime*})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\mathbf{0}}}

where
\mathbb{V}(\rho^{\prime*})
and
\mathbb{V}(\rho^{\prime\prime*})
are the natural induced affine maps (see Proposition
4.10
(
6
)), and
i_{\mathbf{0}}\colon X\to\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}^{\prime})
is the inclusion of the zero section (see Remark
4.6
).

Proof.

We may reduce to the case where
X=\operatorname{Spec}R
,
R\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
. Then the assertion follows from Proposition
2.17
. (Notice that this proposition provides an alternative proof of the assertions after “moreover” of Proposition
4.10
(
6
).)
∎

Remark 4.12
.

In the same situation of Proposition
4.11
, it follows from the pullback diagram of Proposition
4.11
that there is a pullback diagram of prestacks:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}^{\prime\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}^{\prime})\times_{X}\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{V}(\rho^{\prime\prime*})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{V}(\rho^{\prime\prime*})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\rho^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\mathbf{0}}}

where
\iota_{\mathbf{0}},\iota_{\rho^{\prime}}
are sections of the projection
\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}^{\prime})\times_{X}\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})\to\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})
that classify the zero map
\mathrm{pr}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})}^{*}\mathscr{F}^{\prime}\to\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})}
and the composition
\mathrm{pr}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})}^{*}\mathscr{F}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{pr}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})}^{*}\rho^{\prime}}\mathrm{pr}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})}^{*}\mathscr{F}\to\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})}
, respectively.

As an immediate application of the above construction, we can formulate the theory of derived zero loci of a cosection in a relative simple form.

Definition 4.13

(Derived zero locus of a cosection)
.

Let
X
be a prestack, and let
\mathscr{F}
be a connective quasi-coherent sheaf on
X
. For a cosection
\rho\colon\mathscr{F}\to\mathscr{O}_{X}
of
\mathscr{F}
, we define the
derived zero locus of the cosection
\rho
to be the essentially unique closed sub-prestack
\iota\colon Z(\rho)\to X
of
X
that fits into a pullback square of prestacks:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z(\rho)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\rho}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\mathbf{0}}}

where
i_{\mathbf{0}}
and
i_{\rho}
are sections of the projection
\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})\to X
which classify the zero map
\mathscr{F}\to\mathscr{O}_{X}
and the cosection
\rho\colon\mathscr{F}\to\mathscr{O}_{X}
, respectively. If
\mathscr{F}
is furthermore perfect, we will generally abuse notations by writing
Z(\rho)=Z(\rho^{\vee})
and referring to it as also the
derived zero locus of the section
\rho^{\vee}\colon\mathscr{O}_{X}\to\mathscr{F}^{\vee}
.

Remark 4.14

(Affine cases)
.

Suppose
X=\operatorname{Spec}A
, where
A\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
, and
\mathscr{F}
corresponds to connective
A
-module
M
. By adjunction, each cosection map
\rho\colon M\to A
corresponds to an essentially uniquely map of algebras
{\rm ev}_{\rho}\colon\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}(M)\to A
. Notice that if
\rho
is the zero cosection map, then
{\rm ev}_{\mathbf{0}}
coincides with the canonical augmentation map
\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}(M)\to A
. Then we can describe the derived zero locus of
\rho
by the formula
Z(\rho)=\operatorname{Spec}(A\otimes_{\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}(M)}A)
, where
A\otimes_{\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}(M)}A
fits into a pushout square of simplicial commutative rings

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}(M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes_{\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}(M)}A.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{ev}_{\rho}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{ev}_{\mathbf{0}}}

Remark 4.15

(Derived zero loci as relative spectra)
.

In the situation of Definition
4.13
, we can describe the closed immersion (hence an affine morphism)
\iota\colon Z(\rho)\to X
as the relative spectrum (Definition
3.13
) of any of the following equivalent quasi-coherent
\mathscr{O}_{X}
-algebras:

\displaystyle\mathscr{O}_{X}\otimes_{{\rm ev}_{0},\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{*}(\mathscr{F}),{\rm ev}_{\rho}}\mathscr{O}_{X}

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{*}(\operatorname{cofib}(\mathscr{F}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{id}_{\mathscr{F}}}\mathscr{F}))\otimes_{\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{*}(\mathscr{F}),{\rm ev}_{\rho}}\mathscr{O}_{X}

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Sym}_{X}^{*}(\operatorname{cofib}(\mathscr{F}\xrightarrow{\rho}\mathscr{O}_{X}))\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}[T],T\mapsto 1}\mathscr{O}_{X},

where the last equivalence follows from the following pullback squares of prestacks:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z(\rho)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{V}(\operatorname{cofib}(\rho))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{O}_{X})\simeq\operatorname{Spec}_{X}(\mathscr{O}_{X}[T])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\mathbf{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{pr}_{\mathbb{V}(\operatorname{cofib}(\rho))}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{V}(\rho^{\prime\prime*})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{V}(\rho^{*})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\mathbf{0}}}

where
T
is an indeterminate,
\rho^{\prime\prime}
is the canonical morphism
\mathscr{O}_{X}\to\operatorname{cofib}(\rho)
,
i_{\mathbf{1}}\colon X\to\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{O}_{X})
is the map classifying the identity cosection
\operatorname{id}\colon\mathscr{O}_{X}\to\mathscr{O}_{X}
; the bottom square is a pullback square by virtue of Proposition
4.11
, and the outer rectangle is a pullback square by definition of
Z(\rho)
, hence the top square is also a pullback square.

Lemma 4.16
.

Let
X
be a prestack, and let
\mathscr{F}
be a vector bundle on
X
of (finite) rank
n>0
. Let
\iota\colon Z(\rho)\hookrightarrow X
be the derived zero locus of a cosection
\rho\colon\mathscr{F}\to\mathscr{O}_{X}
. Then for any
\mathscr{E}\in\operatorname{QCoh}(X)
, there is a sequence of quasi-coherent sheaves
\mathscr{G}_{i}(X,\mathscr{E})\in\operatorname{QCoh}(X)
,
0\leq i\leq n
, such that
\mathscr{G}_{0}(X,\mathscr{E})=\mathscr{E}
,
\mathscr{G}_{n}(X,\mathscr{E})\simeq\iota_{*}\,\iota^{*}\mathscr{E}
, and for which there are fiber sequences in
\operatorname{QCoh}(X)
for all
0\leq i\leq n
(where we set
\mathscr{G}_{-1}(X,\mathscr{E})=0
):

\mathscr{G}_{i-1}(X,\mathscr{E})\to\mathscr{G}_{i}(X,\mathscr{E})\to\Sigma^{i}(\bigwedge\nolimits^{i}_{X}\mathscr{F})\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}\mathscr{E}.

Moreover, if
\mathscr{E}\in\mathrm{Perf}(X)
, then
\mathscr{G}_{i}(X,\mathscr{E})\in\mathrm{Perf}(X)
.

Proof.

It suffices to construct functorially the desired quasi-coherent sheaves and fiber sequences in the case where
X=\operatorname{Spec}A
,
A\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
; The general case for prestacks will follow from the procedure of §
3.1
. Since tensoring with
\mathscr{E}
is an exact functor, by setting
\mathscr{G}_{i}(X,\mathscr{E})=\mathscr{G}_{i}(X,\mathscr{O}_{X})\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}\mathscr{E}
we may reduce to the case where
\mathscr{E}=\mathscr{O}_{X}
.

We consider the following variant of Construction
2.26
. Let
R
be a discrete commutative ring, and suppose we are given a short exact sequence
0\to M^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\varphi}M\to M^{\prime\prime}\to 0
of finite generated free
R
-modules. For any integer
0\leq i\leq n
, we let
g_{i}(R,\varphi)\in\operatorname{Mod}_{S_{R}^{*}M}^{\mathrm{cn}}
denote the class of the “brutal” truncation

(\bigwedge\nolimits^{i}_{R}M^{\prime})\otimes_{R}(S_{R}^{*}M)\xrightarrow{d_{i}}\cdots\to M^{\prime}\otimes_{R}S_{R}^{*}M\xrightarrow{d_{1}}S_{R}^{*}M

of the complex (
2.1
). Then
g_{i}(R,\rho)
fits into canonical fiber sequences of
S_{R}^{*}M
-modules:

g_{i-1}(R,\varphi)\to g_{i}(R,\varphi)\to\Sigma^{i}(\bigwedge\nolimits_{R}^{i}M^{\prime})\otimes_{R}(S_{R}^{*}M).

By a similar procedure as Construction
2.26
, the constructions
(R,\rho)\in\mathcal{E}_{0}\mapsto(S_{R}^{*}M,g_{i}(R,\varphi)\in\operatorname{SCRMod}^{\rm cn}
admit essentially unique left Kan extensions
(A,\rho)\in\mathcal{E}\mapsto(\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}(M),G_{i}(A,\varphi))\in\operatorname{SCRMod}^{\rm cn}
, for which there are fiber sequences of connective
\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}M
-modules

G_{i-1}(A,\varphi)\to G_{i}(A,\varphi)\to\Sigma^{i}(\bigwedge\nolimits_{A}^{i}M^{\prime})\otimes_{A}(\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}M),

and the formation of
G_{i}(A,\varphi)
commutes sifted colimits and base changes of rings. Now suppose
M
is a locally free
A
-module of rank
n
, and we are given a map of
A
-modules
\rho\colon M\to A
which determines a map of algebras
{\rm ev}_{\rho}\colon\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}(M)\to A
. We set

\mathscr{G}_{i}(\operatorname{Spec}A,\mathscr{O}_{\operatorname{Spec}A}):=G_{i}(A,\operatorname{id}_{M}\colon M\to M)\otimes_{\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}(M),\,\mathrm{ev}_{\rho}}A\in\operatorname{Mod}_{A}^{\rm cn}.

Then the formation of
\mathscr{G}_{i}(\operatorname{Spec}A,\mathscr{O}_{\operatorname{Spec}A})
is functorial on
\rho\colon M\to A
and commutes with base changes. Moreover, there are fiber sequences of connective
A
-modules

\mathscr{G}_{i-1}(\operatorname{Spec}A,\mathscr{O}_{\operatorname{Spec}A})\to\mathscr{G}_{i}(\operatorname{Spec}A,\mathscr{O}_{\operatorname{Spec}A})\to\Sigma^{i}(\bigwedge\nolimits_{A}^{i}M).

It remains to show that the canonical map of
A
-modules
\theta_{A}\colon\mathscr{G}_{n}(\operatorname{Spec}A,\mathscr{O}_{\operatorname{Spec}A})\to\iota_{*}\mathscr{O}_{\operatorname{Spec}A}
is an equivalence. Since the assertion that
\theta_{A}
is an equivalence is Zariski local on
A
, we may assume
M\simeq\mathbb{Z}^{n}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}A
, then
\theta_{A}\simeq\theta_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}A
. It suffices to show that the canonical map
\theta^{\prime}_{\mathbb{Z}}\colon G_{n}(\mathbb{Z},\operatorname{id}\colon\mathbb{Z}^{n}\to\mathbb{Z}^{n})\to\mathbb{Z}
is an equivalence of
\mathbb{Z}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}]
-modules (which implies
\theta_{\mathbb{Z}}\simeq\theta^{\prime}_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}]}\mathbb{Z}
is an equivalence). But this follows from the exactness of the classical Koszul complex (
2.1
) in the case
\rho=\operatorname{id}\colon\mathbb{Z}^{n}\to\mathbb{Z}^{n}
.
∎

Remark 4.17
.

Let
A
be a simplicial commutative ring and
\rho\colon M\to A
a cosection of a locally free
A
-module
M
of rank
n\geq 1
. For a map of connective
A
-modules
\varphi\colon N^{\prime}\to N
, we let
G_{i}(A,\varphi)
denote the
\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}(N)
-module as defined in the proof of Lemma
4.16
, and for any integer
m\geq 0
, let
G_{i,m}(A,\varphi)
denote the
A
-module defined as in Construction
2.26
. Since all these modules are constructed from the complex (
2.1
), by construction, for any
m\geq n
, there are canonical equivalences of
A
-modules:

G_{i}(A,M\xrightarrow{\operatorname{id}_{M}}M)\otimes_{\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}(M)}A\simeq G_{i}(A,M\xrightarrow{\rho}A)\otimes_{\operatorname{Sym}_{A}^{*}(A)}A\simeq G_{i,m}(A,M\xrightarrow{\rho}A).

Consequently, in the situation of Lemma
4.16
, for any given integer
m
such that
m\geq n
, we can equivalently describe the quasi-coherent sheaves
\mathscr{G}_{i}(X,\mathscr{E})
by the formula

\mathscr{G}_{i}(X,\mathscr{E})\simeq\mathscr{G}_{i,m}(X,\rho\colon\mathscr{F}\to\mathscr{O}_{X})\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}\mathscr{E},

where
\mathscr{G}_{i,m}(X,\rho\colon\mathscr{F}\to\mathscr{O}_{X})
is defined as in Construction
3.18
.

4.2.
Derived projectivizations

By virtue of Proposition
3.12
(
2
), we will generally abuse notations by not distinguishing the
\infty
-category
\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
and the opposite
\infty
-category of affine derived schemes. Similarly, if
X
is a prestack and
A\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
, we will generally not distinguish the space of
A
-points
X(A)
and the space of maps
T=\operatorname{Spec}A\to X
.

Let
X
be a prestack
\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}\to\mathcal{S}
and
\mathscr{F}
a connective quasi-coherent sheaf over
X
. For each
A\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
and an
A
-point
\eta\colon T=\operatorname{Spec}A\to X
of
X
, we let
P_{\mathscr{F}}(\eta)
denote the full subcategory of
\operatorname{QCoh}(T)_{\eta^{*}\mathscr{F}/}\times_{\operatorname{QCoh}(T)}\mathrm{Pic}(T)
spanned by
surjective
maps
\eta^{*}\mathscr{F}\to\mathscr{L}
(that is, the induced map
\pi_{0}(\eta^{*}\mathscr{F})\to\pi_{0}(\mathscr{L})
is an epimorphism in
\operatorname{QCoh}(T)^{\heartsuit}
), where
\mathscr{L}
is a line bundle on
T
. Here,
\operatorname{Pic}(T)
denotes the full subcategory of
\operatorname{QCoh}(T)^{\simeq}
spanned by line bundles (see
[
SAG
, §19.2]
for more about Picard functors). Then
P_{\mathscr{F}}(\eta)
is a Kan complex, and the construction

(A\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta},\eta\in X(A))\mapsto(P_{\mathscr{F}}(\eta)\in\mathcal{S})

defines a prestack
P_{\mathscr{F}}\in\mathrm{Fun}(\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta},\mathcal{S})_{/X}
over
X
.

Proposition 4.18
.

Let
X
be a prestack and let
\mathscr{F}
be a connective quasi-coherent sheaf on
X
. Then the prestack
P_{\mathscr{F}}=\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})
is a relative derived scheme over
X
.

Proof.

It suffices to show that for each map of the form
\operatorname{Spec}R\to X
, where
R\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
, the pullback
P_{\mathscr{F}}\times_{X}\operatorname{Spec}R
is a derived scheme over
\operatorname{Spec}R
. Hence we may reduce to the case where
X=\operatorname{Spec}R
,
R\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
, and we wish to prove the functor
P_{\mathscr{F}}
is representable by a derived scheme over
X
. Since
\operatorname{QCoh}(X)\simeq\operatorname{Mod}_{R}
, we may abuse notations and regard
\mathscr{F}\in\operatorname{QCoh}(X)
as a connective
R
-module. Let
(t_{i})_{i\in I}
be a family of elements (of possibly infinite members) that generates the
\pi_{0}(R)
-module
\operatorname{Ext}_{R}^{0}(R,\mathscr{F})
. Then the family of maps
\{t_{i}\colon\mathscr{O}_{X}\to\mathscr{F}\}_{i\in I}
induces a map
\mathscr{O}_{X}^{\oplus I}\to\mathscr{F}
that is surjective on
\pi_{0}
. For each
i\in I
, we let
U_{i}
denote the subfunctor of
P_{\mathscr{F}}

such that for every
A\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
and
\eta\colon T=\operatorname{Spec}A\to X
,
U_{i}(\eta)
is the full subcategory of
P_{\mathscr{F}}(\eta)
spanned by those surjections
u\colon\eta^{*}\mathscr{F}\to\mathscr{L}
for which the composite map

\mathscr{O}_{T}\xrightarrow{\eta^{*}t_{i}}\eta^{*}\mathscr{F}\xrightarrow{u}\mathscr{L}

is surjective on
\pi_{0}
.
The desired result will follow from the following assertions:

(i)

For each
i
, the sub-prestack
U_{i}
is representable by a derived affine scheme.

(ii)

For each
i
, the inclusion
U_{i}\subseteq P_{\mathscr{F}}
is an open immersion.

(iii)

The family of maps
\{U_{i}\to P_{\mathscr{F}}\}_{i\in I}
is jointly surjective.

We first prove assertion (

i

). Let
U_{i}^{\prime}
denote the subfunctor of
U_{i}
such that for each
\eta\colon T=\operatorname{Spec}R\to X
,
U_{i}^{\prime}(\eta)
is the space spanned by surjective cosections
u\colon\eta^{*}\mathscr{F}\to\mathscr{O}_{T}
for which the composition
\mathscr{O}_{T}\xrightarrow{\eta^{*}t_{i}}\eta^{*}\mathscr{F}\xrightarrow{u}\mathscr{O}_{T}
is an isomorphism. On the other hand, for each
(u\colon\eta^{*}\mathscr{F}\to\mathscr{L})\in U_{i}(\eta)
, the epimorphism
f_{i}\colon\mathscr{O}_{T}\xrightarrow{\eta^{*}t_{i}}\eta^{*}\mathscr{F}\xrightarrow{u}\mathscr{L}
between line bundles is necessarily an isomorphism. Since
\operatorname{Pic}(T)_{\mathscr{O}_{T}/}
is contractible, we obtain that the inclusion
U_{i}^{\prime}(\eta)\subseteq U_{i}(\eta)
is a homotopy equivalence, and
U_{i}^{\prime}(\eta)
is canonically homotopy equivalent to the fiber space

\mathrm{fib}\left(\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{QCoh}(T)}(\eta^{*}\mathscr{F},\mathscr{O}_{T})\xrightarrow{\circ(\eta^{*}t_{i})}\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{QCoh}(T)}(\mathscr{O}_{T},\mathscr{O}_{T})\right)

over the point
\operatorname{id}_{\mathscr{O}_{T}}
. Consequently, we obtain a canonical equivalence between the prestack
U_{i}
and the fiber of the morphism
\mathbb{V}(t_{i}^{*})\colon\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})\to\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{O}_{X})\simeq\mathbb{A}_{X}^{1}
over the section determined by
\operatorname{id}_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}
. Therefore,
U_{i}
is representable by an affine closed derived subscheme of
\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})
.

We next prove assertion (

⁢
i
i

). It suffices to show that for every affine derived scheme
Z=\operatorname{Spec}B
and every map
g\colon Z\to P_{\mathscr{F}}
, the functor

(4.2)

(\eta\colon T=\operatorname{Spec}A\to X)\mapsto(U_{i}(\eta)\times_{P_{\mathscr{F}}(\eta)}Z(\eta)\in\mathcal{S})

is representable by a derived open subscheme of
Z
. Let
\mathscr{F}_{Z}=g^{*}\mathscr{F}
. By the definition of
P_{\mathscr{F}}
, the morphism
g\colon Z\to P_{\mathscr{F}}
determines a unique surjection
u_{Z}\colon\mathscr{F}_{Z}\to\mathscr{L}
, where
\mathscr{L}
is a line bundle on
Z
. Let
f_{i}
denote the composition map
\mathscr{O}_{Z}\xrightarrow{g^{*}t_{i}}\mathscr{F}_{Z}\xrightarrow{u_{Z}}\mathscr{L}
. By working Zariski locally over
Z
, we may assume
\mathscr{L}\simeq\mathscr{O}_{Z}
and regard
f_{i}
as an element of
\pi_{0}(B)
. We let
U_{Z,i}=\operatorname{Spec}B[\frac{1}{f_{i}}]
be the derived open subscheme of
Z=\operatorname{Spec}B
defined by localization of
B
with respect to
f_{i}
(where
B[\frac{1}{f_{i}}]\to B
is the localization map of simplicial commutative rings for the localization of
B
with respect to
f_{i}\in\pi_{0}(B)
; see
[
DAG-V
, Proposition 4.1.18]
). To show that
U_{i,Z}\subseteq Z
represents the functor (
4.2
), it suffices to show the following assertion:

(enumi)

Let
h\colon Y=\operatorname{Spec}C\to Z=\operatorname{Spec}B
be any map of derived affine schemes. Then
h^{*}(f_{i})
is an epimorphism on
\pi_{0}
if and only if
h
factorizes through
U_{i,Z}\subseteq Z
.

The above assertion (

enumi

) is a direct consequence of the natural homotopy equivalence

\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}}(B[\frac{1}{f_{i}}],C)\to\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}}^{0}(B,C)

of
[
DAG-V
, Proposition 4.1.18 (1)]
, where
\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}}^{0}(B,C)
denotes the union of those connected components of
\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}}(B,C)
spanned by those maps
B\to C
which carry
f_{i}\in\pi_{0}(B)
to an invertible element of
\pi_{0}(C)(\simeq\pi_{0}(\mathscr{O}_{Y}))
.

To prove assertion (

⁢
i
i
i

), it suffices to show that for each map
g\colon Z=\operatorname{Spec}B\to P_{\mathscr{F}}
from an affine derived scheme
Z=\operatorname{Spec}B
, the family of derived open subschemes
\{U_{i,Z}\}_{i\in I}
forms an Zariski open cover of
Z
, where
U_{i,Z}
is the open subscheme of
Z
that represents the functor (
4.2
) of assertion (

⁢
i
i

). To prove this, we let
z\in|\operatorname{Spec}B|=|\operatorname{Spec}\pi_{0}(B)|
be any point, and let
\kappa
denote the residue field of
\pi_{0}(B)
at
z
. Since the map
(f_{i,Z})_{i\in I}\colon\mathscr{O}_{Z}^{\oplus I}\to\mathscr{L}
is surjective on
\pi_{0}
, we obtain that the induced map
(f_{i,Z}\otimes_{\pi_{0}(B)}\kappa)_{i\in I}\colon\kappa^{\oplus I}\to\kappa
is a surjection of vector spaces. Therefore, there exists
i\in I
such that
f_{i,Z}\otimes_{\pi_{0}(B)}\kappa
is an isomorphism. It follows from the above property (

enumi

) that
z\in U_{i,Z}
. This proves assertion (

⁢
i
i
i

).
∎

Definition 4.19
.

We let
\mathrm{pr}\colon\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})\to X
denote the relative derived scheme over
X
which represents the prestack
P_{\mathscr{F}}
, and refer to it as the
derived projectivization of
\mathscr{F}
(over
X
)
. We will let
\mathscr{O}(1)
(or
\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})}(1)
, if we want to address the dependence on
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})
) denote the universal line bundle on
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})
and let
\rho\colon\mathrm{pr}^{*}\mathscr{F}\to\mathscr{O}(1)
denote the tautological map that is surjective on
\pi_{0}
. We will refer
\rho
as the
tautological quotient map
(or
tautological surjection
).

Remark 4.20

(Affine open cover
\{U_{i}\}
)
.

Let
X
be a prestack and
\mathscr{F}
a connective quasi-coherent sheaf on
X
. The proof of Proposition
4.18
shows that whenever there exists a family of sections
\{t_{i}\colon\mathscr{O}_{X}\to\mathscr{F}\}_{i\in I}
that is jointly surjective on
\pi_{0}

3
3

3

Notice by virtue of Proposition
4.25
(
2
),
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})\simeq\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F}\otimes\mathscr{L})
for any line bundle
\mathscr{L}
, so that it suffices to find a generating family of sections for
\mathscr{F}\otimes\mathscr{L}
. For example, if
X
is a quasi-projective derived scheme, up to twisting
\mathscr{F}
by a line bundle, we could always find such a generating family of sections for
\mathscr{F}
.
, there is a Zariski open cover
\{U_{i}\subseteq\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})\}_{i\in I}
of
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})
, where
U_{i}\subseteq\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})
is the open subfunctor which is characterized by the following universal property:

(enumi^{\prime})

Let
h\colon Y\to\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})
be any map of prestacks. Then
h^{*}(\rho\circ(\mathrm{pr}^{*}t_{i}))
is an epimorphism on
\pi_{0}
if and only if
h
factorizes through
U_{i}\subseteq\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})
.

In particular, over
U_{i}
, the composite map of the restrictions

f_{i}\colon\mathscr{O}_{U_{i}}\xrightarrow{(\mathrm{pr}^{*}t_{i})|_{U_{i}}}\mathrm{pr}_{U_{i}}^{*}\mathscr{F}\xrightarrow{\rho|_{U_{i}}}\mathscr{O}(1)|_{U_{i}}

is surjective on
\pi_{0}
, where
\mathrm{pr}_{U_{i}}
is the composite map
U_{i}\subseteq\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})\xrightarrow{\mathrm{pr}}X
. Therefore,
f_{i}
is invertible, and we let
f_{i}^{-1}
denote an inverse of
f_{i}
. (We may regard
f_{i}^{-1}
as a section of
\mathscr{O}(-1)|_{U_{i}}
.) From the proof of assertion (

⁢
i
i

) of Proposition
4.18
, the cosection induced by the composition

\mathrm{pr}_{U_{i}}^{*}\mathscr{F}\xrightarrow{\rho|_{U_{i}}}\mathscr{O}(1)|_{U_{i}}\xrightarrow{f_{i}^{-1}}\mathscr{O}_{U_{i}}

is classified by a closed immersion
s_{U_{i}}\colon U_{i}\to\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})
,
which fits into a pullback square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{O}_{X}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{U_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{pr}_{U_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{V}(t_{i}^{*})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{X}}

where
s_{X}\colon X\to\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{O}_{X})
is the section which classifies the identity map. If
X=\operatorname{Spec}R
is an affine derived scheme, then
U_{i}
is equivalent to the spectrum of the simplicial commutative
R
-algebra
R\otimes_{R[T]}\operatorname{Sym}_{R}^{*}(\mathscr{F})
, where
T
is an indeterminate,
R[T]\simeq\operatorname{Sym}_{R}^{*}(\mathscr{O}_{X})\to\operatorname{Sym}_{R}^{*}(\mathscr{F})
is the map induced by
t_{i}\colon\mathscr{O}_{X}\to\mathscr{F}
, and
R[T]\to R
is the evaluation map
T\mapsto 1_{R}
.

Proposition 4.21
.

If
X
is a derived scheme and
\mathscr{F}
a connective quasi-coherent sheaf on
X
. Then
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})
is a derived scheme, and the underlying classical scheme of
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})
is canonically identified with the classical projectivization
\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{cl}}(\pi_{0}(\mathscr{F}))=\operatorname{Proj}_{X_{\mathrm{cl}}}^{\mathrm{cl}}\operatorname{Sym}_{\mathrm{cl}}^{*}(\pi_{0}(\mathscr{F}))\to X_{\mathrm{cl}}
of the discrete sheaf
\pi_{0}(\mathscr{F})\in\operatorname{QCoh}(X_{\mathrm{cl}})^{\heartsuit}
over the classical scheme
X_{\mathrm{cl}}=(|X|,\pi_{0}(\mathscr{O}_{X}))
.

Proof.

Let
\{U_{i}\}_{i\in I}
be the Zariski open cover of
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})
that constructed in the proof of Proposition
4.18
, then each
U_{i}
is a relative derived affine schemes over
X
. By virtue of Proposition
4.10
(
1
) and the proof of Proposition
4.18
, the family of classical relative affines schemes
\{(U_{i})_{\mathrm{cl}}\}_{i\in I}
over
X_{\mathrm{cl}}
forms an Zariski open cover of the classical projectivization
\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{cl}}(\pi_{0}(\mathscr{F}))
.
∎

Remark 4.22

(Classical projectivization functors)
.

Let
X
be prestack and
\mathscr{F}
a connective quasi-coherent sheaf over
X
. Let
R\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\heartsuit}
be an ordinary commutative ring and let
\eta\colon T=\operatorname{Spec}R\to X
be a map of prestacks (which necessarily factorizes through the inclusion
X_{\mathrm{cl}}\subseteq X
if
X
is a derived scheme). For any line bundle
\mathscr{L}
on
T
, there are canonical homotopy equivalences

\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{QCoh}(T)}(\eta^{*}\mathscr{F},\mathscr{L})\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{QCoh}(T)^{\heartsuit}}(\pi_{0}(\eta^{*}\mathscr{F}),\mathscr{L})\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{QCoh}(T)^{\heartsuit}}(\eta_{\mathrm{cl}}^{*}(\pi_{0}\mathscr{F}),\mathscr{L}).

(Here
\eta_{\mathrm{cl}}^{*}=\pi_{0}\circ\eta^{*}|_{\operatorname{QCoh}(X_{\mathrm{cl}})^{\heartsuit}}\colon\operatorname{QCoh}(X_{\mathrm{cl}})^{\heartsuit}\to\operatorname{QCoh}(T)^{\heartsuit}
denotes the classical pullback of discrete sheaves.) Therefore, we deduce that the restriction of functor
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})
to the ordinary category of commutative rings
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})|_{\operatorname{CAlg}^{\heartsuit}}\colon\operatorname{CAlg}^{\heartsuit}\to\mathcal{S}
is canonically equivalent to the classical projectivization functor
\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{cl}}(\pi_{0}\mathscr{F})
that carries each pair
(R\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\heartsuit},\eta\colon\operatorname{Spec}R\to X_{\mathrm{cl}})
to the space of line bundle quotients
\eta_{\mathrm{cl}}^{*}(\pi_{0}\mathscr{F})\twoheadrightarrow\mathscr{L}
in
\operatorname{QCoh}(\operatorname{Spec}R)^{\heartsuit}
. In the case where
X
is a derived scheme, this provides anther proof of Proposition
4.21
.

Moreover, combined with Remark
4.5
, we see that for any field
\kappa
and a morphism
\eta\colon\operatorname{Spec}\kappa\to X
, the fiber product
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})\times_{X}\operatorname{Spec}\kappa
is canonically equivalent to the classical projective space
\mathbb{P}_{\kappa}(\pi_{0}(\eta^{*}\mathscr{F}))
of the
\kappa
-vector space
\pi_{0}(\eta^{*}\mathscr{F})\simeq\pi_{0}(\eta^{*}(\pi_{0}\mathscr{F}))
, whose
\kappa
-valued points canonically correspond to the elements of the quotient set
(\operatorname{Ext}^{0}(\pi_{0}(\mathscr{F}),\kappa)-\{0\})/\kappa^{\times}
.

Example 4.23
.

Let
X=\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{Z}
and let
\mathscr{F}
be represented by the complex
\mathbb{Z}\xrightarrow{0}\mathbb{Z}
. Then for any ordinary commutative ring
R\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\heartsuit}
and any map
\eta\colon\mathbb{Z}\to R
, the space of
\eta
-value points
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})(\eta)
is homotopy equivalent to the singleton
\{f\colon\mathscr{F}\otimes R\to R\}
, where
\pi_{1}(f)=0
and
\pi_{0}(f)=\operatorname{id}_{R}
, that is,
\mathrm{Map}_{/\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{Z}}(\operatorname{Spec}R,\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F}))\simeq\{*\}
. In other words, when restricting to the subcategory of ordinary commutative rings, the derived projectivization functor
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})
is the same as the classical projectivization functor
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})_{\mathrm{cl}}=\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{Z}
. The difference only happens when we test the values of
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})
on
non-discrete
simplicial commutative rings. For example, let
\mathbb{Z}[\varepsilon]=\operatorname{Sym}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{*}(\Sigma\,\mathbb{Z})=\mathbb{Z}\oplus\mathbb{Z}\varepsilon
denote the ring of
derived dual numbers
, where we informally think of
\varepsilon
as a generator at homological degree
1
(which necessarily satisfies
\varepsilon^{2}=0
). Let
\eta\colon\mathbb{Z}\to\mathbb{Z}[\varepsilon]
denote the canonical map. Then the space
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})(\eta)
is homotopy equivalent to the set

\left\{\mathbb{Z}[\varepsilon]\oplus\Sigma(\mathbb{Z}[\varepsilon])\xrightarrow{(\operatorname{id},m\cdot\varepsilon)}\mathbb{Z}[\varepsilon]\right\}_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\simeq\mathbb{Z}

which is not homotopy equivalent to the space
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})_{\mathrm{cl}}(\eta)=\{*\}
. (In fact, we could obtain from the later Example
4.35
that there is a canonical equivalence
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})\simeq\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Z}[\varepsilon])
.)

Corollary 4.24
.

Let
X
be a prestack, and suppose
\mathscr{F}\in\operatorname{QCoh}(X)^{\mathrm{cn}}
is locally of finite type (that is, perfect to order
0
in the sense of Definition
4.9
). Then
\mathrm{pr}\colon\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})\to X
is proper.

Proof.

The assertion being local, we may assume
X
is representable by
\operatorname{Spec}R
, where
R
is a simplicial commutative ring. The condition of the corollary implies that
\pi_{0}(\mathscr{F})
is a finitely presented
\pi_{0}(R)
-module in the sense of classical commutative algebra. Therefore, the classical projectivization
\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{cl}}(\pi_{0}(\mathscr{F}))
is proper over
X_{\mathrm{cl}}
(see
[
Sta
,
Tag 01WC
]
). We deduce from Proposition
4.21
that
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})\to X
is proper (see
[
SAG
, Remark 5.0.0.1]
).
∎

Proposition 4.25
.

Let
X
be a prestack and
\mathscr{F}
a connective quasi-coherent sheaf on
X
.

(1)

(The formation of projectivizations commutes with base change)
Let
f\colon X^{\prime}\to X
be a morphism of prestacks. Then there is a natural equivalence
\mathbb{P}(f^{*}\mathscr{F})\simeq\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})\times_{X}X^{\prime}
such that the pullback of the universal line bundle (resp. the tautological quotient map) of
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})
along
f\colon X^{\prime}\to X
is canonically equivalent to the universal line bundle (resp. the tautological quotient map) of
\mathbb{P}(f^{*}\mathscr{F})
.

(2)

(Tensoring with line bundles)
Let
\mathscr{L}
be a line bundle on
X
. Then there is a natural equivalence
g\colon\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})\simeq\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F}\otimes\mathscr{L})
such that there are canonical equivalences
g^{*}(\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F}\otimes\mathscr{L})}(1))\simeq\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})}(1)\otimes\mathrm{pr}_{\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})}^{*}\mathscr{L}
and

g^{*}\big(\mathrm{pr}_{\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F}\otimes\mathscr{L})}^{*}(\mathscr{F}\otimes\mathscr{L})\xrightarrow{\rho_{\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F}\otimes\mathscr{L})}}\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F}\otimes\mathscr{L})}(1)\big)\simeq\big(\mathrm{pr}_{\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})}^{*}\mathscr{F}\xrightarrow{\rho_{\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})}}\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})}(1)\big)\otimes\mathrm{pr}_{\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})}^{*}\mathscr{L},

where
\rho_{\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})}
and
\rho_{\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F}\otimes\mathscr{L})}
are the tautological quotient maps.

Proof.

We first prove assertion (
1
). For any
A\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
and
\eta^{\prime}\colon\operatorname{Spec}A\to X^{\prime}
, we let
\eta\colon\operatorname{Spec}A\to X
denote the composition
f\circ\eta^{\prime}
. Then there is a equivalence of spaces from
\mathbb{P}(f^{*}\mathscr{F})(\eta^{\prime})
to
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})(\eta)\times_{\{\eta\}}\{\eta^{\prime}\}
given by sending
(\eta^{\prime*}(f^{*}\mathscr{F})\to\mathscr{L})
to
(\eta^{*}\mathscr{F}\to\mathscr{L})
.

Assertion (
2
) is proved similarly. For any
A\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
and
\eta\colon\operatorname{Spec}A\to X
, we define a map
g(\eta)
of spaces from
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})(\eta)
to
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F}\otimes\mathscr{L})(\eta)
by sending
(\eta^{*}\mathscr{F}\to\mathscr{L}^{\prime})
to
(\eta^{*}(\mathscr{F}\otimes\mathscr{L})\to(\mathscr{L}^{\prime}\otimes\mathscr{L}))
. Each map
g(\eta)
is an equivalence of spaces since it has an obvious inverse given by sending
(\eta^{*}(\mathscr{F}\otimes\mathscr{L})\to\mathscr{L}^{\prime\prime})
to
(\eta^{*}\mathscr{F}\to\mathscr{L}^{\prime\prime}\otimes\mathscr{L}^{-1})
.
∎

Proposition 4.26

(Finiteness properties)
.

Let
X
be a prestack and
\mathscr{F}
a connective quasi-coherent sheaf on
X
. If
\mathscr{F}
is perfect to order
n
for some integer
n\geq 0
, (resp.
almost perfect, perfect; see Definition
4.9
), then the morphism
\mathrm{pr}\colon\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})\to X
is locally of finite presentation to order
n
(resp. locally almost of finite presentation, locally of ﬁnite presentation).

Proof.

It suffices to prove in the representable case where
X=\operatorname{Spec}R
. Let
\{U_{i}\}_{i\in I}
be the Zariski open cover of
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})
associated to a generating family of sections
\{t_{i}\colon\mathscr{O}_{X}\to\mathscr{F}\}_{i\in I}
constructed in the proof of Proposition
4.18
. As the desired assertion for the morphism
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})\to X
is local on the source with respect to Zariski topology, it suffices to prove the assertion for each
U_{i}
. Consider the pullback square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{O}_{X})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{U_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{pr}|_{U_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{V}(t_{i}^{*})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{X}}

of Remark
4.20
. If
\mathscr{F}
is perfect to order
n
, (resp.
almost perfect, perfect), then the projection
\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})\to X
is locally of finite presentation to order
n
(resp. locally almost of finite presentation, locally of ﬁnite presentation), by virtue of Proposition
4.10
(
6
). Since the projection
\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{O}_{X})\simeq\mathbb{A}_{X}^{1}\to X
is smooth and locally of finite presentation, it follows that the morphism
\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})\to\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{O}_{X})
is locally of finite presentation to order
n
(resp. locally almost of finite presentation, locally of ﬁnite presentation), whence
U_{i}\to X
has the same property from base change.
∎

Theorem 4.27

(Euler fiber sequences)
.

Let
X
be a prestack and
\mathscr{F}
a connective quasi-coherent sheaf on
X
. Let
\mathrm{pr}\colon\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})\to X
be the derived projectivization of
\mathscr{F}
. Then the projection
\mathrm{pr}\colon\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})\to X
admits a relative cotangent complex
L_{\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})/X}
. Moreover, there is a natural fiber sequence of connective quasi-coherent sheaves

L_{\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})/X}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})}}\mathscr{O}(1)\to\mathrm{pr}^{*}\mathscr{F}\xrightarrow{\rho}\mathscr{O}(1)

on
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})
, where
\rho
is the tautological quotient map. We will refer to the above sequence as the Euler fiber sequence. Consequently, if
\mathscr{F}
is almost perfect (resp. perfect, of Tor-amplitude
\leq n
), then
L_{\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})/X}
is almost perfect (resp. perfect, of Tor-amplitude
\leq n
).

We will deduce the above theorem from a general result Proposition
4.30
regarding cotangent complexes for maps of modules. Before stating the result, let us review the definition of relative cotangent complexes of morphisms between prestacks.

Definition 4.28

(See
[
DAG
, §3.2]
)
.

Let
f\colon X\to Y
be a natural transformation between functors
X,Y\colon\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}\to\mathcal{S}
. We say that
f
admits a relative cotangent complex
if there exists an almost connective quasi-coherent sheaf
L_{X/Y}\in\operatorname{QCoh}(X)^{\rm acn}
such that for each
A\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
and
\eta\in X(A)
, the functor
F_{\eta}\colon\operatorname{Mod}_{A}^{\mathrm{cn}}\to\mathcal{S}
,

F_{\eta}(M)={\rm fib}(X(A\oplus M)\to X(A)\times_{Y(A)}Y(A\oplus M))

(where the fiber is taken over the point of
X(A)\times_{Y(A)}Y(A\oplus M))
determined by
\eta
) is corepresentable by
\eta^{*}L_{X/Y}
. If the above condition is satisfied, the (essentially unique) quasi-coherent sheaf
L_{X/Y}\in\operatorname{QCoh}(X)^{\rm acn}
is called the
relative cotangent complex of
f
.

Remark 4.29

(Compare with
[
SAG
, Remark 17.2.4.3]
)
.

Unwinding the definition, we see that
f\colon X\to Y
admits a relative cotangent complex if and only if the following conditions are satisﬁed:

(a)

For each
A\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
and
\eta\in X(A)
, the above defined functor
F_{\eta}\colon\operatorname{Mod}_{A}^{\rm cn}\to\mathcal{S}
is corepresented by an almost connective
A
-module
M_{\eta}
. Notice that by virtue of
[
SAG
, Example 17.2.1.4]
, the almost connective module
M_{\eta}
that corepresents the functor
F_{\eta}
, if exists, is uniquely determined up to contractible ambiguity.

(b)

The module
M_{\eta}
that corepresents
F_{\eta}
is functorial on
A
in the strong sense: for each map
A\to B
in
\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
and
\eta\in X(A)
, let
\eta^{\prime}\in X(B)
denote the image of
\eta
. Then the functor
F_{\eta^{\prime}}
is corepresented by
B\otimes_{A}M_{\eta}
.

If both
(a)
and
(b)
are satisfied, then the cotangent complex
L_{X/Y}\in\operatorname{QCoh}(Y)^{\rm acn}
is described by the formula
\eta^{*}L_{X}=M_{\eta}\in\operatorname{Mod}_{A}
for
\eta\in X(A)
.

Proposition 4.30
.

Let
\mathrm{Perf}
be the functor
(R\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta})\mapsto((\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\mathrm{perf}})^{\simeq}\in\mathcal{S})
, and let
{\rm QC}^{\rm acn}
be the functor
(R\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta})\mapsto((\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\rm acn})^{\simeq}\in\widehat{\mathcal{S}})
, where
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\rm acn}
is the full subcategory of
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}
spanned by almost connective modules, and
(\underline{\hphantom{A}})^{\simeq}
is the operation of taking core of an
\infty
-category (that is, discarding non-invertible morphisms). Let
\operatorname{Mod}_{\Delta^{1}}
be the functor
(R\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta})\mapsto(\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R})^{\simeq}\in\widehat{\mathcal{S}})
so that the objects of
\operatorname{Mod}_{\Delta^{1}}(R)
are morphisms of
R
-modules
M\to N
. Let
F
be the subfunctor of
\operatorname{Mod}_{\Delta^{1}}
whose
R
-points are the full subcategories
F(R)\subseteq\operatorname{Mod}_{\Delta^{1}}(R)
spanned by those
R
-module morphisms
M\to N
for which
M
is almost connective and
N
is perfect. Then there is a natural projection map
F\to{\rm QC}^{\rm acn}\times\mathrm{Perf}
that carries
(M\to N)\in F(R)
to
(M,N)\in{\rm QC}^{\rm acn}(R)\times\mathrm{Perf}(R)
.

(1)

The projection
\alpha\colon F\to{\rm QC}^{\rm acn}\times\mathrm{Perf}
admits a relative cotangent complex
L_{F/({\rm QC}^{\rm acn}\times\mathrm{Perf})}
. Moreover, for each
R
-point
\eta\in F(R)
that classifies a map of
R
-modules
M\to N
,
\eta^{*}L_{F/({\rm QC}^{\rm acn}\times\mathrm{Perf})}
can be canonically identified with
M\otimes_{R}N^{\vee}
.

(2)

The prestack
\mathrm{Perf}
admits an absolute cotangent complex
L_{\mathrm{Perf}}
. Moreover, for each
R
-point
\eta\in\mathrm{Perf}(R)
that classifies a perfect
R
-module
N
,
\eta^{*}L_{\mathrm{Perf}}
can be canonically identified with
\Sigma^{-1}\,N\otimes_{R}N^{\vee}
.

(3)

The projection
F\to{\rm QC}^{\rm acn}
admits a relative cotangent complex
L_{F/{\rm QC}^{\rm acn}}
. Moreover, for each
R
-point
\eta\in F(R)
that classifies a map of
R
-modules
f\colon M\to N
,
\eta^{*}L_{F/{\rm QC}^{\rm acn}}
can be canonically identified with
{\rm fib}(M\xrightarrow{f}N)\otimes_{R}N^{\vee}
.

Proof.

We first prove (
1
). For each
\eta\in F(R)
that classifies a map of
R
-modules
M\to N
for which
M
is almost connective and
N
is perfect, and
I\in\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\mathrm{cn}}
, let
G_{\eta}(I)
denote the fiber

{\rm fib}\left(F(R\oplus I)\to F(R)\times_{{\rm QC}^{\rm acn}(R)\times\mathrm{Perf}(R)}({\rm QC}^{\rm acn}(R\oplus I)\times\mathrm{Perf}(R\oplus I))\right)

over the point
(M,N)
. (Here
R\oplus I
is the trivial square-zero extension of
R
by
I
, see
[
SAG
, Construction 25.3.1.1]
). Unwinding the definition, we see that

\displaystyle G_{\eta}(I)

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R\oplus I}}(M\otimes_{R}(R\oplus I),N\otimes_{R}(R\oplus I))\times_{\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}}(M,N)}\{M\to N\}

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}}(M,N\otimes_{R}(R\oplus I))\times_{\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}}(M,N)}\{M\to N\}

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}}(M,N\otimes_{R}I)\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}}(M\otimes_{R}N^{\vee},I).

Therefore, the functor
I\mapsto G_{\eta}(I)
is almost corepresentable by
M\otimes_{R}N^{\vee}
. The module
M\otimes_{R}N^{\vee}
is functorial on
R
in the strong sense: for each
R\to R^{\prime}
,
(M\otimes_{R}N^{\vee})\otimes_{R}R^{\prime}\simeq(M\otimes_{R}R^{\prime})\otimes_{R}^{\prime}(N\otimes_{R}R^{\prime})^{\vee})
. Thus, we have verified the conditions
(a)
and
(b)
of Remark
4.29
, and we deduce that the projection map
\alpha\colon F\to{\rm QC}^{\rm acn}\times\mathrm{Perf}
admits a relative cotangent complex which is described by
\eta^{*}L_{\alpha}\simeq G_{\eta}
. This proves (
1
).

The analogue of assertion (
2
) in the spectral setting is proved in
[
SAG
, Corollary 19.2.2.3, Remark 19.2.2.4]
. We now show that a similar approach works in the derived context. Let
R\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
,
\eta\in\mathrm{Perf}(R)
that corresponds to a perfect
R
-module
N
. Let
H_{\eta}\colon\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\mathrm{cn}}\to\mathcal{S}
be the functor that carries
I
to the space
\mathrm{Perf}(R\oplus I)\times_{\mathrm{Perf}(R)}\{\eta\}
.
Let
d_{0}\colon R\to R\oplus\Sigma I
be the map of simplicial algebras that corresponds to the trivial derivation of
\Sigma I
(see
[
SAG
, Definition 25.3.1.4]
), so that there is a pullback diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\oplus I}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\oplus\Sigma I.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{0}}

Since
d_{0}
is surjective on
\pi_{0}
, we can apply
[
DAG
, Proposition 5.6.2]
and deduce that digram of
\infty
-categories induced by tensor functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Mod}_{R\oplus I}^{\rm acn}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\rm acn}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\rm acn}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Mod}_{R\oplus\Sigma I}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{*}}

is a pullback square. In other words, the induced map

(4.3)

\operatorname{Mod}_{R\oplus I}^{\rm acn}\to\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\rm acn}\times_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R\oplus I}}\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\rm acn}

is an equivalence. In particular, passing to homotopy fibers over
N
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\operatorname{Mod}_{R\oplus I}^{\mathrm{cn}}\times_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}}\{N\}

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R\oplus\Sigma I}}(N\otimes_{R}(R\oplus\Sigma I),N\otimes_{R}(R\oplus\Sigma I))\times_{\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}(N,N)}}\{\operatorname{id}_{N}\}.

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}}(N,N\otimes_{R}(R\oplus\Sigma I))\times_{\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}}(N,N)}\{\operatorname{id}_{N}\}

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}}(N,N\otimes_{R}\Sigma I)\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}}(\Sigma^{-1}\,N\otimes_{R}N^{\vee},I).

(The first equivalence follows from the fact that any lifting of
\operatorname{id}_{N}
to an endomorphism of
N\otimes_{R}(R\oplus\Sigma I)
is automatically invertible; Compare with the argument before
[
SAG
, Proposition 19.2.2.2]
). Therefore the functor
I\mapsto H_{\eta}(I)
is almost corepresentable by
\Sigma^{-1}\,N\otimes_{R}N^{\vee}
.
\Sigma^{-1}\,N\otimes_{R}N^{\vee}
is evidently functorial on
R
in the strong sense as in (
1
). This proves (
2
).

We now prove assertion (
3
). Notice that from the fiber sequence

\alpha^{*}L_{({\rm QC}^{\rm acn}\times\mathrm{Perf})/{\rm QC}^{\rm acn}}\to L_{F/{\rm QC}^{\rm acn}}\to L_{F/({\rm QC}^{\rm acn}\times\mathrm{Perf})}

we immediate obtain that
L_{F/{\rm QC}^{\rm acn}}
exists and there is a canonical equivalence
\eta^{*}L_{F/{\rm QC}^{\rm acn}}\simeq{\rm fib}(M\otimes_{R}N^{\vee}\xrightarrow{g}N\otimes_{R}N^{\vee})
. To show that the above map
g
coincides with the map
f\otimes\operatorname{id}_{N^{\vee}}
, we will give explicit descriptions of the above fiber sequence in our case.

Let
R\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
and fix an
R
-point
\eta\in F(R)
which corresponds to a map of
R
-modules
f\colon M\to N
, where
M
is almost connective and
N
is perfect. We let
F_{\eta}\colon\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\mathrm{cn}}\to\mathcal{S}
denote the functor corepresented by
\eta^{*}L_{F/{\rm QC}^{\rm acn}}
, that is, for each
I\in\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\mathrm{cn}}
,
F_{\eta}(I)
is the space

F_{\eta}(I):={\rm fib}\left(F(R\oplus I)\to F(R)\times_{{\rm QC}^{\rm acn}(R)}({\rm QC}^{\rm acn}(R\oplus I)\right).

Invoking (
4.3
) and arguing as the proof of assertion (
2
), we can identify
F_{\eta}(I)
with the space of commutative squares
\sigma\colon\Delta^{1}\times\Delta^{1}\to\operatorname{Mod}_{R\oplus\Sigma I}
, depicted as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{*}M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{*}N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{*}M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{*}N,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{id}_{u^{*}M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{N}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{f}^{\prime}}

which is a lifting of the degenerate commutative square
\sigma_{0}\colon\Delta^{1}\times\Delta^{1}\to\operatorname{Mod}_{R}
depicted as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{id}_{M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{id}_{N}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

Similarly, invoking (
4.3
) again, we can identify the space
Z=\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}}(M,N\otimes_{R}\Sigma I)
with space of those maps
\widetilde{f}\colon u^{*}M\to u^{*}N
in
\operatorname{Mod}_{R\oplus\Sigma I}
which is a lifting
f
. If we let
Y=H_{\eta}(I)\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{R}(N,N\otimes\Sigma I)
, then there is a natural map of spaces
h=f^{*}\colon Y\to Z
induced by pullback along
f
, that is,
h
carries
\alpha_{N}\in Y
to
\alpha_{N}\circ u^{*}f\in Z
. Then it follows the above description of
F_{\eta}(I)
that it is homotopy equivalent to the homotopy fiber of
h\colon Y\to Z
,

{\rm hFib}(h)=Y\times_{\mathrm{Fun}(\{1\},Z)}\times\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},Z)\times_{\mathrm{Fun}(\{0\},Z)}\{u^{*}f\}.

Notice that the space
G_{\eta}(I)\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}}(M,N\otimes_{R}I)\simeq\Omega\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}}(M,N\otimes_{R}\Sigma I)
of assertion (
1
) is naturally identified with the loop space
\Omega Z
of
Z
(with base point
z_{0}
that corresponds to
u^{*}f\in\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R\oplus\Sigma I}}(u^{*}M,u^{*}N)
). Therefore, the natural inclusion map of spaces
G_{\eta}(I)\to F_{\eta}(I)
coincide with the inclusion map
\Omega Z\to{\rm hFib}(h)
that carries a loop
\gamma
to
(y_{0},\gamma)
, where
y_{0}\in Y
is the base point that corresponds to
u^{*}\operatorname{id}_{N}
. We thus deduce from the fiber sequence of spaces
\Omega Z\to{\rm hFib}(h)\to Y
that there is a fiber sequence of functors

G_{\eta}\to F_{\eta}\to H_{\eta}.

Since the space
Y=H_{\eta}(I)
is naturally pointed (with base point
y_{0}
), there is a naturally induced fiber sequence of spaces (see, for example,
[
May
, Chapter 8, §6]
)

\Omega Y\xrightarrow{-\Omega h}\Omega Z\to{\rm hFib}(h),

where the map
-\Omega h\colon\Omega H_{\eta}(I)\to G_{\eta}(I)=\Omega Z
is induced from
h=f^{*}\colon Y\to Z
; We could informally depict the map
-\Omega h
as

\hbox to100.12pt{\vbox to58.38pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 50.05782pt\lower-29.54274pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

Therefore, we obtain a fiber sequence of functors
\Omega H_{\eta}\xrightarrow{-\Omega f^{*}}G_{\eta}\to F_{\eta}
. This implies the desired equivalence
\eta^{*}L_{F/{\rm QC}^{\rm acn}}\simeq{\rm fib}(M\xrightarrow{f}N)\otimes_{R}N^{\vee}
of assertion (
3
).
∎

Proof of Theorem
4.27
.

Let
F^{\prime}
be the subfunctor of
F
such that for each
R\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
,
F^{\prime}(R)
is the full subcategory of
F(R)
spanned by those maps of
R
-modules
M\to N
for which
N
is a line bundle. Let
F^{\prime\prime}
be the subfunctor of
F^{\prime}
such that for each
R\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
,
F^{\prime\prime}(R)
is spanned by those maps of
R
-modules
M\to N
which are surjective on
\pi_{0}
. By virtue of
[
SAG
, Proposition 19.2.4.5]
, we see that
F^{\prime}\subseteq F
is an open immersion. By the same argument as the proof of assertion (

⁢
i
i

) of Proposition
4.18
, we see that the inclusion
F^{\prime\prime}\subseteq F^{\prime}
is an open immersion. Therefore we deduce that
F^{\prime\prime}
admits a relative cotangent complex over
{\rm QC}^{\rm acn}
and the relative cotangent complex of
F^{\prime\prime}\to{\rm QC}^{\rm acn}
is given by the restriction of the relative cotangent complex of
F\to{\rm QC}^{\rm acn}
to the open subfunctor
F^{\prime\prime}\subseteq F
.

Let
P_{\mathscr{F}}
denote the functor represented by
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})
. Suppose we are given a pair of objects
(R\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta},\eta\in P_{\mathscr{F}}(R))
which corresponds to a surjective map
\eta^{*}\mathscr{F}\to\mathscr{L}
in
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\mathrm{cn}}
. We let
\eta^{\prime}\in X(R)
denote the image of
\eta
under the projection
P_{\mathscr{F}}\to X
. Then
\eta^{*}\mathscr{F}\to\mathscr{L}
corresponds to a point
\eta^{\prime\prime}\in F^{\prime\prime}(R)
. For each
I\in\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\mathrm{cn}}
, we let
F^{\prime\prime}_{\eta}(I)
denote the fiber

{\rm fib}\left(P_{\mathscr{F}}(R\oplus I)\to P_{\mathscr{F}}(R)\times_{X(R)}X(R\oplus I)\right).

Unwinding the definition, we see that
I\mapsto F^{\prime\prime}_{\eta}(I)
coincides with the functor

I\mapsto\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}}(\eta^{\prime\prime*}L_{F^{\prime\prime}/{\rm QC}^{\rm acn}},I).

We deduce from Proposition
4.30
(
3
) that
F^{\prime\prime}_{\eta}
is corepresentable by the connective
R
-module
M_{\eta}=\operatorname{fib}(\eta^{*}\mathscr{F}\to\mathscr{L})\otimes_{R}\mathscr{L}^{\vee}
. Since the formation of
\eta\mapsto M_{\eta}
is functorial on
R
in the strong sense (that is, for any map of simplicial commutative rings
R\to A
, let
\eta_{A}\in P_{\mathscr{F}}(A)
denote the image of
\eta
, then
M_{\eta_{A}}\simeq M_{\eta}\otimes_{R}A
), the conditions
(a)
and
(b)
of Remark
4.29
have been verified. Therefore, we obtain that
P_{\mathscr{F}}\to X
admits a relative cotangent complex which is characterized by the formula

L_{P_{\mathscr{F}}/X}\simeq{\rm fib}(\mathrm{pr}^{*}\mathscr{F}\to\mathscr{O}(1))\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})}}\mathscr{O}(-1).

This proves Theorem
4.27
.
∎

Remark 4.31

(Cotangent complexes over
U_{i}
)
.

If
X
and
\mathscr{F}
satisfies the condition of Remark
4.20
, then we let
\{U_{i}\subseteq\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})\}_{i\in I}
be the corresponding Zariski open cover of
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})
associated to a generating family of sections
\{t_{i}\colon\mathscr{O}_{X}\to\mathscr{F}\}_{i\in I}
. As
U_{i}\to X
is the base change of the affine morphism
\mathbb{V}(t^{*})\colon\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})\to\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{O}_{X})
along the section
s_{X}\colon X\to\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{O}_{X})
, by virtue of Proposition
4.10
(
6
), we obtain an equivalence

{\rm cofib}\big(t_{i,U_{i}}\colon\mathscr{O}_{U_{i}}\to\mathrm{pr}_{U_{i}}^{*}\mathscr{F}\big)\otimes\mathscr{O}(-1)|_{U_{i}}\simeq L_{U_{i}/X}.

Since the composition
f_{i}=\rho|_{U_{i}}\circ t_{i,U_{i}}
is an isomorphism, by virtue of the property (
TR4
) of the stable
\infty
-category
\operatorname{QCoh}(U_{i})
(see
[
HA
, Theorem 1.1.2.14]
) we obtain a canonical equivalence

{\rm fib}\big(\rho|_{U_{i}}\colon\mathrm{pr}_{U_{i}}^{*}\mathscr{F}\to\mathscr{O}(1)|_{U_{i}}\big)\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm cofib}\big(t_{i,U_{i}}\colon\mathscr{O}_{U_{i}}\to\mathrm{pr}_{U_{i}}^{*}\mathscr{F}\big).

Therefore we obtain an equivalence

\varphi_{i}\colon{\rm fib}\big(\rho|_{U_{i}}\colon\mathrm{pr}_{U_{i}}^{*}\mathscr{F}\to\mathscr{O}(1)|_{U_{i}}\big)\otimes\mathscr{O}(-1)|_{U_{i}}\xrightarrow{\sim}L_{U_{i}/X}

over each
U_{i}
. However, unlike the classical situation, it seems to be difficult to explicitly glue these equivalences
\varphi_{i}
on
U_{i}
to an equivalence
\varphi
on
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})
, as least for two reasons: first, as we are working with
\infty
-categories, the “cocycle conditions” for gluing
\varphi
are not conditions but potentially infinite additional data which we need to specify on double, triple, fourfold intersections, etc. Second, since we wish to glue in general non-discrete modules, it is difficult to specify concretely the morphisms
\varphi_{i}
and their compatibility condition data. It is due to these difficulties that we adopt the above functorial approach in our proof of Theorem
4.27
.

Corollary 4.32

(Closed immersions)
.

Let
X
be a prestack and let
\varphi\colon\mathscr{F}\to\mathscr{E}
be a surjective map of quasi-coherent sheaves on
X
(that is, the induced map
\pi_{0}(\mathscr{F})\to\pi_{0}(\mathscr{E})
is an epimorphism in
\operatorname{QCoh}(X)^{\heartsuit}
). Then

(1)

The sujrection
\varphi\colon\mathscr{F}\to\mathscr{E}
induces a canonical closed immersion
\iota\colon\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{E})\to\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})
such that the pullback of the tautological quotient map on
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})
is canonically equivalent to the tautological quotient map on
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{E})
.

(2)

The closed immersion
\iota\colon\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{E})\to\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})
admits a relative cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{E})/\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})}
which can be described by the formula

L_{\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{E})/\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})}\simeq\mathrm{pr}_{\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{E})}^{*}({\rm cofib}(\varphi\colon\mathscr{F}\to\mathscr{E}))\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{E})}}\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{E})}(-1).

Proof.

For each
A\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
and
\eta\colon\operatorname{Spec}A\to X
,
\varphi
induces a natural map of spaces from
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{E})(\eta)
to
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})(\eta)
by sending
(\eta^{*}\mathscr{E}\to\mathscr{L})
to the composition
(\eta^{*}\mathscr{F}\xrightarrow{\eta^{*}\varphi}\eta^{*}\mathscr{E}\to\mathscr{L})
