\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\big\langle\operatorname{QCoh}(\mathbb{A}^{2}),~\operatorname{QCoh}\big(\mathbb{A}^{1}\coprod_{\rm pt}\mathbb{P}^{1}\coprod_{\rm pt}\mathbb{A}^{1}\big)\otimes\operatorname{QCoh}(\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{Z}[\varepsilon])\big\rangle,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Perf}\big(\mathbb{A}^{2}\cup(\mathbb{A}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1})\cup(\{(0,0)\}\times\mathbb{P}^{2})\cup(\mathbb{A}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1})\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\big\langle\mathrm{Perf}(\mathbb{A}^{2}),~\mathrm{Perf}\big(\mathbb{A}^{1}\coprod_{\rm pt}\mathbb{P}^{1}\coprod_{\rm pt}\mathbb{A}^{1}\big)\otimes\mathrm{Perf}(\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{Z}[\varepsilon])\big\rangle.

7.3.4.
Examples satisfying (

a
′

) but violating (

b

)

By virtue of Lemma
7.16
, as long as the pair
(X,\mathscr{F})
in the situation of (

⋆

‣
7.3.1
) satisfies condition (

a
′

) but violates condition (

b

),
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})
is classical and irreducible, but
\mathbb{P}(\Sigma\mathscr{F}^{\vee})
is not classical. For example:

Example 7.28
.

Let
X=\mathbb{A}^{2}=\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{Z}[x,y]
, and let
\mathscr{F}
denote the cokernel of the map

\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{2}}\xrightarrow{(x,y,0)^{T}}\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{2}}^{\oplus 3}.

Then
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})
is the quadric cone
Q=\{xX+yY=0\}\subseteq\mathbb{A}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}=\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{Z}[x,y]\times\operatorname{Proj}\mathbb{Z}[X,Y,Z]
, which is an irreducible threefold with a generic
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-bundle structure over
X=\mathbb{A}^{2}
and with an
A_{1}
-singularity at the point
((0,0),[0,0,1])\in Q
. By virtue of Proposition
4.33
again,
\mathbb{P}(\Sigma\mathscr{F}^{\vee})\simeq\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{Z}[\varepsilon]
, where
\mathbb{Z}[\varepsilon]=\operatorname{Sym}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{*}(\Sigma\,\mathbb{Z})
is the ring of derived dual numbers. Therefore, by virtue of Theorem
7.5
we obtain semiorthogonal decompositions

\displaystyle\operatorname{QCoh}(Q)

\displaystyle=\big\langle\operatorname{QCoh}(\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{Z}[\varepsilon]),~~\operatorname{QCoh}(\mathbb{A}^{2})\otimes\mathscr{O}_{Q}(1),\operatorname{QCoh}(\mathbb{A}^{2})\otimes\mathscr{O}_{Q}(2)\big\rangle,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Perf}(Q)

\displaystyle=\big\langle\mathrm{Perf}(\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{Z}[\varepsilon]),~~\mathrm{Perf}(\mathbb{A}^{2})\otimes\mathscr{O}_{Q}(1),\mathrm{Perf}(\mathbb{A}^{2})\otimes\mathscr{O}_{Q}(2)\big\rangle.

We may replace
X=\mathbb{A}^{2}
by any reduced surface
S
over a field
\kappa
and
\mathscr{F}
by
\mathscr{O}_{S}\oplus\mathscr{I}_{p}
, where
\mathscr{I}_{p}
is the ideal sheaf of a non-singular rational point
p\in S(\kappa)
. The above semiorthogonal decompositions are compatible with results in
[
Ka19
, §7.1]
,
[
X21
, Theorem 4.6]
and
[
PS21
, Theorem 3.6]
on nodal threefolds.

Similar to Example
7.21
, we may consider the global version of Example
7.28
:

Example 7.29
.

Let
X
be a scheme and
Y\subseteq X
be a regularly immersed closed subscheme of codimension
2
. Let
\mathscr{F}=\mathscr{O}_{X}\oplus\mathscr{I}_{Y}
, where
\mathscr{I}_{Y}
is the ideal sheaf of
Y
.

•

\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})
is the classical scheme which is a
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-bundle over
X\backslash Y
,
\mathbb{P}^{2}
-bundle over
Y
, and has
\mathbb{A}_{1}
-singularity along a unique section of the
\mathbb{P}^{2}
-bundle over
Y
. More concretely, let
U\subseteq X
be any open subscheme for which
Y\cap U\subset U
is defined by the equation
(f_{1}=f_{2}=0)
, where
(f_{1},f_{2})\in\mathscr{O}_{X}(U)
is a regular sequence. Then by virtue of Proposition
4.33
,
\mathbb{P}_{U}(\mathscr{F})\subseteq U\times\mathbb{P}^{2}=U\times\operatorname{Proj}\mathbb{Z}[X_{0},X_{1},X_{2}]
is an effective divisor defined by the equation
(f_{1}X_{1}+f_{2}X_{2})=0
.

•

By virtue of Example
4.35
,
\mathbb{P}(\Sigma\mathscr{F}^{\vee})\simeq\mathbb{V}_{Y}(\Sigma(\det\mathcal{N}_{Y/X})^{\vee})
which we could informally think of as the total space
(\det\mathbb{N}_{Y/X})[-1]
of the
(-1)
-shifted determinant bundle of normal bundle, where
\mathcal{N}_{Y/X}
is the normal bundle of
Y
inside
X
. More concretely, over the open subsets
U\subseteq X
of the previous bullet,

\mathbb{P}_{U}(\Sigma\mathscr{F}^{\vee})
is equivalent to
(Y\cap U)\times\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{Z}[\varepsilon]
, where
\mathbb{Z}[\varepsilon]=\operatorname{Sym}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{*}(\Sigma\mathbb{Z})
as usual.

By virtue of Theorem
7.5
we obtain semiorthogonal decompositions

\displaystyle\operatorname{QCoh}(\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F}))

\displaystyle=\big\langle\operatorname{QCoh}(\mathbb{V}_{Y}(\Sigma(\det\mathcal{N}_{Y/X})^{\vee})),~~\operatorname{QCoh}(X)\otimes\mathscr{O}(1),\operatorname{QCoh}(X)\otimes\mathscr{O}(2)\big\rangle,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Perf}(\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F}))

\displaystyle=\big\langle\mathrm{Perf}(\mathbb{V}_{Y}(\Sigma(\det\mathcal{N}_{Y/X})^{\vee})),~~\mathrm{Perf}(X)\otimes\mathscr{O}(1),\mathrm{Perf}(X)\otimes\mathscr{O}(2)\big\rangle.

7.3.5.
Examples satisfying (

b

) or even (

b
′

) but violating (

c

)

By virtue of Lemma
7.16
, as long as the pair
(X,\mathscr{F})
in the situation of (

⋆

‣
7.3.1
) satisfies condition (

b

) (resp. (

b
′

)) but violates condition (

c

),
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})
and
\mathbb{P}(\Sigma\mathscr{F}^{\vee})
are classical (resp. classical and irreducible) but the correspondence scheme
\widehat{Z}
is not classical.

Example 7.30
.

Let
X=\mathbb{A}^{2}=\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{Z}[x,y]
, and let
\mathscr{F}
be the cokernel of the map

\mathscr{O}_{X}^{\oplus 2}\xrightarrow{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}x&0\\
0&y\\
\end{smallmatrix}\big)}\mathscr{O}_{X}^{\oplus 2}.

By virtue of Lemma
7.16
, both
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})
and
\mathbb{P}(\Sigma\mathscr{F}^{\vee})
are classical and reducible (in fact, they are both equivalent to the blowup of
\{xy=0\}\subseteq\mathbb{A}^{2}
along the origin
\mathbf{0}
), but the universal incidence space
\widehat{Z}
is
not
classical. Furthermore, Theorem
7.5
implies that the diagram
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})\xleftarrow{r_{+}}\widehat{Z}\xrightarrow{r_{-}}\mathbb{P}(\Sigma\mathscr{F}^{\vee})
induces equivalences of
\infty
-categories

r_{+,*}\,r_{-}^{*}\colon\operatorname{QCoh}(\mathbb{P}(\Sigma\,\mathscr{F}^{\vee}))\simeq\operatorname{QCoh}(\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F}))\qquad r_{+,*}\,r_{-}^{*}\colon\mathrm{Perf}(\mathbb{P}(\Sigma\,\mathscr{F}^{\vee}))\simeq\mathrm{Perf}(\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})).

(Moreover, the same argument of
[
JL18
, Theorem 4.1]
shows that the composition of equivalences
(r_{-,*}\,r_{+}^{*})\circ(r_{+,*}\,r_{-}^{*})
is a non-trivial spherical twist in the sense of
[
ST
,
AL
]
.)

Example 7.31
.

Let
X=\mathbb{A}^{4}=\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{Z}[x,y,z,w]
and let
\mathscr{F}
be the cokernel of the map

\mathscr{O}_{X}^{\oplus 3}\xrightarrow{\sigma=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}x&y&w\\
z&x&y\\
w&z&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)}\mathscr{O}_{X}^{\oplus 3}.

Then
\operatorname{codim}X_{1}=1
and
\operatorname{codim}X_{2}=X_{3}=4
. By virtue of Lemma
7.16
,
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})
and
\mathbb{P}(\Sigma\mathscr{F}^{\vee})
are classical, irreducible threefolds (which are both birational equivalent to the determinantal variety
X_{1}=\{-xw^{2}+y^{2}w+z^{2}w-xyz=0\}\subseteq\mathbb{A}^{4}
), and
\widehat{Z}
is
not
classical. Furthermore, the diagram
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})\xleftarrow{r_{+}}\widehat{Z}\xrightarrow{r_{-}}\mathbb{P}(\Sigma\mathscr{F}^{\vee})
induces equivalences of
\infty
-categories

r_{+,*}\,r_{-}^{*}\colon\operatorname{QCoh}(\mathbb{P}(\Sigma\,\mathscr{F}^{\vee}))\simeq\operatorname{QCoh}(\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F}))\qquad r_{+,*}\,r_{-}^{*}\colon\mathrm{Perf}(\mathbb{P}(\Sigma\,\mathscr{F}^{\vee}))\simeq\mathrm{Perf}(\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})).

(Moreover, the same argument of
[
JL18
, Theorem 4.1]
shows that the composition of equivalences
(r_{-,*}\,r_{+}^{*})\circ(r_{+,*}\,r_{-}^{*})
is a non-trivial spherical twist in the sense of
[
ST
,
AL
]
.)

There are also similar examples where
X
is an irreducible sixfold,
\sigma
is a
4\times 4
-matrix, and
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})
and
\mathbb{P}(\Sigma\mathscr{F}^{\vee})
are classical, irreducible fivefolds which are connected by a flop, but the universal incidence space
\widehat{Z}
which induces the derived equivalence is not classical.

7.3.6.
Examples satisfying (

c

) but possibly violating (

c
′

)

Example 7.32

(Symmetric matrices)
.

The examples of generic symmetric matrices of size
k\times k
, where
k\geq 3
, are typical situations where condition (

c

) is satisfied but condition (

c
′

) is not. We consider a concrete case, where
X=\mathbb{A}^{6}=\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{Z}[a,b,c,e,d,f]
is the space of symmetric
3\times 3
-matrices, and
\sigma\colon\mathscr{O}_{X}^{\oplus 3}\to\mathscr{O}_{X}^{\oplus 3}
is the tautological map

\sigma=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}a&b&c\\
b&d&e\\
c&e&f\end{array}\right).

Then
\operatorname{codim}_{X}(X_{1})=1
,
\operatorname{codim}_{X}(X_{2})=3
, and
\operatorname{codim}_{X}(X_{3})=6
. Let
Z=X_{1}
denote the symmetric determinantal variety. Then both
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})
and
\mathbb{P}(\Sigma\mathscr{F}^{\vee})
are (classical, irreducible) resolutions of
Z
. More concretely, they are both isomorphic to the subscheme
\widetilde{Z}
of
\mathbb{A}^{6}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}=\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{Z}[a,b,c,e,d,f]\times\operatorname{Proj}\mathbb{Z}[X,Y,Z]
defined by the equations

\displaystyle aX+bY+cZ=0,\qquad bX+dY+eZ=0,\qquad cX+eY+fZ=0.

The universal incidence space
\widehat{Z}
is classical, with two irreducible components, and isomorphic to the classical fiber product
\widetilde{Z}\times_{Z}^{\mathrm{cl}}\widetilde{Z}
. Moreover, by virtue of Theorem
7.5
, the diagram
\widetilde{Z}\xleftarrow{r_{+}}\widetilde{Z}\times_{Z}^{\mathrm{cl}}\widetilde{Z}\xrightarrow{r_{-}}\widetilde{Z}
induces auto-equivalences of
\infty
-categories

r_{+,*}\,r_{-}^{*}\colon\operatorname{QCoh}(\widetilde{Z})\simeq\operatorname{QCoh}(\widetilde{Z})\qquad r_{+,*}\,r_{-}^{*}\colon\mathrm{Perf}(\widetilde{Z})\simeq\mathrm{Perf}(\widetilde{Z}).

By virtue of
[
JL18
, Theorem 4.1]
, the above auto-equivalences are non-trivial, and the composition
(r_{-,*}\,r_{+}^{*})\circ(r_{+,*}\,r_{-}^{*})
is a (non-trivial) spherical twist in the sense of
[
ST
,
AL
]
.

Example 7.33

(Symmetric products of curves,
[
Tod21
, §5.5]
)
.

Let
C
be a complex smooth projective curve of genus
g\geq 1
and let
0\leq\delta\leq g-1
be an integer. Let
C^{(g-1-\delta)}
and
C^{(g-1+\delta)}
denote the
(g-1-\delta)
-th and
(g-1+\delta)
-th symmetric products of
C
, respectively. Let
X
denote Picard variety
\operatorname{Pic}^{g-1+\delta}(C)
of line bundles of degree (
g-1+\delta
) on
C
. By virtue of
[
JL18
, §3.1.3]
, there exists a quasi-coherent sheaf
\mathscr{F}
of perfect-amplitude contained in
[0,1]
and rank
\delta
over
X
, for which there are canonical identifications

\mathrm{pr}_{+}={\rm AJ}\colon C^{(g-1+\delta)}=\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})\to X\qquad\mathrm{pr}_{-}={\rm AJ}^{\vee}\colon C^{(g-1-\delta)}=\mathbb{P}(\Sigma^{\vee}\mathscr{F})\to X,

where
{\rm AJ}
is the Abel–Jacobi map which carries an effective divisor
D
on
C
to
\mathscr{O}(D)
, and
{\rm AJ}^{\vee}
is the Serre-dual version of
{\rm AJ}
which carries
D
to
\mathscr{O}(K_{C}-D)
.

•

By virtue of
[
JL18
, Lemma B.1]
, the condition (

c

) is always satisfied by the pair
(X,\mathscr{F})
, that is, the universal incidence space
\widehat{Z}
is classical, and is isomorphic to the classical fiber product of
C^{(g-1-\delta)}
and
C^{(g-1+\delta)}
over
X
.

•

Suppose
g\geq 3
. Then by virtue of the proof of
[
J19
, Corollary 5.1]
, we see that the condition (

c
′

) is satisfied (that is,
\widehat{Z}
is irreducible) if
C
is not hyperelliptic.

The converse of the second assertion is almost true in the following sense:

•

Suppose
g\geq 3
, then as long as
C
is hyperelliptic and
0\leq\delta\leq g-3
, the condition (

c
′

) is violated, that is,
\widehat{Z}
is reducible.
(To prove this assertion, observe that any complete linear series
g_{g-1-\delta}^{1}
on the hyperelliptic curve
C
is of the form
g_{2}^{1}+p_{1}+\cdots+p_{g-\delta-3}
, where
p_{i}\in C(\mathbb{C})
. Therefore the Brill–Noether locus
W_{g-1-\delta}^{1}(C)\neq\emptyset
and
\dim(W_{g-1-\delta}^{1}(C))=g-\delta-3
, that is, the degeneracy locus
X_{2}\neq\emptyset
satisfies
\operatorname{codim}_{X}(X_{2})=\delta+3<\delta+2\cdot 2
, which violates (

c
′

). See
[
ACGH
, Chapter IV, §3]
for the involved notations.)

Since the condition (

c

) is satisfied by all curves
C
, Theorem
7.5
in this case reproduces Toda’s result
[
Tod21
, Corollary 5.11]
(see also
[
JL18
, Corollary 3.10]
,
[
BK19
]
):

\displaystyle\operatorname{QCoh}(C^{(g-1+\delta)})

\displaystyle=\big\langle\Phi(\operatorname{QCoh}(C^{(g-1-\delta)})),~\operatorname{QCoh}(\operatorname{Pic}^{g-1+\delta}(C))(1),\cdots,\operatorname{QCoh}(\operatorname{Pic}^{g-1+\delta}(C))(\delta)\big\rangle,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Perf}(C^{(g-1+\delta)})

\displaystyle=\big\langle\Phi(\mathrm{Perf}(C^{(g-1-\delta)})),~\mathrm{Perf}(\operatorname{Pic}^{g-1+\delta}(C))(1),\cdots,\mathrm{Perf}(\operatorname{Pic}^{g-1+\delta}(C)(\delta)\big\rangle

where
\Phi=r_{+,*}\,r_{-}^{*}
is the functor induced by the diagram
C^{(g-1+\delta)}\xleftarrow{r_{+}}\widehat{Z}\xrightarrow{r_{-}}C^{(g-1-\delta)}
of the classical fiber product. Therefore, to understand the
\infty
-category
\operatorname{QCoh}(C^{(g-1+\delta)})
is equivalent to understand the
\infty
-category
\operatorname{QCoh}(C^{(g-1-\delta)})
, the morphism algebra of the relative exceptional sequence
\{\mathscr{O}_{C^{(g-1+\delta)}}(i)\}_{1\leq i\leq\delta}
, and the morphism spaces from
\operatorname{QCoh}(C^{(g-1-\delta)})
to
\mathscr{O}_{C^{(g-1+\delta)}}(i)
. By a result of Lin
[
L21
]
, the
\infty
-category
\operatorname{QCoh}(C^{(g-1-\delta)})
is indecomposable. On the other hand, the results on Beilinson’s relations (Corollary
6.21
) completely describe the morphism algebra of the part
\{\mathscr{O}_{C^{(g-1+\delta)}}(i)\}_{1\leq i\leq\delta}
: for all
1\leq i,j\leq\delta
,

\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{\operatorname{Pic}^{g-1+\delta}(C)}(\mathscr{O}_{C^{(g-1+\delta)}}(i),\mathscr{O}_{C^{(g-1+\delta)}}(j))\simeq\operatorname{Sym}^{j-i}_{\operatorname{Pic}^{g-1+\delta}(C)}(\mathscr{F})\simeq S^{j-i}_{\operatorname{Pic}^{g-1+\delta}(C)}(\pi_{0}(\mathscr{F}))

(the last equivalence follows from Lemma
7.18
). Moreover, we can describe the dual relative exceptional sequences in terms of the sheaf of relative differentials
\Omega_{{\rm AJ}}^{i}
(Proposition
6.19
).

Remark 7.34
.

Notice that in the situation of (

⋆

‣
7.3.1
), as long as the pair
(X,\mathscr{F})
satisfies condition (

c

), all the three spaces
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{F})
,
\mathbb{P}(\Sigma\mathscr{F}^{\vee})
and
\widehat{Z}
are classical. Therefore, the classical results in
[
JL18
, Theorem 3.4]
,
[
J21
, Theorem 6.16]
are already applicable to these cases. Moreover, (

c

) (or even (

c
′

)) is satisfied by a generic map
\sigma\colon\mathscr{W}\to\mathscr{V}
if
\mathscr{W}^{\vee}\otimes\mathscr{V}
is globally generated. The new information we get in these cases from the current paper is the generalized Serre’s theorem
5.6
, and Beilison’s relations (Proposition
6.19
, Corollary
6.21
). Furthermore, by virtue of Lemmata
7.16
and
7.18
, we can formulate the output of these results in terms of classical algebraic geometry (although the proofs require the machinery of derived algebraic geometry).

8.
Hecke correspondence moduli as derived projectivizations

Many moduli problems naturally fit into the framework of derived projectivizations.
We will consider one such example in this section: the Hecke correspondence moduli. To motivate our discussion, we first consider the following two examples:

Example 8.1

(Hilbert schemes of points on surfaces)
.

Let
X
be a smooth complex surface, and for an integer
n\geq 0
, let
\mathrm{Hilb}_{n}
denote the moduli space of Hilbert scheme of points on
X
, that is,
\mathrm{Hilb}_{n}
parametrizes ideal sheaves
\mathscr{I}_{n}\subseteq\mathscr{O}_{X}
for which
\mathscr{O}_{X}/\mathscr{I}_{n}
is a zero dimensional sheaf of length
n
. We let
\mathrm{Hilb}_{n,n+1}
denote the
nested
Hilbert scheme which parametrizes the pairs
(\mathscr{I}_{n}\supseteq\mathscr{I}_{n+1})
, where
\mathscr{I}_{n}\in\mathrm{Hilb}_{n}
,
\mathscr{I}_{n+1}\in\mathrm{Hilb}_{n+1}
, for which there is an exact sequence
0\to\mathscr{I}_{n+1}\to\mathscr{I}_{n}\to\mathbb{C}_{x}\to 0
for some closed point
x\in X(\mathbb{C})
. There is a diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Hilb}_{n,n+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Hilb}_{n+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Hilb}_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{+}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{-}}

where
p_{\pm}
are the forgetful maps which carry
(\mathscr{I}_{n}\supseteq\mathscr{I}_{n+1})
to
\mathscr{I}_{n+1}
and
\mathscr{I}_{n}
, respectively, and
p_{X}
is the map which carries
(\mathscr{I}_{n}\supseteq\mathscr{I}_{n+1})
to
x\in X
, where
\mathscr{I}_{n}/\mathscr{I}_{n+1}\simeq\mathbb{C}_{x}
. We let
Z_{n}\subseteq\mathrm{Hilb}_{n}\times X
denote the universal subscheme. From the work of
[
ES
]
, we know that

•

The projection
\pi_{-}=(p_{-}\times p_{X})\colon\mathrm{Hilb}_{n,n+1}\to\mathrm{Hilb}_{n}\times X
identifies
\mathrm{Hilb}_{n,n+1}
as the projectivization
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{I}_{Z_{n}})
, where
\mathscr{I}_{Z_{n}}
is the ideal sheaf of
Z_{n}
.

•

The projection
\pi_{+}=(p_{+}\times p_{X})\colon\mathrm{Hilb}_{n-1,n}\to\mathrm{Hilb}_{n}\times X
identifies
\mathrm{Hilb}_{n-1,n}
as the projectivization
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{E}\kern-1.0ptxt^{1}(\mathscr{I}_{Z_{n}},\mathscr{O}_{\mathrm{Hilb}_{n}\times X})\simeq\mathbb{P}(\omega^{\mathrm{cl}}_{Z_{n}})
, where
\omega^{\mathrm{cl}}_{Z_{n}}
is the classical dualizing sheaf of the Cohem–Macaulay scheme
Z_{n}
.

In particular, the situation fits into the picture of the classical version (
[
JL18
, Theorem 3.1]
) of the projectivization formula Theorem
7.5
, and there is a semiorthogonal decomposition:

\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathrm{Hilb}_{n,n+1})=\langle\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathrm{Hilb}_{n-1,n}),~\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathrm{Hilb}_{n}\times X)\otimes\mathscr{O}(1)\rangle.

See
[
BK19
]
(or
[
JL18
, §3.1.4]
,
[
J19
, §5.2]
) for more details.

Example 8.2

(One-point modifications of Gieseker stable sheaves on surfaces
[
Ne19
,
Ne18
]
)
.

Let
X
be a smooth complex projective surface with an ample divisor
H\subseteq X
. We fix a pair of invariants
(r,c_{1})\in\mathbb{N}\times H^{2}(X,\mathbb{Z})
such that
r>0
and
\gcd(r,c_{1}\cdot H)=1
, and let
\mathcal{M}
be the disjoint union
\bigsqcup_{c_{2}\geq\frac{r-1}{2r}c_{1}^{2}}\mathcal{M}_{r,c_{1},c_{2}}(X)
, where
\mathcal{M}_{r,c_{1},c_{2}}(X)
is the moduli space of Gieseker stable (discrete) sheaves with numerical invariants
(r,c_{1},c_{2})
. We consider the moduli space
\mathfrak{Z}
which parametrizes pairs of Gieseker stable sheaves
(\mathscr{F}^{\prime}\subset\mathscr{F})
for which
\mathscr{F},\mathscr{F}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}
and
\mathscr{F}/\mathscr{F}^{\prime}\simeq\mathbb{C}_{x}
, where
x\in X(\mathbb{C})
is a closed point and
\mathbb{C}_{x}
is the skyscraper sheaf supported at
x
. Then the moduli
\mathfrak{Z}
fits into a similar diagram as in Example
8.1
,

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{Z}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{+}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{-}}

where
p_{+},p_{-}
and
p_{X}
are the forgetful maps which carry
(\mathscr{F}^{\prime}\subset\mathscr{F})
to
\mathscr{F}^{\prime}
,
\mathscr{F}
and
x
, respectively. Similar to Example
8.1
, the forgetful maps
\pi_{\pm}=(p_{\pm}\times_{S}p_{X})
are (derived) projectivizations of sheaves of perfect-amplitude in
[0,1]
. See
[
Ne19
,
Ne18
]
for more details.

This section considers a simultaneous generalization of the above two examples. More concretely, let
X\to S
be a family of derived schemes over a fixed prestack
S
, and let
\mathfrak{M}
denote the moduli prestack of connective quasi-coherent sheaves on
X
over
S
.

In §
8.1
, we will define a prestack
\mathfrak{Z}
of “one-point modifications of connective sheaves on
X
over
S
”, which parametrizes exact triangles of the form

\mathscr{F}^{\prime}\to\mathscr{F}\to x_{*}\mathscr{L}

where
\mathscr{F}^{\prime},\mathscr{F}\in\mathfrak{M}
,
\mathscr{L}
is a line bundle, and
x\colon S\to X
is a section of
X\to S
. We show that under mild assumptions (
X\to S
is separated), the forgetful map
\pi_{-}\colon\mathfrak{Z}\to\mathfrak{M}\times_{S}X
is always a
derived projectivization
(Proposition
8.5
). Moreover, if we restrict our attention to the moduli of “one-point modifications of perfect objects on
X
over
S
with a specified Tor-amplitude”, and suppose that
X\to S
is smooth and separated, then both the restrictions of the forgetful functors
\pi_{\pm}\colon\mathfrak{Z}\to\mathfrak{M}\times_{S}X
are derived projectivizations (Proposition
8.7
).

In §
8.2
, we consider the moduli
\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda}
of general “flags of one-point modifications of sheaves on
X
over
S
”, where
\lambda
is a set partition which encodes the combinatorial information of the flags. We show that the moduli of general flags also fit into similar derived projectivization pictures as above (Propositions
8.11
and
8.13
).

In §
8.3
, we focus on the case where
X\to S
is a family of smooth, separated derived surfaces. In this case, the above forgetful functors are derived projectivizations of sheaves of perfect-amplitude in
[0,1]
. In particular, we could apply all the results of this paper to this situation; see Corollary
8.15
for more detail.

Remark 8.3
.

This section’s exposition and notations closely follow Neguţ’s works
[
Ne17
,
Ne18
,
Ne19
]
which study the shuffle algebra structures and Hecke correspondences for the moduli of Gieseker stable sheaves on surfaces; see also
[
PZ20
,
Z21
]
for more results in this direction. We expect the results of this paper to be helpful for the study of similar algebraic structures for the general prestacks of perfect objects of Tor-amplitude
[0,1]
on a surface
X
or a family
X\to S
of surfaces. Furthermore, the general framework of this section allows us to consider the similar structures on the moduli spaces with other types of stability conditions. For example, it allows us to study algebraic structures on the moduli spaces
\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(X/S)
of Bridgeland (semi-)stable objects on a family
X\to S
of surfaces, where
X\to S
is equipped with (a family of) Bridgeland stability conditions
\sigma
in the sense of

[
Bri07
,
Bri08
,
AB12
,
BM11
,
BM14
,
BLM+
]
. We wish to explore these aspects in the future.

8.1.
Hecke correspondences: the case of one-step flags

Let
X
be a prestack over the fixed base prestack
S
. In this subsection, we construct moduli functors
\mathfrak{Z}
(resp.
\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}
) of “one-step flag of one-point modifications of connective quasi-coherent sheaves (resp. perfect coherent sheaves)” on
X
and study their basic properties.

Construction 8.4
.

Let
X
be a prestack over
S
. Consider the following constructions:

(1)

We let
\mathfrak{M}
denote the functor
\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}\to\mathcal{S}
over
S
which carries each map
\eta\colon T=\operatorname{Spec}R\to S
to the space
\mathfrak{M}(\eta)=\operatorname{QCoh}(X\times_{S}T)^{\mathrm{cn},\,\simeq}
. We let
\mathscr{U}\in\operatorname{QCoh}(X\times_{S}\mathfrak{M})^{\mathrm{cn}}
denote the universal connective quasi-coherent sheaf on
X\times_{S}\mathfrak{M}
.

(2)

We let
\mathfrak{Z}\colon\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}\to\mathcal{S}
denote the moduli functor over
S
which carries each map
\eta\colon T=\operatorname{Spec}R\to S
to the full subspace of

\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\operatorname{QCoh}(X\times_{S}T)^{\mathrm{cn}})^{\simeq}\times\mathrm{Map}_{/T}(T,X\times_{S}T)\times\operatorname{Pic}(T)^{\simeq}

spanned by those elements

((\mathscr{F}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\phi}\mathscr{F});\,x\colon T\to X\times_{S}T;\,\mathscr{L}\in\operatorname{Pic}(T))

for which there is a fiber sequence

\mathscr{F}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\phi}\mathscr{F}\to x_{*}\mathscr{L}

in
\operatorname{QCoh}(X\times_{S}T)
. (Here,
\mathrm{Map}_{/T}(T,X\times_{S}T)
denotes the space of sections
x\colon T\to X\times_{S}T
of the natural projection
X\times_{S}T\to T
.) We could informally think of
\mathfrak{Z}
as the “moduli of one-step flag of one-point modifications of connective quasi-coherent sheaves on
X
over
S
”. We denote the universal element of
\mathfrak{Z}
by

((\mathscr{U}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\phi_{\rm univ}}\mathscr{U});\,x_{\rm univ};\,\mathscr{L}_{\rm univ}),

where
\phi_{\rm univ}\colon\mathscr{U}^{\prime}\to\mathscr{U}
is a morphism in
\operatorname{QCoh}(X\times_{S}\mathfrak{Z})^{\mathrm{cn}}
,
x_{\rm univ}\colon\mathfrak{Z}\to X\times_{S}\mathfrak{Z}
is a section of the projection
X\times_{S}\mathfrak{Z}\to\mathfrak{Z}
,
\mathscr{L}_{\rm univ}\in\operatorname{Pic}(X\times_{S}\mathfrak{Z})
is a line bundle, and for which there is a universal fiber sequence in
\operatorname{QCoh}(X\times_{S}\mathfrak{Z})
:

\mathscr{U}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\phi_{\rm univ}}\mathscr{U}\to(x_{\rm univ})_{*}\mathscr{L}_{\rm univ}.

(3)

We consider the following natural forgetful maps

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{Z}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{M},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{+}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{-}}

where
p_{+}
,
p_{-}
, and
p_{X}
are the respective forgetful maps which carry each element

\zeta=((\mathscr{F}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\phi}\mathscr{F});\,x\colon T\to X\times_{S}T;\,\mathscr{L}\in\operatorname{Pic}(T))\in\mathfrak{Z}(\eta),

where
\eta\colon T=\operatorname{Spec}R\to S
, to the elements
p_{+}(\zeta)=\mathscr{F}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{M}(\eta)
,
p_{-}(\zeta)=\mathscr{F}\in\mathfrak{M}(\eta)
, and the composition
p_{X}(\zeta)=(T\xrightarrow{x}X\times_{S}T\xrightarrow{\mathrm{pr}_{X}}X)\in X(\eta)
, respectively.

(4)

We consider the following maps:

\displaystyle\pi_{+}=(p_{+}\times_{S}p_{X})\colon\mathfrak{Z}\to\mathfrak{M}\times_{S}X,\qquad\pi_{-}=(p_{-}\times_{S}p_{X})\colon\mathfrak{Z}\to\mathfrak{M}\times_{S}X.

(Therefore
\pi_{+}
is the map which carries the element
\zeta=((\mathscr{F}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\phi}\mathscr{F});x;\mathscr{L})
to
(\mathscr{F}^{\prime},\mathrm{pr}_{X}\circ x)
, and
\pi_{+}
is the map which carries
\zeta=((\mathscr{F}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\phi}\mathscr{F});\,x;\,\mathscr{L})
to
(\mathscr{F},\mathrm{pr}_{X}\circ x)
.)

The following result relates the functors
\mathfrak{Z}
with derived projectivizations.

Proposition 8.5

(Compare with
[
Ne19
, Definition 2.5, Proposition 2.8]
)
.

In the situation of Construction
8.4
, suppose that the map
X\to S
is separated and a relative derived scheme, then the projection map
\pi_{-}\colon\mathfrak{Z}\to\mathfrak{M}\times_{S}X
identifies
\mathfrak{Z}
with the derived projectivization
\mathbb{P}_{\mathfrak{M}\times_{S}X}(\mathscr{U})
and for which there is a canonical identification
\mathscr{O}(1)\simeq\mathscr{L}_{\rm univ}
.

Proof.

For any map
\eta\colon T=\operatorname{Spec}R\to S
, where
R\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
, and a map
\widetilde{\eta}=(f\times_{S}g)\colon T\to\mathfrak{M}\times_{S}X
over
\eta
, we set
\mathscr{F}=(\operatorname{id}_{X}\times_{S}f)^{*}(\mathscr{U})\in\operatorname{QCoh}(X\times_{S}T)^{\mathrm{cn}}
and
x=(g\times_{S}\operatorname{id}_{T})\colon T\to X\times_{S}T
, so that
\widetilde{\eta}^{*}\,\mathscr{U}\simeq x^{*}\mathscr{F}
. Then the space of liftings
T\to\mathfrak{Z}
of
\widetilde{\eta}
is the space of pairs
((\mathscr{F}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\phi}\mathscr{F})\in\operatorname{QCoh}(X\times_{S}T)^{\mathrm{cn}}_{/\mathscr{F}},\mathscr{L}\in\operatorname{Pic}(T))
for which there is a fiber sequence
\mathscr{F}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\phi}\mathscr{F}\to x_{*}\mathscr{L}
. Such a space is homotopy equivalent to the space of pairs
(\mathscr{L}\in\operatorname{Pic}(T),(\mathscr{F}\xrightarrow{\varphi}x_{*}\mathscr{L})\in\operatorname{QCoh}(X\times_{S}T)_{\mathscr{F}/})
for which
\varphi
is surjective on
\pi_{0}
(and a homotopy inverse map is given by setting
\mathscr{F}^{\prime}
to be the fiber of
\varphi
). We claim that the above space of pairs
(\mathscr{L},\varphi)
is homotopy equivalent to the space of pairs
(\mathscr{L}\in\operatorname{Pic}(T),x^{*}\mathscr{F}\xrightarrow{\rho}\mathscr{L})
for which
\rho
is surjective on
\pi_{0}
, where
\rho
is the map induced by
\varphi
from the adjunction

\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{QCoh}(X\times_{S}T)}(\mathscr{F},x_{*}\mathscr{L})\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{QCoh}(T)}(x^{*}\mathscr{F},\mathscr{L}).

In other words, the space of liftings
T\to\mathfrak{Z}
of
\widetilde{\eta}
along
\pi_{-}
is canonical homotopy equivalent to the space
\mathbb{P}_{\mathfrak{M}\times_{S}X}(\mathscr{U})(\widetilde{\eta})
over
\widetilde{\eta}
, where
\mathbb{P}_{\mathfrak{M}\times_{S}X}(\mathscr{U})
is the derived projectivization (§
4.2
), which will prove the desired assertion.

Let
\rho\colon x^{*}\mathscr{F}\to\mathscr{L}
be a morphism which corresponds to
\varphi\colon\mathscr{F}\to x_{*}\mathscr{L}
through the above adjunction. Then to prove the above claim, it suffices to show the following:

(enumi)

\rho
is surjective on
\pi_{0}
if and only if
\varphi
is surjective on
\pi_{0}
.

We now prove (

enumi

). First, if
\varphi
is surjective on
\pi_{0}
, then the counit map
x^{*}x_{*}\mathscr{L}\to\mathscr{L}
is surjective on
\pi_{0}
(since
x
is an affine morphism), and
x^{*}\varphi
is surjective on
\pi_{0}
(since the property “surjection on
\pi_{0}
” is stable under pullback). Therefore, we obtain that the composition
\rho\colon x^{*}\mathscr{F}\xrightarrow{x^{*}\varphi}x^{*}x_{*}\mathscr{L}\to\mathscr{L}
is surjective on
\pi_{0}
. On the other hand, if
\rho
is surjective on
\pi_{0}
, then
x_{*}\rho
is surjective on
\pi_{0}
(since
x
is affine). To show
\varphi\colon\mathscr{F}\to x_{*}x^{*}\mathscr{F}\xrightarrow{x_{*}\rho}x_{*}\mathscr{L}
is surjective on
\pi_{0}
, it suffices to show that the unit map
\mathscr{F}\to x_{*}x^{*}\mathscr{F}
is surjective on
\pi_{0}
for all closed immersions
x
and connective quasi-coherent sheaves
\mathscr{F}
. As this assertion is local, it suffices to show that if
A\to B
is map of simplicial commutative rings which induces a surjective map of discrete commutative rings
\pi_{0}(A)\twoheadrightarrow\pi_{0}(B)
, and
M
is a connective
A
-module, then the natural map
M\to M\otimes_{A}B
induces an epimorphism
\pi_{0}(M)\twoheadrightarrow\pi_{0}(M\otimes_{A}B)
. But this is evident by virtue of
\pi_{0}(M\otimes_{A}B)\simeq\operatorname{Tor}_{0}^{\pi_{0}(A)}(\pi_{0}(M),\pi_{0}(B))
(see
[
HA
, Corollary 7.2.1.23]
).
∎

Next, we restrict our attention to the “one-step flag of one-point modifications” of quasi-coherent sheaves with a specified range of perfect-amplitude.

Notation 8.6
.

Suppose that the map of prestacks
X\to S
is smooth, separated and a relative derived scheme of constant relative dimension
d\geq 1
. Then the smooth morphism
X\to S
is equipped with a relative canonical line bundle given by the formula
\mathscr{K}_{X/S}=\bigwedge^{d}L_{X/S}
, where
L_{X/S}
is the relative cotangent complex (Definition
4.28
). In this situation:

(1)

For any prestack
Y
, we let
\mathrm{Perf}(Y)_{[0,d-1]}
denote the full subcategory of
\operatorname{QCoh}(Y)
spanned by objects of perfect-amplitude contained in
[0,d-1]
(that is, spanned by connective perfect objects of Tor-amplitude
\leq d-1
).

(2)

We let
\mathfrak{M}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]}
denote the subfunctor of
\mathfrak{M}
such that for each map
\eta\colon T=\operatorname{Spec}R\to S
,
\mathfrak{M}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]}(\eta)=\mathrm{Perf}(X\times_{S}T)_{[0,d-1]}^{\simeq}
is the full subcategory of
\mathfrak{M}(\eta)=\operatorname{QCoh}(X\times_{S}T)^{\mathrm{cn},\,\simeq}
spanned by objects of perfect-amplitude contained in
[0,d-1]
. By abuse of notation, we also let
\mathscr{U}\in\mathrm{Perf}(X\times_{S}\mathfrak{M}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]})_{[0,d-1]}
denote the universal perfect object.

(3)

Similarly, we let
\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]}
denote the subfunctor of
\mathfrak{Z}
such that for each map
\eta\colon T=\operatorname{Spec}R\to S
,
\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]}(\eta)
is the full subcategory of
\mathfrak{Z}(\eta)
spanned by those

\zeta=((\mathscr{F}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\phi}\mathscr{F});\,x\colon T\to X\times_{S}T;\,\mathscr{L}\in\operatorname{Pic}(T))\in\mathfrak{Z}(\eta)

for which
\mathscr{F},\mathscr{F}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{M}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]}(T)
(that is,
\mathscr{F}
and
\mathscr{F}^{\prime}
are of perfect-amplitude in
[0,d-1]
). By abuse of notation, we use the same notation as Construction
8.4
,

((\mathscr{U}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\phi_{\rm univ}}\mathscr{U});\,x_{\rm univ};\,\mathscr{L}_{\rm univ}),

to denote the universal element of
\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]}
, where
\mathscr{U}^{\prime},\mathscr{U}\in\mathrm{Perf}(\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]}\times_{S}X)_{[0,d-1]}
.

The following result shows that when restricted to perfect objects with a specified range of Tor-amplitudes, the projection
\pi_{+}
also fits in the picture of derived projectivizations.

Proposition 8.7

(Compare with
[
Ne19
, Proposition 2.8]
)
.

In the situation of Notation
8.6
and Construction
8.4
, the following is true:

(1)

The restriction
\pi_{-}|_{\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]}}\colon\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]}\to\mathfrak{M}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]}\times_{S}X
of the projection map
\pi_{-}
identifies
\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]}
with the derived projectivization
\mathbb{P}_{\mathfrak{M}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]}\times_{S}X}(\mathscr{U})
, and for which there is a canonical identification of line bundles
\mathscr{O}(1)\simeq\mathscr{L}_{\rm univ}
.

(2)

The restriction
\pi_{+}|_{\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]}}\colon\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]}\to\mathfrak{M}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]}\times_{S}X
of the projection map
\pi_{+}
identifies
\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]}
with the derived projectivization

\mathbb{P}_{\mathfrak{M}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]}\times_{S}X}(\Sigma^{d-1}\,\mathscr{U}^{\vee}\otimes\mathscr{K}_{X/S})
, and for which there is a canonical identification of line bundles
\mathscr{O}(1)\simeq\mathscr{L}_{\rm univ}^{\vee}
.

Notation.
In the above assertion (
2
) and for the rest of this section, by abuse of notations, we shall use the same notation
\mathscr{K}_{X/S}
to denote the line bundle
f^{*}\mathscr{K}_{X/S}
for any given map of prestacks
f\colon Y\to X
. For example, in the expression of (
2
),
\mathscr{U}^{\vee}\otimes\mathscr{K}_{X/S}
denotes the object
\mathscr{U}^{\vee}\otimes\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{*}\mathscr{K}_{X/S}
, where
\mathrm{pr}_{2}\colon\mathfrak{M}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]}\times_{S}X\to X
is the natural projection.

Proof.

For any map
\eta\colon T=\operatorname{Spec}R\to S
,
R\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
, since
X\to S
is a smooth, separated relative derived scheme, any section
x\colon T\to X\times_{S}T
of the projection
X\times_{S}T\to T
is a quasi-smooth closed immersion. In particular,
x_{*}\colon\operatorname{QCoh}(T)\to\operatorname{QCoh}(X\times T)
carries perfect complexes to perfect complexes (Theorem
3.7
(
3
)). Let
x_{!}
denote the left adjoint of the pullback functor
x^{*}
, then there is a canonical equivalence
x_{!}(\mathscr{E})\simeq\Sigma^{-d}x_{*}(\mathscr{E}\otimes\mathscr{K}_{X/S}^{\vee})
for all
\mathscr{E}\in\operatorname{QCoh}(X\times_{S}T)
(Theorem
3.7
(
22g
)). In particular, for any
\mathscr{L}\in\operatorname{Pic}(T)
, we have canonical equivalences
(x_{*}\mathscr{L})^{\vee}\simeq x_{!}(\mathscr{L}^{\vee})\simeq\Sigma^{-d}x_{*}(\mathscr{L}^{\vee}\otimes\mathscr{K}_{X/S}^{\vee})
(Theorem
3.7
(
22d
)), so that
x_{*}\mathscr{L}
and
\Sigma^{d}\,(x_{*}\mathscr{L})^{\vee}
both have perfect-amplitude contained in
[0,d]
.

We first prove assertion (
1
). By virtue of Proposition
8.5
, we only need to show for any
\mathscr{F}\in\mathrm{Perf}(X\times_{S}T)_{[0,d-1]}
,
\mathscr{L}\in\operatorname{Pic}(T)
, and a map
\varphi\colon\mathscr{F}\to x_{*}\mathscr{L}
which is surjective on
\pi_{0}
, the fiber of
\varphi
is of perfect-amplitude in
[0,d-1]
. Let
\mathscr{F}^{\prime}=\operatorname{fib}(\varphi)
, then
\mathscr{F}^{\prime}
is clearly a connective perfect object. On the other hand, from the fiber sequence
\Sigma^{d-1}\mathscr{F}^{\vee}\to\Sigma^{d-1}\mathscr{F}^{\prime\vee}\to\Sigma^{d}(x_{*}\mathscr{L})^{\vee}
we obtain that
\Sigma^{d-1}\mathscr{F}^{\prime\vee}
is a connective perfect object. Therefore
\mathscr{F}^{\prime}
is of perfect-amplitude contained in
[0,d-1]
. This proves assertion (
1
).

We next prove assertion (
2
). For any given map
\widetilde{\eta}=(f^{\prime}\times_{S}g)\colon T\to\mathfrak{M}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]}\times_{S}X
, we set
\mathscr{F}^{\prime}=(\operatorname{id}_{X}\times_{S}f^{\prime})^{*}(\mathscr{U})\in\mathrm{Perf}(X\times_{S}T)^{\mathrm{cn}}_{[0,d-1]}
and
x=(g\times_{S}\operatorname{id}_{T})\colon T\to X\times_{S}T
, so that
\widetilde{\eta}^{*}\,\mathscr{U}\simeq x^{*}\mathscr{F}^{\prime}
. Then the space of liftings of
\widetilde{\eta}
along
\pi_{+}|_{\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]}}
is canonically identified with the space of pairs

((\mathscr{F}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\phi}\mathscr{F})\in(\mathrm{Perf}(X\times_{S}T)_{[0,d-1]})_{\mathscr{F}^{\prime}/},\mathscr{L}\in\operatorname{Pic}(T))

for which there is a fiber sequence
\mathscr{F}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\phi}\mathscr{F}\to x_{*}\mathscr{L}
. This space is homotopy equivalent to the space of pairs
(\mathscr{L}\in\operatorname{Pic}(T),x_{*}\mathscr{L}\xrightarrow{\psi}\Sigma\mathscr{F}^{\prime})
for which
{\operatorname{fib}}(\psi)
is of perfect-amplitude in
[0,d-1]
(and a homotopy inverse of the above map is given by setting
\mathscr{F}={\rm fib}(\psi)
). There are canonical homotopy equivalences of spaces

\displaystyle\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{QCoh}(X\times_{S}T)}(x_{*}\mathscr{L},\Sigma\mathscr{F}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{QCoh}(X\times_{S}T)}(\Sigma^{-1}\,\mathscr{F}^{\prime\vee},(x_{*}\mathscr{L})^{\vee})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{QCoh}(X\times_{S}T)}(\Sigma^{-1}\,\mathscr{F}^{\prime\vee},x_{!}(\mathscr{L}^{\vee}))

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{QCoh}(X\times_{S}T)}(\Sigma^{-1}\,\mathscr{F}^{\prime\vee},\Sigma^{-d}x_{*}(\mathscr{L}^{\vee}\otimes\mathscr{K}_{X/S}^{\vee}))

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\operatorname{QCoh}(T)}(x^{*}(\Sigma^{d-1}\,\mathscr{F}^{\prime\vee}\otimes\mathscr{K}_{X/S}),\mathscr{L}^{\vee}).

Let
\rho\colon x^{*}(\Sigma^{d-1}\,\mathscr{F}^{\prime\vee}\otimes\mathscr{K}_{X/S})\to\mathscr{L}^{\vee}
denote the map which corresponds to
\psi\colon x_{*}\mathscr{L}\to\Sigma\,\mathscr{F}^{\prime}
via the above equivalences. To prove the assertion (
2
), it remains to show

(enumi^{\prime})

\rho
is surjective on
\pi_{0}
if and only if
\operatorname{fib}(\psi)
is of perfect-amplitude in
[0,d-1]
.

On the one hand, the assertion (

enumi

) of Proposition
8.5
implies that

(enumienumi^{\prime})

\rho
is surjective on
\pi_{0}
if and only if
\Sigma^{d}\psi^{\vee}\colon\Sigma^{d-1}\mathscr{F}^{\prime\vee}\to\Sigma^{d}(x_{*}\mathscr{L})^{\vee}
is surjective on
\pi_{0}
.

We now deduce assertion (

enumi
′

) from (

enumienumi
′

) as follows. If we assume
\mathscr{F}:=\operatorname{fib}(\psi)
is of perfect-amplitude in
[0,d-1]
, then
\operatorname{fib}(\Sigma^{d}\psi^{\vee})\simeq\Sigma^{d-1}\mathscr{F}^{\vee}
is connective, which implies that
\rho
is surjective on
\pi_{0}
, by virtue of assertion (

enumienumi
′

). On the other hand, if
\rho
is surjective on
\pi_{0}
, then
\mathscr{M}:=\operatorname{fib}(\Sigma^{d}\psi^{\vee})
is connective by virtue of assertion (

enumienumi
′

). Let
\mathscr{F}:=\operatorname{fib}(\psi)
, then from the fiber sequence
\mathscr{F}^{\prime}\to\mathscr{F}\to x_{*}\mathscr{L}
we obtain that
\mathscr{F}
is connective and perfect. By virtue of the equivalence
\mathscr{M}\simeq\Sigma^{d-1}\mathscr{F}^{\vee}
again and the connectivity of
\mathscr{M}
, we obtain
\mathscr{F}
is of perfect-amplitude contained in
[0,d-1]
. This proves assertion (

enumi
′

).
∎

8.2.
Hecke correspondences: the case of general flags

Let
X\to S
be a map of prestacks. In this section, we generalize the constructions and results of the preceding §
8.1
to the case of general “flags of one-point modifications” of quasi-coherent sheaves on
X
over
S
.

Construction 8.8

(Compare with
[
Ne18
, Definition 2.13]
)
.

Let
X
be a prestack over
S
. A
set partition
\lambda
of size
n
is an equivalence relation on the set
\{1,2,\ldots,n\}
. Let
\lambda
a set partition of size
n\geq 1
, and we let
\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda}\colon\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}\to\mathcal{S}
denote the functor which carries each map
\eta\colon T=\operatorname{Spec}R\to S
to the full subspace of

\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{n},\operatorname{QCoh}(X\times_{S}T)^{\mathrm{cn}})^{\simeq}\times\mathrm{Map}_{/T}(T,X\times_{S}T)^{\times n}\times(\operatorname{Pic}(T)^{\simeq})^{\times n}

spanned by those elements

\zeta=((\mathscr{F}_{0}\xrightarrow{\phi_{1}}\mathscr{F}_{1}\xrightarrow{\phi_{2}}\cdots\xrightarrow{\phi_{n}}\mathscr{F}_{n}),\{x_{i}\colon T\to X\times_{S}T\}_{1\leq i\leq n},\{\mathscr{L}_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq n})

for which the following conditions are satisfied:

•

The sections satisfy
x_{i}=x_{j}
if
i\sim j
in
\lambda
;

•

There are equivalences
{\rm cofib}(\mathscr{F}_{i-1}\xrightarrow{\phi_{i}}\mathscr{F}_{i})\simeq(x_{i})_{*}\mathscr{L}_{i}
.

We denote the universal element of
\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda}
by

\zeta_{\rm univ}=((\mathscr{U}_{0}\xrightarrow{\phi_{1,\rm univ}}\mathscr{U}_{1}\xrightarrow{\phi_{2,\rm univ}}\cdots\xrightarrow{\phi_{n,\rm univ}}\mathscr{U}_{n}),\{x_{i,\rm univ}\}_{1\leq i\leq n},\{\mathscr{L}_{i,\rm univ}\}_{1\leq i\leq n}),

where
\mathscr{U}_{i}\in\operatorname{QCoh}(X\times_{S}\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda})^{\mathrm{cn}}
,
x_{i,\rm univ}\in\mathrm{Map}_{/\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda}}(\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda},X\times_{S}\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda})
,
\mathscr{L}_{i,\rm univ}\in\operatorname{Pic}(\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda})
.

Notice that if
n=1
, then
\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda}=\mathfrak{Z}
. By convention, we set
\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda}=\mathfrak{M}
if
n=0
.

Variant 8.9
.

In the situation of Construction
8.8
, we let
\widetilde{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\lambda}
be the functor which carries each map
\eta\colon T=\operatorname{Spec}R\to S
to the space of elements

\widetilde{\zeta}=(\zeta,\{\varphi_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq n})

where

\zeta=((\mathscr{F}_{0}\xrightarrow{\phi_{1}}\mathscr{F}_{1}\xrightarrow{\phi_{2}}\cdots\to\mathscr{F}_{n}),\{x_{i}\colon T\to X\times_{S}T\}_{1\leq i\leq n},\{\mathscr{L}_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq n})\in\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda}(\eta),

(\varphi_{i}\colon\mathscr{F}_{i}\to(x_{i})_{*}\mathscr{L}_{i})\in\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\operatorname{QCoh}(X\times_{S}T))^{\simeq},

and for which the following conditions are satisfied:

•

The sections satisfy
x_{i}=x_{j}
if
i\sim j
in
\lambda
;

•

The sequences
\mathscr{F}_{i-1}\xrightarrow{\phi_{i}}\mathscr{F}_{i}\xrightarrow{\varphi_{i}}(x_{i})_{*}\mathscr{L}_{i}
are fiber sequences for all
1\leq i\leq n
.

Then by virtue of
[
HA
, Remark 1.1.1.7]
, the natural forgetful map
\widetilde{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\lambda}(\eta)\to\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda}(\eta)
which carries
\widetilde{\zeta}=(\zeta,\{\varphi_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq n})
to
\zeta
is a trivial Kan fibration. Therefore, we obtain that the forgetful map
\widetilde{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\lambda}\to\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda}
is a natural isomorphism of functors. From now on, we will fix an inverse of the isomorphism
\widetilde{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\lambda}\to\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda}
. Unwinding the definitions, this means that we will fix a choice of fiber sequences of the universal quasi-coherent sheaves

\mathscr{U}_{i-1}\xrightarrow{\phi_{i,\rm univ}}\mathscr{U}_{i}\xrightarrow{\varphi_{i,\rm univ}}(x_{i,\rm univ})_{*}(\mathscr{L}_{i,\rm univ})

in
\operatorname{QCoh}(X\times_{S}\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda})
for all
1\leq i\leq n
.

We also consider the natural forgetful maps between the functors of the form
\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda}
:

Notation 8.10

(Compare with
[
Ne18
, §2.14]
)
.

In the situation of Construction
8.8
:

(1)

Let
\lambda
be a set partition of size
n\geq 1
. We let
\lambda|
denote the set partition of
\{1,\ldots,n-1\}
which is obtained by dropping the last element of
\lambda
, and let
|\lambda
denote the set partition of
\{2,\ldots,n\}
which is obtained by dropping the first element of
\lambda
.

(2)

We consider the following maps:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda|}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{Z}_{|\lambda},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{+}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{X,i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{-}}

where
p_{+}
,
p_{-}
, and
p_{X}
are the forgetful maps which carry each element

\zeta=((\mathscr{F}_{0}\xrightarrow{\phi_{1}}\mathscr{F}_{1}\xrightarrow{\phi_{2}}\cdots\xrightarrow{\phi_{n}}\mathscr{F}_{n}),\{x_{i}\colon T\to X\times_{S}T\}_{1\leq i\leq n},\{\mathscr{L}_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq n})\in\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda}(\eta),

where
\eta\colon T=\operatorname{Spec}R\to S
is any given map, to the elements

\displaystyle p_{+}(\zeta)

\displaystyle=((\mathscr{F}_{0}\xrightarrow{\phi_{1}}\mathscr{F}_{1}\to\cdots\xrightarrow{\phi_{n-1}}\mathscr{F}_{n-1}),\{x_{i}\colon T\to X\times_{S}T\}_{1\leq i\leq n-1},\{\mathscr{L}_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq n-1})\in\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda|}(\eta),

\displaystyle p_{-}(\zeta)

\displaystyle=((\mathscr{F}_{1}\xrightarrow{\phi_{2}}\mathscr{F}_{2}\to\cdots\xrightarrow{\phi_{n}}\mathscr{F}_{n}),\{x_{i}\colon T\to X\times_{S}T\}_{2\leq i\leq n},\{\mathscr{L}_{i}\}_{2\leq i\leq n})\in\mathfrak{Z}_{|\lambda}(\eta),

and the composition
p_{X,i}(\zeta)=(T\xrightarrow{x_{i}}T\times_{S}X\xrightarrow{\mathrm{pr}_{X}}X)\in X(\eta)
, respectively.

(3)

We define a forgetful map
\pi_{-}
with source
\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda}
by the following rules: we let

•

\pi_{-}=(p_{-}\times_{S}p_{X,1})\colon\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda}\to\mathfrak{Z}_{|\lambda}\times_{S}X
, if
1
is not equivalent to any
i\in\{2,\ldots,n\}
in
\lambda
.

•

\pi_{-}=p_{-}\colon\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda}\to\mathfrak{Z}_{|\lambda}
, if
1
is equivalent to some
i\in\{2,\ldots,n\}
in
\lambda
.

Similarly, we define a forgetful map
\pi_{+}
with source
\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda}
by the following rules: we let

•

\pi_{+}=(p_{+}\times_{S}p_{X,n})\colon\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda}\to\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda|}\times_{S}X
, if
n
is not equivalent to any
i\in\{1,\ldots,n-1\}
in
\lambda
.

•

\pi_{+}=p_{+}\colon\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda}\to\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda|}
, if
1
is equivalent to some
i\in\{1,\ldots,n-1\}
in
\lambda
.

The following result is a direct generalization of Proposition
8.5
:

Proposition 8.11

(Compare with
[
Ne18
, Proposition 2.16]
))
.

In the situation of Construction
8.8
and Notation
8.10
, and suppose that the map of prestacks
X\to S
is separated and a relative derived scheme.

(1)

If
1
is not equivalent to any
i\in\{2,\ldots,n\}
in
\lambda
, then the map
\pi_{-}\colon\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda}\to\mathfrak{Z}_{|\lambda}\times_{S}X
identifies
\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda}
with the derived projectivization
\mathbb{P}_{\mathfrak{Z}_{|\lambda}\times_{S}X}(\mathscr{U}_{1})
, and for which there is a canonical identification
\mathscr{O}(1)\simeq\mathscr{L}_{1,\rm univ}
.

(2)

If
1
is equivalent to some
i\in\{2,\ldots,n\}
in
\lambda
, then the map
\pi_{-}\colon\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda}\to\mathfrak{Z}_{|\lambda}
identifies
\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda}
with the derived projectivization
\mathbb{P}_{\mathfrak{Z}_{|\lambda}}(x_{i,\mathrm{univ}}^{*}(\mathscr{U}_{1}))
, and for which there is a canonical identification
\mathscr{O}(1)\simeq\mathscr{L}_{1,\rm univ}
.

Proof.

The assertion (
1
) can be proved in the same way as Proposition
8.5
. To prove assertion (
2
), assume that
1\sim i
in
\lambda
, where
2\leq i\leq n
, so that
x_{1}=x_{i}
, then for any
\eta\colon T=\operatorname{Spec}R\to S
, the fiber of
\pi_{-}
over a point

\zeta=((\mathscr{F}_{1}\xrightarrow{\phi_{2}}\cdots\xrightarrow{\phi_{n}}\mathscr{F}_{n}),\{x_{2}\colon T\to X\times_{S}T\}_{2\leq i\leq n},\{\mathscr{L}_{2}\}_{1\leq 2\leq n})\in\mathfrak{Z}_{|\lambda}(\eta)

is canonically identified with the space of pairs
(\mathscr{F}_{1}\to x_{1*}\,\mathscr{L}_{1},\mathscr{L}_{1}\in\operatorname{Pic}(T))
for which the map
\mathscr{F}_{1}\to x_{1*}\,\mathscr{L}_{1}
is surjective on
\pi_{0}
. By virtue of the adjunction

\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{\operatorname{QCoh}(X\times_{S}T)}(\mathscr{F}_{1},x_{1\,*}\mathscr{L}_{1})\simeq\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{\operatorname{QCoh}(T)}(x_{i}^{*}\mathscr{F}_{1},\mathscr{L}_{1})

and the same argument of the proof of Proposition
8.5
, the fiber of
\pi_{-}
over
\zeta
is canonically homotopy equivalent to
\mathbb{P}_{\mathfrak{Z}_{|\lambda}}(x_{i}^{*}\mathscr{U}_{1})(\eta)
, which proves the desired assertion.
∎

Next, we focus our attention to general “flags of one-point modifications” of quasi-coherent sheaves with a specified range of perfect-amplitude.

Notation 8.12
.

In the situation of Construction
8.8
and Notation
8.6
, we let
(\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]}
denote the subfunctor of
\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda}
such that, for each
\eta\colon T=\operatorname{Spec}R\to S
,
(\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]}(\eta)
is the full subcategory of
\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda}(\eta)
spanned by those elements

\zeta=((\mathscr{F}_{0}\xrightarrow{\phi_{1}}\mathscr{F}_{1}\xrightarrow{\phi_{2}}\cdots\xrightarrow{\phi_{n}}\mathscr{F}_{n}),\{x_{i}\colon T\to X\times_{S}T\}_{1\leq i\leq n},\{\mathscr{L}_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq n})\in\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda}(\eta)

for which
\mathscr{F}_{i}\in\mathfrak{M}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]}(\eta)=\mathrm{Perf}(X\times_{S}T)_{[0,d-1]}^{\simeq}
,
0\leq i\leq n
. By abuse of notation, we denote the universal element of
(\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]}
by the same notation as Construction
8.8
:

\zeta_{\rm univ}=((\mathscr{U}_{0}\xrightarrow{\phi_{1,\rm univ}}\mathscr{U}_{1}\xrightarrow{\phi_{2,\rm univ}}\cdots\xrightarrow{\phi_{n,\rm univ}}\mathscr{U}_{n}),\{x_{i,\rm univ}\}_{1\leq i\leq n},\{\mathscr{L}_{i,\rm univ}\}_{1\leq i\leq n}),

where
\mathscr{U}_{i}\in\mathrm{Perf}(X\times_{S}(\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]})_{[0,d-1]}
,
x_{i,\rm univ}
are sections of the projection
X\times_{S}(\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]}\to(\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]}
,
\mathscr{L}_{i,\rm univ}\in\operatorname{Pic}((\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]})
. Then there are fiber sequences on
(\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]}\times_{S}X
:

\mathscr{U}_{i-1}\xrightarrow{\phi_{i,\rm univ}}\mathscr{U}_{i}\xrightarrow{\varphi_{i,\rm univ}}(x_{i,\rm univ})_{*}\mathscr{L}_{i,\rm univ}.

The following is a generalization of Proposition
8.7
and
[
Ne18
, Proposition 2.16]
:

Proposition 8.13
.

Suppose that
X\to S
is map of prestacks which is smooth, separated, and a relative derived scheme of constant relative dimension
d\geq 1
. We let
\mathscr{K}_{X/S}
denote the relative canonical line bundle of
X\to S
and let
\lambda
be a set partition of size
n\geq 2
. Consider the moduli functors and maps defined in Notations
8.10
and
8.12
. Then:

(1)

The restriction map
\pi_{-}|_{(\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]}}
is a derived projectivization of a quasi-coherent sheaf of perfect-amplitude contained in
[0,d-1]
. More concretely:

(i)

If
1
is not equivalent to any element
i\in\{2,\ldots,n\}
in
\lambda
, then the restriction

\pi_{-}|_{(\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]}}\colon(\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]}\to(\mathfrak{Z}_{|\lambda})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]}\times_{S}X

identifies
(\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]}
with the derived projectivization

\mathbb{P}_{(\mathfrak{Z}_{|\lambda})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]}\times_{S}X}(\mathscr{U}_{1})
, and for which there is a canonical identification
\mathscr{O}(1)\simeq\mathscr{L}_{1,\rm univ}
.

(ii)

If
1
is equivalent to some
i\in\{2,\ldots,n\}
in
\lambda
, then the restriction

\pi_{-}|_{(\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]}}\colon(\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]}\to(\mathfrak{Z}_{|\lambda})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]}

identifies
(\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]}
with the derived projectivization

\mathbb{P}_{(\mathfrak{Z}_{|\lambda})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]}}(x_{i,\mathrm{univ}}^{*}(\mathscr{U}_{1}))
, and for which there is a canonical identification
\mathscr{O}(1)\simeq\mathscr{L}_{1,\rm univ}
.

(2)

The restriction map
\pi_{+}|_{\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]}}
is a derived projectivization of a quasi-coherent sheaf of perfect-amplitude contained in
[0,d-1]
. More concretely:

(i)

If
1
is not equivalent to any element
i\in\{2,\ldots,n\}
in
\lambda
, then the restriction

\pi_{+}|_{(\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]}}\colon(\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]}\to(\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda|})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]}\times_{S}X

identifies
(\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]}
with the derived projectivization

\mathbb{P}_{(\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda|})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]}\times_{S}X}(\Sigma^{d-1}\,\mathscr{U}_{1}^{\vee}\otimes\mathscr{K}_{X/S})
,
and for which there is a canonical identification
\mathscr{O}(1)\simeq\mathscr{L}_{n,\rm univ}^{\vee}
;

(ii)

If
1
is equivalent to some
i\in\{2,\ldots,n\}
in
\lambda
, then the restriction

\pi_{+}|_{(\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]}}\colon(\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]}\to(\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda|})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]}

identifies
(\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]}
with the derived projectivization

\mathbb{P}_{(\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda|})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,d-1]}}(\Sigma^{d-1}x_{i,\mathrm{univ}}^{*}(\mathscr{U}_{1}^{\vee})\otimes\mathscr{K}_{X/S})
,
and for which there is a canonical identification
\mathscr{O}(1)\simeq\mathscr{L}_{n,\rm univ}^{\vee}
.

Proof.

The assertions (
1i
) and (
2i
) follow from the same argument of Proposition
8.7
; The assertions (
1ii
) and (
2ii
) are proved similarly, in view of the proof of Proposition
8.11
(
2
).
∎

8.3.
Hecke correspondences for surfaces

In the situation of Proposition
8.13
, if we set
d=2
, that is, if
X\to S
is a family of smooth, separated derived surfaces, then both the projections
\pi_{\pm}|_{(\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1]}}
are derived projectivizations of sheaves of perfect-amplitude contained in
[0,1]
. In particular, we could apply the generalized Serre’s Theorem
5.6
, the results on Beilinson’s relations (Proposition
6.19
, Corollary
6.21
), and the projectivization formula (Theorem
7.5
) to these situations. For simplicity of exposition, we only include here part of all these results for the “one-step flags”
\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1]}
; The generalizations of these results to the “general flags”
(\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1]}
are straightforward, by virtue of Proposition
8.13
.

Notation 8.14
.

Let
X\to S
be map of prestacks which is smooth, separated, and a relative derived surface, with relative canonical line bundle
\mathscr{K}_{X/S}=\bigwedge^{2}L_{X/S}
, where
L_{X/S}
is the relative cotangent complex (Definition
4.28
). Let the functors
\mathfrak{Z}
,
\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1]}
,
\mathfrak{M}
,
\mathfrak{M}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1]}
,
\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda}
,
\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1]}
be defined as in Constructions
8.4
and
8.8
, and Notations
8.6
and
8.12
.

(1)

We let
\mathfrak{Z}_{2}^{\bullet}
(resp.
(\mathfrak{Z}_{2}^{\bullet})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1]}
) denote the prestack
\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda}
(resp.
(\mathfrak{Z}_{\lambda})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1]}
) in the case where
\lambda
is the set partition of
\{1,2\}
for which
1\sim 2
. We let

((\mathscr{U}_{0}\xrightarrow{\phi_{1,\rm univ}}\mathscr{U}_{1}\xrightarrow{\phi_{2,\rm univ}}\mathscr{U}_{2});\,x_{\rm univ};\,(\mathscr{L}_{1,\rm univ},\mathscr{L}_{2,\rm univ}))

denote the universal element of
(\mathfrak{Z}_{2}^{\bullet})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1]}
, where
\mathscr{U}_{i}\in\mathrm{Perf}(X\times_{S}(\mathfrak{Z}_{2}^{\bullet})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1]})_{[0,1]}
,
x_{\rm univ}\colon(\mathfrak{Z}_{2}^{\bullet})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1]}\to X\times_{S}(\mathfrak{Z}_{2}^{\bullet})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1]}
is a section of the natural projection
X\times_{S}(\mathfrak{Z}_{2}^{\bullet})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1]}\to(\mathfrak{Z}_{2}^{\bullet})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1]}
, and
\mathscr{L}_{i,\rm univ}\in\operatorname{Pic}((\mathfrak{Z}_{2}^{\bullet})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1]})
are line bundles, and for which there are universal fiber sequences of quasi-coherent sheaves on
(\mathfrak{Z}_{2}^{\bullet})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1]}\times_{S}X
:

\mathscr{U}_{0}\xrightarrow{\phi_{1,\rm univ}}\mathscr{U}_{1}\to(x_{\rm univ})_{*}\mathscr{L}_{1,\rm univ},\qquad\mathscr{U}_{1}\xrightarrow{\phi_{2,\rm univ}}\mathscr{U}_{2}\to(x_{\rm univ})_{*}\mathscr{L}_{2,\rm univ}.

These fiber sequences induce canonical maps

\rho_{1,\rm univ}\colon x_{\rm univ}^{*}(\mathscr{U}_{1})\to\mathscr{L}_{1,\rm univ},\qquad\rho_{2,\rm univ}\colon\Sigma\,x_{\rm univ}^{*}(\mathscr{U}_{1}^{\vee})\to\mathscr{L}_{2,\rm univ}^{\vee}\otimes\mathscr{K}_{X/S}^{\vee}.

(2)

We let
(\widehat{\mathfrak{Z}}_{2}^{\bullet})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1]}
denote the universal incidence locus (Notation
7.4
) for the derived projectivizations
\pi_{-}|\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1]}
and
\pi_{+}|\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1]}
of Proposition
8.13
over
\mathfrak{M}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1]}\times_{S}X
. Unwinding the definitions,
(\widehat{\mathfrak{Z}}_{2}^{\bullet})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1]}
is the closed subfunctor of
(\mathfrak{Z}_{2}^{\bullet})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1]}
defined by the pullback square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\widehat{\mathfrak{Z}}_{2}^{\bullet})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathfrak{Z}_{2}^{\bullet})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathfrak{Z}_{2}^{\bullet})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{V}_{(\mathfrak{Z}_{2}^{\bullet})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1]}}(\Sigma\,(\mathscr{L}_{1,\rm univ}^{\vee}\boxtimes\mathscr{L}_{2,\rm univ})\otimes\mathscr{K}_{X/S}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\mathbf{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\mathbf{0}}}

Here,
i_{\mathbf{0}}
is the inclusion of the zero section, and
i_{\mathbf{1}}
is the section of the natural projection
\mathbb{V}_{(\mathfrak{Z}_{2}^{\bullet})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1]}}(\Sigma(\mathscr{L}_{1,\rm univ}^{\vee}\boxtimes\mathscr{L}_{2,\rm univ})\otimes\mathscr{K}_{X/S})\to(\mathfrak{Z}_{2}^{\bullet})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1]}
that classifies the composite map

\Sigma\,\mathscr{L}_{1,\rm univ}^{\vee}\boxtimes(\mathscr{L}_{2,\rm univ}\otimes\mathscr{K}_{X/S})\xrightarrow{\Sigma\,\rho_{1,\rm univ}^{\vee}\boxtimes\rho_{2,\rm univ}^{\vee}}x_{\rm univ}^{*}(\mathscr{U}_{1})\otimes x_{\rm univ}^{*}(\mathscr{U}_{1}^{\vee})\xrightarrow{\rm ev}\mathscr{O}_{(\mathfrak{Z}_{2}^{\bullet})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1]}}.

(3)

Let
\delta
be an integer. For a prestack
Y
, we let
\mathrm{Perf}(Y)_{[0,1],\operatorname{rk}=\delta}
denote the full subcategory of
\mathrm{Perf}(Y)
spanned by perfect objects of Tor-amplitude in
[0,1]
and rank
\delta
. Similarly, we let
\mathfrak{M}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1],\operatorname{rk}=\delta}
,
\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1],\operatorname{rk}=\delta}
,
(\mathfrak{Z}_{2}^{\bullet})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1],\operatorname{rk}=\delta}
and
(\widehat{\mathfrak{Z}}_{2}^{\bullet})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1],\operatorname{rk}=\delta}
denote the subfunctors of
\mathfrak{M}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1]}
,
\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1]}
,
(\mathfrak{Z}_{2}^{\bullet})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1]}
and
(\widehat{\mathfrak{Z}}_{2}^{\bullet})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1]}
such that their spaces of
T
-point over a map
\eta\colon T\to S
are respectively spanned by those elements for which the components
\mathscr{F}_{i}
all have rank
\delta
, that is,
\mathscr{F}_{i}\in\mathrm{Perf}(X\times_{S}T)_{[0,1],\operatorname{rk}=\delta}
. (These notations are justified by the following fact: if
x
is a quasi-smooth closed immersion and
\mathscr{L}
is a line bundle, then
x_{*}\mathscr{L}
is perfect of rank
0
. Therefore, if a pair of perfect quasi-coherent sheaves
(\mathscr{F}_{i-1},\mathscr{F}_{i})
fits into a fiber sequence
\mathscr{F}_{i-1}\to\mathscr{F}_{i}\to x_{*}\mathscr{L}
, then
\operatorname{rk}\mathscr{F}_{i-1}=\operatorname{rk}\mathscr{F}_{i}
.)

(4)

In particular, for each
\delta\geq 0
, we have a pullback square and a commutative square:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathfrak{Z}_{2}^{\bullet})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1],\operatorname{rk}=\delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1],\operatorname{rk}=\delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1],\operatorname{rk}=\delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{M}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1],\operatorname{rk}=\delta}\times_{S}X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r_{+}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r_{-}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{pr}_{-}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{pr}_{+}}

and

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\widehat{\mathfrak{Z}}_{2}^{\bullet})^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1],\operatorname{rk}=\delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1],\operatorname{rk}=\delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1],\operatorname{rk}=\delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{M}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1],\operatorname{rk}=\delta}\times_{S}X,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r_{+}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r_{-}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{pr}_{-}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{pr}_{+}}

respectively, where
\mathrm{pr}_{+}=\pi_{-}|\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1],\operatorname{rk}=\delta}
and
\mathrm{pr}_{-}=\pi_{+}|\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1],\operatorname{rk}=\delta}
are the forgetful maps (notice the change of signs due to the different conventions) and the maps
r_{\pm}
are given by the compositions
r_{\pm}^{\prime}\circ\iota
.

Corollary 8.15
.

Let
X\to S
be map of prestacks which is smooth, separated, and a relative derived surface, with relative canonical line bundle
\mathscr{K}_{X/S}
. Consider the moduli functors and the maps between them defined in Notation
8.14
. For simplicity of expressions, we introduce the following notations:

\mathfrak{X}:=\mathfrak{M}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1],\operatorname{rk}=\delta}\times_{S}X,\qquad\mathscr{W}:=\Sigma\,\mathscr{U}^{\vee}\otimes\mathscr{K}_{X/S}\in\mathrm{Perf}(\mathfrak{X}).

In particular,
\mathscr{L}_{\rm univ}
denotes the universal line bundle on
\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1],\operatorname{rk}=\delta}
,
\mathscr{U}\in\mathrm{Perf}(\mathfrak{X})
denotes the universal perfect object over
\mathfrak{X}=\mathfrak{M}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1],\operatorname{rk}=\delta}\times_{S}X
, and the natural projections

\mathrm{pr}_{\pm}=\pi_{\mp}|_{\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1],\operatorname{rk}=\delta}}\colon\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1],\operatorname{rk}=\delta}\to\mathfrak{X}

are the maps which, for any given
\eta\colon T=\operatorname{Spec}R\to S
,
R\in\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}
, carry the element

\zeta=((\mathscr{F}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\phi}\mathscr{F});\,x\colon T\to X\times_{S}T;\,\mathscr{L}\in\operatorname{Pic}(T))\in\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1],\operatorname{rk}=\delta}(\eta)

to the respective elements

\displaystyle\mathrm{pr}_{+}(\zeta)

\displaystyle=(\mathscr{F},(T\xrightarrow{x}X\times_{S}T\xrightarrow{\mathrm{pr}_{X}}X))\in\mathfrak{X}(\eta),

\displaystyle\mathrm{pr}_{-}(\zeta)

\displaystyle=(\mathscr{F}^{\prime},(T\xrightarrow{x}X\times_{S}T\xrightarrow{\mathrm{pr}_{X}}X))\in\mathfrak{X}(\eta).

(1)

If the rank
\delta\geq 1
. Then:

(1i)

For any
d\in\mathbb{Z}
, there are canonical equivalences

(\mathrm{pr}_{+})_{*}(\mathscr{L}_{\rm univ}^{\otimes d})\simeq\begin{cases}\operatorname{Sym}_{\mathfrak{X}}^{d}(\mathscr{U})&\text{if~}d\geq 0;\\
0&\text{if ~}-\delta+1\leq d\leq-1;\\
\Sigma^{1-\delta}\big(\operatorname{Sym}_{\mathfrak{X}}^{-d-\delta}(\mathscr{U})\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\mathfrak{X}}}\det\mathscr{U}\big)^{\vee}&\text{if~}d\leq-\delta\end{cases}

and canonical fiber sequences

\displaystyle\operatorname{Sym}_{\mathfrak{X}}^{d}(\mathscr{W})\to(\mathrm{pr}_{-})_{*}(\mathscr{L}_{\rm univ}^{\otimes-d})\to\Sigma^{1+\delta}\big(\operatorname{Sym}_{\mathfrak{X}}^{\delta-d}(\mathscr{W})\otimes\det\mathscr{W}\big)^{\vee}.

(1ii)

For any perfect stack
T
(see Definition
6.3
; for example, for any quasi-compact, quasi-separated derived scheme
T
) and any morphism
T\to\mathfrak{X}
, we let
\mathrm{pr}_{+,T}\colon\mathfrak{Z}_{T}\to T
denote the base-change of
\mathrm{pr}_{+}\colon\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1],\operatorname{rk}=\delta}\to\mathfrak{X}
along
T\to\mathfrak{X}
, let
\mathscr{L}_{T}
denote the base-change of
\mathscr{L}_{\rm univ}
, and let
\mathscr{F}_{T}
,
x_{T}
be similarly defined. Then the sequence of line bundles
\mathscr{O}_{\mathfrak{Z}_{T}}=\mathscr{L}_{T}^{0},\mathscr{L}_{T},\cdots,\mathscr{L}_{T}^{\otimes\delta-1}
is a relative exceptional sequence on
\mathfrak{Z}_{T}
over
T
, whose left dual relative exceptional sequence is given by

\Sigma^{\delta-1}\bigwedge^{\delta-1}(L_{\mathrm{pr}_{+,T}}\otimes\mathscr{L}_{T}),\cdots,\Sigma(L_{\mathrm{pr}_{+,T}}\otimes\mathscr{L}_{T}),\mathscr{O}_{\mathfrak{Z}_{T}}
. (Notice that, by virtue of Euler’s fiber sequence Theorem
4.27
,
L_{\mathrm{pr}_{+,T}}\otimes\mathscr{L}_{T}
is canonically equivalent to
\operatorname{fib}(x_{T}^{*}(\mathscr{F}_{T})\to\mathscr{L}_{T})
.) Moreover, these two relative exceptional sequences satisfy Beilinson’s relations Corollary
6.21
.

(1iii)

The functors

\Phi=r_{+\,*}\,r_{-}^{*}\colon\operatorname{QCoh}(\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1],\operatorname{rk}=\delta})\to\operatorname{QCoh}(\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1],\operatorname{rk}=\delta})

and the functors

\Psi_{i}=\mathrm{pr}_{+}^{*}(\underline{\hphantom{A}})\otimes\mathscr{L}_{\rm univ}^{\otimes i}\colon\operatorname{QCoh}(\mathfrak{X})\to\operatorname{QCoh}(\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1],\operatorname{rk}=\delta}),

for all
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, and their restrictions to the subcategories of perfect objects, are all fully faithful. Furthermore, there are an induced semiorthogonal decompositions

\displaystyle\operatorname{QCoh}(\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1],\operatorname{rk}=\delta})

\displaystyle=\big\langle\Phi(\operatorname{QCoh}(\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1],\operatorname{rk}=\delta})),~\Psi_{1}(\operatorname{QCoh}(\mathfrak{X})),\cdots,\Psi_{\delta}(\operatorname{QCoh}(\mathfrak{X}))\big\rangle.

\displaystyle\mathrm{Perf}(\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1],\operatorname{rk}=\delta})

\displaystyle=\big\langle\Phi(\mathrm{Perf}(\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1],\operatorname{rk}=\delta})),~\Psi_{1}(\mathrm{Perf}(\mathfrak{X})),\cdots,\Psi_{\delta}(\mathrm{Perf}(\mathfrak{X}))\big\rangle.

(2)

If the rank
\delta=0
, then for each
d\in\mathbb{Z}
, there are canonical equivalences

\displaystyle(\mathrm{pr}_{+})_{*}(\mathscr{L}_{\rm univ}^{\otimes d})

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{cofib}\Big(\big(\operatorname{Sym}_{\mathfrak{X}}^{-d}(\mathscr{U})\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\mathfrak{X}}}\det\mathscr{U}\big)^{\vee}\to\operatorname{Sym}_{\mathfrak{X}}^{d}(\mathscr{U})\Big).

\displaystyle(\mathrm{pr}_{-})_{*}(\mathscr{L}_{\rm univ}^{\otimes-d})

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{cofib}\Big(\big(\operatorname{Sym}_{\mathfrak{X}}^{-d}(\mathscr{W})\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\mathfrak{X}}}\det\mathscr{W})^{\vee}\to\operatorname{Sym}_{\mathfrak{X}}^{d}(\mathscr{W})\Big).

Moreover, the functors

r_{+\,*}\,r_{-}^{*}\colon\operatorname{QCoh}(\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1],\operatorname{rk}=0})\to\operatorname{QCoh}(\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1],\operatorname{rk}=0})\qquad r_{+\,*}\,r_{-}^{*}\colon\mathrm{Perf}(\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1],\operatorname{rk}=0})\to\mathrm{Perf}(\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1],\operatorname{rk}=0})

r_{-\,*}\,r_{+}^{*}\colon\operatorname{QCoh}(\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1],\operatorname{rk}=0})\to\operatorname{QCoh}(\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1],\operatorname{rk}=0})\qquad r_{-\,*}\,r_{+}^{*}\colon\mathrm{Perf}(\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1],\operatorname{rk}=0})\to\mathrm{Perf}(\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1],\operatorname{rk}=0})

are exact equivalences of stable
\infty
-categories.

(3)

If the rank
\delta\leq-1
. Then:

(3i)

For all
d\in\mathbb{Z}
, there are canonical equivalences

(\mathrm{pr}_{-})_{*}(\mathscr{L}_{\rm univ}^{\otimes-d})\simeq\begin{cases}\operatorname{Sym}_{\mathfrak{X}}^{d}(\mathscr{W})&\text{if~}d\geq 0;\\
0&\text{if ~}\delta+1\leq d\leq-1;\\
\Sigma^{1+\delta}\big(\operatorname{Sym}_{\mathfrak{X}}^{-d+\delta}(\mathscr{W})\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\mathfrak{X}}}\det\mathscr{W}\big)^{\vee}&\text{if~}d\leq\delta,\end{cases}

and canonical fiber sequences

\displaystyle\operatorname{Sym}_{\mathfrak{X}}^{d}(\mathscr{U})\to(\mathrm{pr}_{+})_{*}(\mathscr{L}_{\rm univ}^{\otimes d})\to\Sigma^{1-\delta}\big(\operatorname{Sym}_{\mathfrak{X}}^{-\delta-d}(\mathscr{U})\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\mathfrak{X}}}\det\mathscr{U}\big)^{\vee}.

(3ii)

For any perfect stack
T
(for example, for any quasi-compact, quasi-separated derived scheme
T
) and any morphism
T\to\mathfrak{X}
, we let
\mathrm{pr}_{-,T}\colon\mathfrak{Z}_{T}\to T
denote the base-change of
\mathrm{pr}_{-}\colon\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1],\operatorname{rk}=\delta}\to\mathfrak{X}
along
T\to\mathfrak{X}
, let
\mathscr{L}_{T}
denote the base-change of
\mathscr{L}_{\rm univ}
, and let
\mathscr{F}^{\prime}_{T}
,
x_{T}
be similarly defined.
Then the sequence of line bundles
\mathscr{O}_{\mathfrak{Z}_{T}}=\mathscr{L}_{T}^{0},\mathscr{L}_{T}^{\vee},\cdots,\mathscr{L}_{T}^{\otimes-\delta-1}
is a relative exceptional sequence on
\mathfrak{Z}_{T}
over
T
, whose left dual relative exceptional sequence is given by

\Sigma^{\delta-1}\bigwedge^{\delta-1}(L_{\mathrm{pr}_{-,T}}\otimes\mathscr{L}_{T}^{\vee}),\cdots,\Sigma(L_{\mathrm{pr}_{-,T}}\otimes\mathscr{L}_{T}^{\vee}),\mathscr{O}_{\mathfrak{Z}_{T}}
.
(Notice that, by virtue of Euler fiber sequence Theorem
4.27
,
L_{\mathrm{pr}_{-,T}}\otimes\mathscr{L}_{T}^{\vee}
is canonically equivalent to
\operatorname{fib}(x_{T}^{*}(\Sigma\,\mathscr{F}_{T}^{\prime\vee}\otimes\mathscr{K}_{X_{T}/T})\to\mathscr{L}_{T}^{\vee})
.) Moreover, these two relative exceptional sequences satisfy Beilinson’s relations Corollary
6.21
.

(3iii)

The functors

\Phi^{\prime}=r_{-\,*}\,r_{+}^{*}\colon\operatorname{QCoh}(\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1],\operatorname{rk}=\delta})\to\operatorname{QCoh}(\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1],\operatorname{rk}=\delta})

and the functor

\Psi_{i}^{\prime}=\mathrm{pr}_{-}^{*}(\underline{\hphantom{A}})\otimes\mathscr{L}_{\rm univ}^{\otimes-i}\colon\operatorname{QCoh}(\mathfrak{X})\to\operatorname{QCoh}(\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1],\operatorname{rk}=\delta}),

for all
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, and their restrictions to the subcategories of perfect objects, are all fully faithful. Furthermore, there are an induced semiorthogonal decompositions

\displaystyle\operatorname{QCoh}(\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1],\operatorname{rk}=\delta})

\displaystyle=\big\langle\Phi^{\prime}(\operatorname{QCoh}(\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1],\operatorname{rk}=\delta})),~\Psi_{1}^{\prime}(\operatorname{QCoh}(\mathfrak{X})),\cdots,\Psi_{-\delta}^{\prime}(\operatorname{QCoh}(\mathfrak{X}))\big\rangle.

\displaystyle\mathrm{Perf}(\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1],\operatorname{rk}=\delta})

\displaystyle=\big\langle\Phi^{\prime}(\mathrm{Perf}(\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{[0,1],\operatorname{rk}=\delta})),~\Psi_{1}^{\prime}(\mathrm{Perf}(\mathfrak{X})),\cdots,\Psi_{-\delta}^{\prime}(\mathrm{Perf}(\mathfrak{X}))\big\rangle.

Proof.

In view of Proposition
8.7
, the desired results follow from the Generalized Serre’s Theorem
5.6
, the results on Beilinson’s relations (Proposition
6.19
, Corollary
6.21
), and the projectivization formula (Theorem
7.5
).
∎

Remark 8.16
.

In the situation of assertion
2
, the same argument of
[
JL18
, Theorem 4.1]
shows that the equivalence functors are non-trivial, and the composition of equivalences
(r_{-,*}\,r_{+}^{*})\circ(r_{+,*}\,r_{-}^{*})
is a spherical twist in the sense of
[
ST
,
AL
]
.

Remark 8.17
.

As a consequence, we could “decategorify” all the formulae of Corollary
8.15
and obtain the corresponding formulae on the level of any additive invariant
\mathbb{E}
in the sense of
[
BGT
, Definition 6.1]
; see Remark
7.7
. For example, we could take
\mathbb{E}
to be algebraic K-theory, topological Hochschild homology, or topological cyclic homology.

Appendix A
Simplicial Models for Symmetric and Koszul Algebras

In this appendix, we provide the concrete simplicial models for symmetric algebras and Koszul algebras, especially in the case of two-term complexes of locally free modules. These constructions are useful for computations in concrete examples. (However, they do not play a role in the proofs of this paper, so we might delete them in the final version.)

A.1.
Dold–Puppe and Quillen’s construction

In this subsection, we show that Lurie’s framework of non-abelian derived functors recovers the construction of Dold–Puppe
[
DP
]
and Quillen
[
Qu
]
using simplicial resolutions.
Let
R
be an ordinary commutative ring, and suppose that
F\colon\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\mathrm{cn}}\to\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\mathrm{cn}}
is a functor which preserves sifted colimits and projective
R
-modules (for example, if
F
is one of the functors
\operatorname{Sym}^{n}_{R}
,
\bigwedge^{n}_{R}
, or
\Gamma_{R}^{n}
of Construction
2.10
). We let
f=F|\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\mathrm{proj}}
be the restriction of
F
to projective
R
-modules. From the canonical equivalence
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\mathrm{cn}}\simeq\operatorname{N}_{\bullet}^{\rm hc}(\mathrm{Fun}(\operatorname{{\bf\Delta}},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\heartsuit})^{\circ})
, we obtain that each
R
-module
M\in\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\mathrm{cn}}
corresponds to a simplicial
R
-module
P_{\bullet}
for which each
P_{i}
is a projective
R
-module. We could regard
P_{\bullet}
as a simplicial object of
\operatorname{Mod}^{\mathrm{cn}}_{R}
via the map
\operatorname{{\bf\Delta}}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\mathrm{proj}}\subseteq\operatorname{Mod}^{\mathrm{cn}}_{R}
. By virtue of
[
HTT
, Theorem 4.2.4.1]
and
[
HTT
, Proposition 5.5.9.14]
, we can identify
M
with the geometric realization
|P_{\bullet}|
of
P_{\bullet}
(in other words,
P_{\bullet}
is a simplicial resolution of
M
). Since
F
preserves sifted colimits, we obtain canonical equivalences

F(M)\simeq F(|P_{\bullet}|)\simeq|f(P_{\bullet})|,

where
f(P_{\bullet})
is the simplicial object obtained by applying
f
level-wisely to
P_{\bullet}
, that is,

f(P_{\bullet})\colon\operatorname{{\bf\Delta}}^{\mathrm{op}}\xrightarrow{P_{\bullet}}\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\mathrm{proj}}\xrightarrow{f=F|\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\mathrm{proj}}}\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\mathrm{proj}}\subseteq\operatorname{Mod}^{\mathrm{cn}}_{R}.

Similarly, the symmetric algebra functor
\operatorname{Sym}_{R}^{*}\colon\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\mathrm{cn}}\to\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}_{R}
could also be computed by choosing a simplicial resolution
P_{\bullet}
, where
P_{n}
are projective
R
-modules. We could summarize the above discussion by saying that Lurie’s construction of non-abelian derived functors extends the construction of Dold–Puppe
[
DP
]
and Quillen
[
Qu
]
using simplicial resolutions.

A.2.
Simplicial symmetric algebras

By virtue of the preceding subsection, in the case where
R
is a discrete commutative ring and
N\in\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\mathrm{cn}}
is a connective
R
-module which is represented by a complex
P_{*}
of discrete projective
R
-modules, we could compute the derived symmetric algebra
\operatorname{Sym}_{R}^{*}(N)
by the Dold–Kan construction
{\rm DK}_{\bullet}(P_{*})
(
[
HA
, Construction 1.2.3.5]
). In this subsection, we spell out this construction in the case where
N
is represented by a cofiber of a map
\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M
between finite locally free
R
-modules.

Construction A.1
.

Let
R
be a discrete commutative ring, and let
\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M
be a morphism of finitely generated locally free modules. The
simplicial symmetric algebras of
\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M
over
R
is a simplicial object of discrete commutative rings

\operatorname{Sym}_{\Delta}^{*}(R,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M)\in\mathrm{Fun}(\operatorname{{\bf\Delta}}^{\mathrm{op}},\operatorname{CAlg}_{R}^{\heartsuit})

defined as follows: The terms of
\operatorname{Sym}_{\Delta}^{*}(R,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M)
are given by for all
n\geq 0
,

(\operatorname{Sym}_{\Delta}^{*}(R,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M))_{n}=S^{*}_{R}(M)\otimes S_{R}^{*}(M^{\prime})^{\otimes n};

The face maps
d_{i}\colon(\operatorname{Sym}_{\Delta}^{*}(R,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M))_{n}\to(\operatorname{Sym}_{\Delta}^{*}(R,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M))_{n-1}
,
0\leq i\leq n
, and the degeneracy maps
s_{i}\colon(\operatorname{Sym}_{\Delta}^{*}(R,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M))_{n}\to(\operatorname{Sym}_{\Delta}^{*}(R,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M))_{n+1}
,
0\leq i\leq n
, are respectively given by the formulae: for
f\in S_{R}^{*}(M)
and
g_{i}\in S_{R}^{*}(M^{\prime})
,

\displaystyle d_{i}(f\otimes g_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes g_{n})=\begin{cases}(f\cdot(S_{R}^{*}(\rho)(g_{1})))\otimes g_{2}\otimes\cdots\otimes g_{n}&\text{if~}i=0;\\
f\otimes g_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes g_{i-1}\otimes(g_{i}\cdot g_{i+1})\otimes g_{i+1}\otimes\cdots\otimes g_{n}&\text{if~}1\leq i\leq n-1;\\
(f\cdot(S_{R}^{*}(0)(g_{n})))\otimes g_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes g_{n-1}&\text{if~}i=n.\end{cases}

\displaystyle s_{i}(f\otimes g_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes g_{n})=f\otimes g_{1}\otimes\cdots g_{i}\otimes 1\otimes g_{i+1}\otimes\cdots\otimes g_{n}.

Here
S_{R}^{*}(\rho),S_{R}^{*}(0)\colon S_{R}^{*}(M^{\prime})\to S_{R}^{*}(M)
denote the canonical morphisms of algebras induced by the morphisms
\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M
and
0\colon M^{\prime}\to M
, respectively. Notice
S_{R}^{*}(0)
by definition factorizes as a composition
S_{R}^{*}(M^{\prime})\twoheadrightarrow R\hookrightarrow S_{R}^{*}(M)
. There is a natural grading of
\operatorname{Sym}_{\Delta}^{*}(R,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M)
,

\operatorname{Sym}_{\Delta}^{*}(R,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M)=\bigoplus_{d\geq 0}\operatorname{Sym}_{\Delta}^{d}(R,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M)

where for each
d\geq 0
,
\operatorname{Sym}_{\Delta}^{d}(R,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M)
is the simplicial module that has terms

(\operatorname{Sym}_{\Delta}^{d}(R,\rho\colon M^{\prime}\to M))_{n}=\bigoplus_{d_{0}+d_{1}+\cdots+d_{n}=d,d_{i}\geq 0}S_{R}^{d_{0}}M\otimes_{R}S_{R}^{d_{1}}M^{\prime}\otimes_{R}\cdots\otimes_{R}S_{R}^{d_{n}}M^{\prime}.

The face maps and degeneracy maps are given by the same formulae.

A.3.
Simplicial Koszul algebras

The concrete models of the preceding subsection provides simplicial models for derived zero loci of a cosection. Let
R
be a discrete commutative ring, and let
\rho\colon M\to R
be a cosection of a connective
R
-module
M
. By virtue of Remark
4.15
and §
A.1
, we could compute the simplicial commutative ring
R\otimes_{{\rm ev}_{0},\operatorname{Sym}_{R}^{*}(M),{\rm ev}_{\rho}}R
