(whose spectrum is the derived zero locus of
\rho
) using either the simplicial model
\operatorname{Sym}_{\Delta}^{*}(R,\operatorname{id}_{M})\otimes_{\operatorname{Sym}_{\Delta}^{*}(M),{\rm ev}_{\rho}}R
or the simplicial model
\operatorname{Sym}_{\Delta}^{*}(R,\rho\colon M\to R)\otimes_{R[T],T\mapsto 1}R
.

In this subsection, we spell out the above simplicial model for
R\otimes_{{\rm ev}_{0},\operatorname{Sym}_{R}^{*}(M),{\rm ev}_{\rho}}R
in the case where
M
is a finite free
R
-module. Let
R
be a discrete commutative ring, and let
\mathbf{r}=(r_{1},\cdots,r_{m})
be a sequence of elements
r_{i}\in R
, which we may regard as a section
\mathbf{r}\colon R\to M^{\vee}
, where
M=R^{m}
, or dually, a cosection map
\mathbf{r}^{\vee}\colon M=R^{m}\to R
. Therefore, by adjunction, the cosection maps
\mathbf{r}^{\vee}
and
\mathbf{0}^{\vee}
induce maps of discrete commutative algebras
\mathrm{ev}_{\mathbf{r}}\colon S_{R}^{*}(M)\to R
and
\mathrm{ev}_{\mathbf{0}}\colon S_{R}^{*}(M)\to R
, respectively.

Construction A.2
.

Let
R
be a discrete commutative ring, and let
\mathbf{r}=(r_{1},\cdots,r_{m})
be a sequence of elements
r_{i}\in R
. Then
simplicial Koszul algebras of the sequence
\mathbf{r}
, which we shall denote by
\mathrm{Kos}_{\Delta}^{*}(R,\mathbf{r})
, is the following simplicial object of the ordinary category of discrete commutative rings

\mathrm{Kos}_{\Delta}^{*}(R,\mathbf{r})=\operatorname{Sym}_{\Delta}^{*}(R,\operatorname{id}_{M}\colon M\to M)\otimes_{S_{R}^{*}(M)}R\in\mathrm{Fun}(\operatorname{{\bf\Delta}}^{\mathrm{op}},\operatorname{CAlg}_{R}^{\heartsuit}).

Here, the
S_{R}^{*}(M)
-algebra structure of
R
is given by the map
\mathrm{ev}_{\mathbf{r}}\colon S_{R}^{*}(M)\to R
. Unwinding the definition, the simplicial Koszul algebra takes the form:

\mathrm{Kos}_{\Delta}^{*}(R,\mathbf{r})_{n}=S^{*}_{R}(M)^{\otimes n}=S_{R}^{*}(M)\otimes_{R}S_{R}^{*}(M)\otimes_{R}\cdots\otimes_{R}S_{R}^{*}(M).

The face maps

d_{i}\colon S^{*}_{R}(M)^{\otimes n}\to S^{*}_{R}(M)^{\otimes(n-1)}

and the degeneracy maps

s_{i}\colon S^{*}_{R}(M)^{\otimes n}\to S^{*}_{R}(M)^{\otimes(n+1)}

are respectively given by the formulae: for all
g_{i}\in S_{R}^{*}(M)
,

\displaystyle d_{i}(g_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes g_{n})=\begin{cases}\mathrm{ev}_{\mathbf{r}}(g_{1})\cdot(g_{2}\otimes\cdots\otimes g_{n})&\text{if~}i=0;\\
g_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes g_{i-1}\otimes(g_{i}\cdot g_{i+1})\otimes g_{i+1}\otimes\cdots\otimes g_{n}&\text{if~}1\leq i\leq n-1;\\
\mathrm{ev}_{\mathbf{0}}(g_{n})\cdot(g_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes g_{n-1})&\text{if~}i=n.\end{cases}

\displaystyle s_{i}(g_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes g_{n})=g_{1}\otimes\cdots g_{i}\otimes 1\otimes g_{i+1}\otimes\cdots\otimes g_{n}.

In particular, the face maps

d_{0},d_{1}\colon\mathrm{Kos}_{\Delta}^{*}(R,\mathbf{r})_{1}=S_{R}^{*}(M)\to\mathrm{Kos}_{\Delta}^{*}(R,\mathbf{r})_{0}=R

are respectively given by

d_{0}(g)=\mathrm{ev}_{\mathbf{r}}(g)
and
d_{1}(g)=\mathrm{ev}_{\mathbf{0}}(g).

Hence, there is an augmentation map
\mathrm{Kos}_{\Delta}^{*}(R,\mathbf{r})_{0}\to R/(\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{0})R=R/\mathbf{r}R
.

Remark A.3

(Bar convention)
.

Since
S_{R}^{*}(M)=R[X_{1},\ldots,X_{m}]
, therefore we can write

\mathrm{Kos}_{\Delta}^{*}(R,\mathbf{r})_{n}=R[X_{1},\ldots,X_{m}]^{\otimes n}.

The morphisms
\mathrm{ev}_{\mathbf{r}}\colon R[X_{1},\ldots,X_{m}]\to R
and
\mathrm{ev}_{\mathbf{0}}\colon R[X_{1},\ldots,X_{m}]\to R
are respectively given by the formulae:
\mathrm{ev}_{\mathbf{r}}\colon X_{i}\mapsto r_{i}
, and
\mathrm{ev}_{\mathbf{0}}\colon X_{i}\mapsto 0
. If we use the “bar convention” and write elements of
R[X_{1},\ldots,X_{m}]^{\otimes n}
in the form
f[g_{1}|g_{2}|\cdots|g_{n}]
, where
f\in R
and
g_{i}\in R[X_{1},\ldots,X_{m}]
, then we can rewrite the formulae of face and degeneracy maps as

\displaystyle d_{i}[g_{1}|\cdots|g_{n}]=\begin{cases}\mathrm{ev}_{\mathbf{r}}(g_{1})[g_{2}|\cdots|g_{n}]&\text{if~}i=0;\\
[g_{1}|\cdots|g_{i-1}|g_{i}\cdot g_{i+1}|g_{i+2}|\cdots|g_{n}]&\text{if~}1\leq i\leq n-1;\\
\mathrm{ev}_{\mathbf{0}}(g_{n})[g_{1}|\cdots|g_{n-1}]&\text{if~}i=n.\end{cases}

\displaystyle s_{i}[g_{1}|\cdots|g_{n}]=[g_{1}|\cdots|g_{i}|\,1\,|g_{i+1}|\cdots|g_{n}].

In the situation of Construction
A.2
, by virtue of Remark
4.15
and §
A.1
, we obtain that the geometric realization of the simplicial Koszul algebra
\mathrm{Kos}_{\Delta}^{*}(R,\mathbf{r})
(when regarded as a simplicial diagram in
\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}_{R}
) is canonically equivalent to the simplicial commutative ring
R\otimes_{{\rm ev}_{0},\operatorname{Sym}_{R}^{*}(M),{\rm ev}_{\mathbf{r}^{\vee}}}R
. Next, we compare it with another common description:

Lemma A.4
.

Let
R
be a discrete commutative ring, and
\mathbf{r}=(r_{1},\ldots,r_{m})
be a sequence of elements of
R
. Then
\mathbf{r}
determines a canonical ring homomorphism
\mathbb{Z}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{m}]\to R
that carries
x_{i}
to
r_{i}
. If we let
\psi
denote the canonical equivalence

\psi\colon\mathrm{N}_{\bullet}^{\rm hc}(\mathrm{Fun}(\operatorname{{\bf\Delta}}^{\mathrm{op}},\operatorname{CAlg}_{R}^{\heartsuit})^{\circ})\simeq\operatorname{CAlg}^{\Delta}_{R},

then there is a canonical equivalence of simplicial commutative
R
-algebras:

\psi(\mathrm{Kos}_{\Delta}^{*}(R,\mathbf{r}))\simeq R\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{m}]}\frac{\mathbb{Z}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{m}]}{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m})}.

Proof.

Since the formation of
\mathrm{Kos}_{\Delta}^{*}(R,\mathbf{r})
commutes with base change of discrete commutative rings
R^{\prime}\to R
, we may reduce to the case where
R=\mathbb{Z}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{m}]
,
r_{i}=x_{i}
. It follows from Illusie’s result Corollary
2.30
that
\mathrm{Kos}_{\Delta}^{*}(R,\mathbf{r})
is quasi-isomorphism to the Koszul complex
\mathrm{Kos}^{*}(R,\mathbf{x})
; On the other hand, we know the latter complex resolves the module
\mathbb{Z}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{m}]/(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m})
. Hence the augmentation map
\mathrm{Kos}_{\Delta}^{*}(R,\mathbf{x})\to\mathbb{Z}[\mathbf{x}]/(\mathbf{x})
is a homotopy equivalence of simplicial commutative rings.
∎
